# ThunderCats 5.5E RPG — Campaign and Setting Guide

THE ROLEPLAYING GAME 5.5E COMPATIBLE

CAMPAIGN AND SETTING GUIDE

## Foreword

### Foreword

Growing up in the ‘80s, ThunderCats was a staple of my childhood cartoon experience. I was always obsessed with its rich lore and the depth of its characters. As I entered my teenage years in the 90s, I discovered Dungeons and Dragons and found a similar sense of lore, storytelling, and character adventure. I’m surprised it took me 30 more years to figure out how to combine these two passions. If you’re looking to roll for initiative and use your “sight beyond sight,” you’ve found the right comprehensive guide to all things ThunderCats. This guide merges the beloved universe of ThunderCats with the intricate mechanics of Dungeons and Dragons, offering a unique and immersive experience for fans of both. You will find detailed character profiles, world-building elements, and adventure hooks designed to bring Third Earth to your tabletop. Whether you’re a seasoned Dungeon Master or a curious player, this resource provides everything you need to embark on epic quests alongside Lion-O, Cheetara, and the rest of the Thunderian crew. It’s been my childhood dream and my adult professional game designer honor to bring these 2024 mechanics to life for you! I could not have done this without my third-earth Lynnvandarians! —Tommy Gofton, Designer I grew up in the ‘90s and ‘00s, so the ThunderCats phenomenon originally passed me by. Still, I’ve always felt like an old soul, like someone who missed out on an iconic era. ThunderCats was always there in the background, but I never took the time to really look into it. That changed when I had the opportunity to work on this book. I dove in headfirst and never looked back. I watched the original series and the 2011 reboot, read the Star Comics and Wildstorm runs, and became especially drawn to the Dynamite comics. Along the way, I started forming my own fan theories, some of which ended up influencing my contributions to these pages. I suppose I felt I had to make up for lost time. Now, I can proudly call myself a fan. One with a deep appreciation and understanding of the world of ThunderCats. My hope is that longtime fans can see the care and dedication that went into capturing its spirit, and that you’ll enjoy our take on this beloved franchise. —Devin Wilson, Designer and Writer I can remember sprinting into my grandparents’ screen door, dropping my book bag somewhere in the kitchen, and come crashing into the living room… to plop roughly a few feet in front of their giant wooden console TV and flick the switch. After the warm up of the radiation-spewing glass tube that weighed more than me and my sister combined, there he would be, flashing his extendable sword and shining that crimson and black symbol across the skies—Lion-O. If I was fast enough, I would catch the last half of the opening sequence of ThunderCats. From those earliest of days, I knew how great of a story this was. Later, I would start telling my own tales with my collection of action figures, and now, I get to do the same through the official ThunderCats RPG. And so do gamers around the world. I couldn’t be happier. Childhood dream come true. When Tommy asked me to come and be one of the leads on this project, I don’t think I truly had the words for what it meant. Now I think I do: Justice. Truth. Honor. Loyalty. This is the code of the ThunderCats Roleplaying Game; may playing it make you as happy as making it has me. —Bryan C.P. Steele, Designer and Writer

## Feel the Magic, Hear the Roar

Feel the Magic, Hear the Roar “Brought together by nobility. Forged by adversity. Steeled by resistance. We are the ThunderCats. Succeeded by skill. Carried by confidence. Proven by bravery. Matters of fate brought us together, but we are made a team by how we work together. We have our challenges, but if we believe in each other, nothing can stand in our way.” —Lion-O, Lord of The ThunderCats “Thunder, Thunder, Thunder, ThunderCats, Ho!” The battle cry of Lion-O echoes through storm-charged skies, his Sword of Omens blazing with ancient power. The light of its call cuts across the wilderness of Third Earth, a world where forgotten science and wild sorcery intertwine. Through tangled jungles, ruined citadels, and towers pulsing with lost energy, the ThunderCats are on the move! From beyond any known galaxy, bringing with them the laws and ideals of their doomed planet, Thundera, come the ThunderCats. On the strange and dangerous world of Third Earth, they forge a new destiny, facing foes born of both magic and machine. The vile mu’tants plot from the shadows, while the deathless Mumm-Ra schemes in his decaying tomb, ever hungry for the Eye of Thundera’s light. Now you must answer its call. This is your chance to live your own ThunderCats adventures using the rules of the “World’s Greatest Roleplaying Game”. Harness the power of thundrillium to sustain your technology, pilot starships through cosmic storms, or wield enchanted armaments and ancient spells. On Third Earth, laser rifles clash with mystic blades, wizards duel robots, and courage is as vital a resource as energy cells. Inspired by the timeless spirit of 1980s adventure, where heroism meets heart and every battle carries a lesson, this book brings the action, spectacle, and excitement of ThunderCats to your tabletop. Whether you guard the Cat’s Lair, explore the reborn lands of New Thundera, or challenge Mumm-Ra’s endless evil, one truth will always remain: The ThunderCats are loose!

### What Is

ThunderCats? ThunderCats is an American animated adventure series that aired from 1985 to 1989, produced by Rankin/Bass Productions and based on characters created by Ted Wolf. The show ran for 130 episodes across four seasons, telling the saga of heroic humanoid cats fleeing the destruction of their homeworld, Thundera, to settle on Third Earth. There they battled the evil Mutants and their undead overlord, Mumm-Ra, in a struggle for the mystical Eye of Thundera. The concept began in the early 1980s when Wolf partnered with Stan Weston of Leisure Concepts Inc. (LCI) to pitch the idea to Rankin/Bass, the studio known for its animated television specials. Founders Arthur Rankin Jr. and Jules Bass approved the project, hiring comic writer Leonard Starr to shape the story and characters. LCI artist Mike Germakian designed the ThunderCats, their vehicles, and the now-iconic logo of a black cat’s head in a red circle. Production followed the distinctive Rankin/Bass model: scripts and voice work were completed in the United States, while Pacific Animation Corporation (PAC) in Japan handled the 2D cel animation under Masaki Iizuka’s supervision. Although animated overseas, the show’s visual style was

intentionally tailored to feel Western rather than resembling Japanese anime. Composer Bernard Hoffer created the music, assigning each hero a unique theme such as Cheetara’s light flutes and Tygra’s brassy trumpets, while Jules Bass provided the show’s lyrics. The series’ success extended beyond television. Marvel Comics published a ThunderCats line from 1985 to 1988 under its Star Comics imprint, and DC’s Wildstorm imprint later revived the property in 2002 with five miniseries and several one-shots. Two new animated adaptations followed: a 2011 reboot that reimagined the original story with a darker tone and a 2020 comedy series, ThunderCats Roar, which adopted a cartoonish art style. Both lasted a single season but kept the franchise alive for new audiences. In 2024, ThunderCats returned once again through Dynamite Entertainment, which launched a bold new comic series written by Declan Shalvey and illustrated by Drew Moss. This reimagining introduced a fresh vision of Thundera’s legacy and expanded its mythos through several spin-offs, continuing the enduring roar of a legend that began four decades ago.

### Using This Book

This book is your gateway to bringing the world of ThunderCats to life in your Fifth Edition games. It guides both players and Game Masters (GMs) through creating characters, adventures, and stories set in this iconic universe. You’ll need the Fifth Edition core rules to use this book effectively. This introduction provides an overview of the ThunderCats universe: the titular ThunderCats themselves, their history, eras of adventure, the eldritch Ancient Spirits, currencies, and new conditions unique to ThunderCats. Chapter 1 details how to create ThunderCats characters with options that reflect the themes of ThunderCats. It offers species options, the Mechanist class, a new subclass for each core class, as well as new backgrounds, feats, and spells. You can use this material in ThunderCats adventure or any other Fifth Edition setting. Chapter 2 provides an overview of Third Earth and beyond, along with adventure hooks for key locations. Whether you’re a player seeking inspiration for your character’s backstory or a GM looking for an intriguing place to set an adventure, this chapter has all the handy details you’ll need. Chapter 3 focuses on thundrillium, thundrainium, equipment, and vehicles. Thundrillium is a rare and valuable material that fuels the technology of the ThunderCats, while thundrainium is an element that weakens them but offers new advantages when used in weapons and armor. This chapter also presents devices that use thundrillium along with new adventuring gear, magic items, and even a few scientific inventions. Chapter 4 presents new monsters and NPCs drawn from ThunderCats. From the legendary heroes themselves to some of the deadly enemies which inhabit their new home, these creatures add challenges to your ThunderCats adventures (or any other Fifth Edition world). Chapter 5 offers a short adventure you can use to launch your ThunderCats adventures or easily integrate into your own ongoing campaign.

### The ThunderCats

### of Thundera

In a distant galaxy, far in our future, there orbited the alien planet Thundera. Upon it lived the Thunderians, humanoid cat-like people in a technologically advanced civilization that balanced both science and magic. They revered the great god Catamountain alongside a pantheon of other feline deities, weaving ancient rituals into their modern philosophy and hightech innovations. Thundera was rich in resources like silver and platinum. Yet the true treasure was thundrillium, a remarkable substance that powered their most extraordinary technologies.

Thundera was ruled by a king, supported by the senate and a council of noble families. From these families came the ThunderCats, an order of elite warriors chosen to protect the world. Service among the ThunderCats was a lifelong honor and a duty, often reserved for the eldest heirs of noble lineage. Though some viewed the call to arms with hesitation, none ever refused it, and their valor ensured that Thundera’s abundant prosperity remained secure. At the head of this order stood the Lord of the ThunderCats, chosen through the mystical power of the Eye of Thundera. This ancient artifact, set into the hilt of the legendary Sword of Omens, bound its wielder to the mantle of leadership. Each Lord of the ThunderCats had to prove their worth by completing a series of grueling trials, and these tests were faced without the aid of the Sword’s power. Traditionally, the title passed within the royal bloodline, though the Eye itself held final authority and could choose another should the reigning Lord prove unworthy or unable to serve. Such interventions were rare, however, and more often than not the current Lord could pass the title along without objection from the Eye. When a dispute arose among the ThunderCats that even the Lord could not resolve, a Thunderduel was called. Instead of endless argument, the disagreeing ThunderCats would settle their differences through combat. This ritual ensured that every member, regardless of rank, had a fair means to make a claim, with the exception of the Lord, who was exempt. Participants donned ceremonial lamellar armor and wielded wooden weapons, with the challenged ThunderCat granted the first strike. Victory in the duel meant winning the dispute, while breaking the rules or falling to the ground meant defeat. For generations, the ThunderCats defended their world with unwavering devotion. Yet, even their unmatched might could not forestall tragedy. The planet Thundera was destroyed, sending Thunderian refugees throughout the cosmos. Many

### ThunderCats

within the inner circle of Jaga the Wise, the then Lord of the ThunderCats, are known to have survived, eventually finding refuge on the strange and distant planet of Third Earth. Now, these isolated few bear the weight of their legacy, sworn to uphold the Code of Thundera and honor their fallen world in this new home. Justice. The ThunderCats dedicate themselves to ensuring fairness for all beings. They act as sworn protectors of the innocent, confronting evil whenever it arises. Their commitment to justice often puts them in direct conflict with those who would harm others. Truth. The ThunderCats swear to speak the truth at all times, avoiding false promises or accusations. This integrity earns them the trust of others and reinforces their reputation as defenders of good. While younger ThunderCats have occasionally lied to hide mistakes or mischief, they invariably come forward with the truth when circumstances grow serious. Honor. The ThunderCats show unwavering respect for friends, family, elders, and all beings. No creature is too small or insignificant to merit their consideration. They treat all beings fairly, without bias, and never belittle or disregard another in need. Loyalty. Above all, the ThunderCats value loyalty. They remain steadfast to their friends, family, allies, and the Code itself. They never abandon those in need, and their unity forms a bond far stronger than the treachery often found among evil beings. Their devotion ensures that no ThunderCat faces a challenge alone.

### Chronicles of the

ThunderCats The Book of Omens serves as the definitive record of the ThunderCats’ history and heritage, preserving the legacy of Thundera and its people within its pages. Though not entirely all-knowing, its ancient accounts offer invaluable insight into the origins of their civilization. Some passages are vague, their meanings lost to time, while others reflect the biases of those who first recorded them. Yet, with Thundera destroyed and its people scattered across the stars, the Book of Omens remains a vital link to their past. What follows is the history of the ThunderCats as it is known today.

### The Code of Thundera

The ThunderCats live by the Code of Thundera, a set of moral principles that guides their every action. They hold this Code in the highest regard, following it faithfully and striving to inspire its values in all they encounter. The Code of Thundera is built on four core virtues:

### Pre-Thundera

### Eye of Thundera

The Thunderians did not originate on Thundera, but arrived there during an event known as the Great Migration. Little is known of their history before this era, as the records of their past were lost in the mysterious catastrophe that prompted their exodus. The Book of Omens was written afterward to preserve what little knowledge remained. Even the name of their original world has vanished, though some believe it too was called Thundera, its name carried forward in remembrance of their lost home. The true cause of the Great Migration remains unknown, though many scholars believe the original world had been stripped of its vital resources, including the precious thundrillium. As supplies dwindled and chaos spread, its desperate people sought a new home, one they hoped to manage wisely enough to avoid repeating their downfall. Though much of their early history is shrouded in myth, fragments of their evolutionary past have survived. The ancestors of the Thunderians were fierce predators who, upon rising to two legs, retained their primal instincts. They forged weapons before they formed language, gathering in small packs that valued strength and survival above all else. Distrust of outsiders ran deep, and as packs grew into tribes, conflict became a way of life. Across their world, for countless generations, the tribes clashed in an endless struggle for dominance. It was not until the son of the Machairodont leader united two rival tribes that the cycle of war began to break. His vision of unity spread across the land, ushering in a century of peace during which all major tribes were joined as one people. From this unification emerged a shared culture and faith, their once-divided gods merging into a single pantheon under the great deity Catamountain. Centuries later, as their civilization advanced, the Thunderians turned their eyes to the heavens. Under the leadership of Leonus, who would become the first Lord of the ThunderCats, they built the technology to traverse the stars and embarked on the Great Migration to their new home of Thundera. While the original reasons behind the Great Migration are lost to time, it is known that the landing on Thundera was anything but smooth. In preparation, artifacts such as the Thunderscope had been created to locate lost settlers and recover their scattered belongings. Despite the initial chaos, however, Thundera soon proved to be an ideal world, rich in thundrillium and blessed with diverse, habitable environments. During the search for a site to build the new capital, Leonus discovered something extraordinary within an ancient lava basin atop Mount Grimalkin, a dormant volcano. As he unearthed a brilliant ruby-red gem, a crimson beam shot into the sky, summoning a thunderstorm so fierce that Leonus believed he heard the laughter of the god Catamountain within

its roar. When he descended from the peak, he did so as the first Lord of the ThunderCats, proclaiming a new code across the length and breadth of the land. That of justice, truth, honor, and loyalty. The Code of Thundera. The gem became known as the Eye of Thundera, regarded as a divine gift from Catamountain to guide his people in their new beginning. With Thundera now secure and the past left behind, Leonus began chronicling the Book of Omens to preserve the Code and the story of this dawn for generations to come.

### ThunderCats

With Leonus’ discovery of the Eye of Thundera and the creation of a new code of honor, the noble houses of each tribe united to form the ThunderCats. Together, these proud warriors guided the settlers as Thundera became their new home. Before his death, Leonus began constructing both the capital city and the first Cats’ Lair, a central stronghold for the ThunderCats. Though he passed before its completion, his son Lion-L ensured his father’s vision was realized. Under Lion-L’s rule, the capital flourished into a thriving metropolis, and his son, Lion-L II, carried the legacy forward. Recognizing that their growing civilization would one day face threats beyond their world, Lion-L II reshaped Thundera and led its people into a new age of strength and unity. His foresight proved true when, generations later, Thundera faced its first interplanetary war.

### War With Plun-Darr

On the nightside of Thundera’s great sun turned its sister planet, Plun-Darr. Its blasted surface of boiling oceans and scum-covered deserts was home to a warlike species called the mu’tants. Drawn by Thundera’s rich resources, the mu’tants began invading as soon as they developed interstellar travel. Cougartha, then Lord of the ThunderCats, recognized that the times Lion-L II had foretold had finally arrived. He led the Thunderians to a swift victory against the first wave of invaders, but he understood that larger armies would follow. To prepare for these coming threats, Cougartha commissioned a powerful magical sword to defend Thundera and guide the ThunderCats in battle. He requested that the Eye of Thundera be set between the hilt and blade, fulfilling the final vision of Lion-L II. From this, the Sword of Omens, the greatest weapon in ThunderCat history, was created. As the battles continued, the warlords of Plun-Darr repeatedly launched major offensives, yet each time the Sword of Omens enabled the ThunderCats to repel them, protecting their world from destruction.

### Sword of Plun-Darr

Generations later, the mu’tant attacks intensified under a new warlord, Ratilla the Terrible, who wielded the mu’tants’ dark counterpart to the Sword of Omens, the Sword of Plun-Darr. With this formidable weapon, the mu’tants struck hard, first raiding the thundrillium mines. The ThunderCats responded immediately, only to discover the raid was a diversion for a massive ambush on the capital city led by Ratilla himself. Despite suffering heavy losses and widespread damage, the ThunderCats managed to repel the invaders and force them to retreat. Despite the victory, this battle took a toll on the body of king Claudus. Weakened, nearly blind, and mourning his wife, Claudus could no longer care for his infant son Lion-O or the sacred treasures of Thundera. With his energy spent on ruling from the throne, and no longer able to fight, he entrusted the planet’s future and Lion-O’s safety to his most trusted warrior, Jaga, who would act as Lord of the ThunderCats until Lion-O came of age. Ratilla continued his assaults, though none matched the devastation of his first attack. His campaigns eventually focused on the Treasure of Thundera, bringing him dangerously close to claiming it. Only through Jaga’s valiant efforts was Ratilla defeated, and the Sword of Plun-Darr secured by Thundera. To prevent its power from ever being misused, Jaga cast the sword into a deep canyon, sealing it within molten rock. However, that power refused to stay locked away.

### Premonitions

Unbeknownst to even Jaga, the Sword of Plun-Darr held a power so immense it could not be contained. This power erupted at the core of Thundera, setting in motion a chain of cataclysmic events that ultimately began the planet’s destruction. One of Jaga’s ThunderCats, Cheetara, began experiencing vivid dreams and visions of the looming disaster. Concerned, she confided in Jaga, and together they approached King Claudus. Though they trusted Cheetara’s intuition, they needed confirmation. Claudus summoned Cougaris, his chief scientist, and after a tense period of investigation, their fears were verified. In response, Claudus convened an emergency senate meeting, and after hours of deliberation and discussion, it was unanimously decided that Thundera faced inevitable destruction.

### Exodus

To prevent widespread panic, many senators returned to their home provinces to deliver the news of Thundera’s impending destruction in person and lead the exodus. Though a planetary announcement might have saved hundreds of thousands of lives, they did the best they could with the information available. Poorer provinces evacuated their citizens in arks, packing as many as possible onto every ship. In some areas, there simply were not enough vessels, and returning for those left behind proved impossible. Other regions fared better, and in some affluent areas, nearly everyone escaped without issue. In the capital, an abundance of transports allowed Jaga to redirect ships to underrepresented regions. Some reached their destinations in time, while others did not. Jaga put the needs of Thundera before the ThunderCats, even if it only meant saving a dozen more lives. As the first stage of the exodus began, his focus turned to the nobles. The situation became increasingly dire as Thundera’s crust began to fracture, shaking the entire planet. Jaga realized they would not survive the night and needed to evacuate as many ThunderCats as possible immediately. Women, children, and the next Lord of the ThunderCats would leave first with the Sword of Omens, escorted by a few trusted warriors. Jaga intended to lead the final wave himself, remaining behind until everyone else was safe. Claudus, however, refused to allow it. He insisted that Jaga lead the first wave, arguing that the Lord of the ThunderCats could not perish with the planet. Jaga resisted, but Claudus explained that he had chosen Jaga as his successor for a reason. The risk of losing Jaga now was too great, and Claudus urged him to protect his son and ensure a next generation of ThunderCats. Whether out of loyalty to his king or adherence to the Code of Thundera, Jaga agreed. Deep down, both knew this would be their last meeting. No further ships could escape from the Cat’s Lair, just as hundreds of others failed to leave the planet. Though they lost Thundera that day, they preserved their culture and ensured the survival of the ThunderCats, leaving the possibility of one day building a New Thundera.

### Plun-Darr’s Last Laugh

As Jaga led the first wave of ThunderCats into the void of space, they watched in horror as their homeworld exploded behind them. Their grief was cut short when the fleet was suddenly attacked by a coalition of mu’tant ships. The long-warring tribes of Plun-Darr had united under the reptilian Slithe, who planned to exploit the ThunderCats’ vulnerability and steal the Sword of Omens to claim the power of the Eye of Thundera. The battle was fierce, and many ships from both sides were destroyed, but the mu’tants avoided firing on Jaga’s lead ship, unwilling to risk damaging the Eye of Thundera aboard. Soon, only Jaga’s ship remained, and a mu’tant boarding party made their way inside. Slithe and his cohorts used the chaos as a distraction to reach the Sword of Omens, only to be confronted by a young Lion-O struggling to wield it. Confident in easy victory, the mu’tants advanced, but the Sword awakened and granted its power to Lion-O, allowing him to drive them back. With the ThunderCats defeating the remaining attackers, Slithe and his forces were forced to retreat. The mu’tants continued to avoid destroying Jaga’s ship, hoping to regroup, but they soon discovered that debris from Thundera had devastated Plun-Darr, leaving few reinforcements to spare. Fueled by rage, they turned back to pursue Jaga’s ship. Meanwhile, the navigational systems were damaged, leaving Jaga with limited maneuverability and only one reachable destination, a remote blue planet called Third Earth. The journey would be long, requiring suspension capsules for the crew, but the autopilot could not be trusted. Determined to ensure their safe arrival, Jaga chose to manually pilot the ship for as long as his body would allow. The ThunderCats protested, but Jaga insisted that this was the only way. While suspension capsules slowed aging, some time would still pass, and the old and wizened Jaga knew he might not survive the journey. With final farewells, the ThunderCats entered suspension and awaited their arrival on Third Earth.

### Third Earth

Years later, the now-ancient Jaga realized his body was failing just as Third Earth came into view. Knowing he could no longer guide the ThunderCats in person he set the ship on autopilot and transplanted his essence to a spiritual plane, joining it with the Eye of Thundera. It proved to have been a wise decision not to trust the autopilot, as the ship crash-landed in the northern hemisphere of Third Earth. Snarf, the faithful caretaker of young Lion-O, was the first to awaken. He quickly discovered that Lion-O’s suspension capsule had malfunctioned,and the boy had aged during the journey. Lion-O emerged as a fully grown man, though there was little time to adjust before danger arrived. The mu’tants, pursuing Jaga’s ship, attempted to ambush the ThunderCats. Lion-O and Snarf located the Sword of Omens and awakened the rest of the ThunderCats just in time to repel the attackers. Once the immediate threat had passed, the team took stock of their situation, realizing they were alone in this strange new world. They also learned that Lion-O could still communicate with Jaga through the Sword of Omens. They began salvaging equipment and supplies from the wreckage and scouted the land for a site to establish a new Cats’ Lair. Meanwhile, while searching for the ThunderCats and a location for their new Castle Plun-Darr, Slithe and the mu’tants discovered an ancient onyx pyramid. Their ship was suddenly struck by lightning that repeatedly lashed the four obelisks surrounding the pyramid, forcing a hasty landing. A fiery ball of light appeared and led the mu’tants into the pyramid, where a bandaged figure rose from a central sarcophagus. He revealed himself as Mumm-Ra, an ancient sorcerer awakened by the proximity of the Eye of Thundera, and proposed an alliance to claim it. Overconfident, the mu’tants rejected his offer and threatened to destroy the pyramid with their ship’s cannons. Mumm-Ra demonstrated his power by sinking their ship into the sand. Most of the mu’tants made it out, though some perished beneath the sands. The helpless mu’tants then reluctantly accepted Mumm-Ra’s deal. Using Mumm-Ra’s teleportation powers, the mu’tants launched a surprise attack against the ThunderCats, but were repelled. Mumm-Ra then entered the battle himself, and though the ThunderCats managed to drive him back, they were now aware of his presence and the threat he posed.

### Cats’ Lair

Using salvage from their crashed ship, the ThunderCats built a ThunderTank to aid in exploration and defense. Meanwhile, a scouting party was captured by robotic bears, who brought them to their village and introduced them to Ro-Bear Bill, the friendly and informative leader of the berbils. He explained that his people had come to Third Earth from their home planet, Ro-Bear.

As he guided the ThunderCats through the village, an alarm suddenly sounded. Ro-Bear Bill explained that it warned of an imminent Trollog attack. These bulldog-like creatures routinely raided the village for berbil fruit, which they then delivered to the Giantors, enormous humanoid guardians of the trollberry bushes. Since Trollogs could only consume the leaves of these bushes, they were compelled to serve the Giantors by stealing berbil fruit. The berbils sympathized with the Trollogs’ plight and defended themselves with only crude darts, their efforts largely ineffective. Seeing the berbils in danger, the ThunderCats intervened, driving the Trollogs away with the Sword of Omens. When the empty-handed Trollogs returned, the Giantors, enraged, attempted to attack the village themselves, only to be repelled by the ThunderCats and their newly christened ThunderTank. Unbeknownst to the ThunderCats and the berbils, Mumm-Ra had been observing from his Onyx Pyramid. Angered that the ThunderCats had gained the berbils as allies, he transformed into a swarm of locusts that merged into a massive, threatening form to attack the village. Despite a few close calls, the ThunderCats triumphed once again. That evening, the berbils celebrated their victory with a grand feast for their new friends. Ro-Bear Bill noticed Tygra’s blueprints for the Cats’ Lair and offered the help of his people to bring it to life. With gratitude, the ThunderCats accepted the offer. Soon, the looming Cats’ Lair stood over the Plains of Fertility, ready for whatever schemes Mumm-Ra or the mu’tants might devise next.

### Castle Plun-Darr

While the ThunderCats were building their own base, the mu’tants, aided by Mumm-Ra, were constructing a fortress called Castle Plun-Darr. ThunderCat scouts discovered the site and watched from the trees as the Brutefolk, a primitive and docile race, were enslaved and forced to work under harsh conditions. The scouts’ presence was eventually noticed, and they were attacked by mu’tant guards, but they managed to escape and report back to Cat’s Lair. Upon their return, the scouts informed the ThunderCats of the mu’tants’ activities. Ro-Bear Bill explained about the Brutefolk’s nature. The ThunderCats then headed off in the ThunderTank to free the Brutefolk. The ThunderTank smashed through the fortress’s barriers, and the ThunderCats quickly spread out to combat the mu’tants while others entered Castle Plun-Darr itself, freeing the Brutefolk from their cells. Once liberated, the Brutefolk happily returned to their homes, and the ThunderCats departed, mission accomplished. Even though the Brutefolk were freed, Castle Plun-Darr was still completed, built through mu’tant labor. Realizing the mu’tants would be ready for another assault, the ThunderCats settled in, preparing for a prolonged struggle with their persistent adversaries.

### Friends and Foes

As time went by, the ThunderCats explored Third Earth, seeking thundrillium and confronting local threats. They quickly encountered the planet’s unusual environment and its diverse inhabitants. Many locals were wary of the alien newcomers, a tension heightened by mu’tant activity and the resurgence of Mumm-Ra. The ThunderCats and mu’tants also brought advanced technology, unfamiliar to the largely primitive civilizations that relied on magic or basic tools and weapons. Despite this initial distrust, the ThunderCats gradually earned the respect of Third Earth’s people with their Code of Thundera. These alliances allowed them to establish a network of support that extended far beyond the Cats’ Lair. As they aided their new friends, the ThunderCats became symbols of justice and hope, solidifying their role as the planet’s protectors. The mu’tants remained a constant threat, frequently plotting ambushes and escalating their attacks under the guidance of Mumm-Ra, who eventually raised their sunken starship to be salvaged for technology. They also received some reinforcements from Plun-Darr from the mu’tant commander Rataro-O, which only intensified rivalries among the mu’tant tribes. Meanwhile, Mumm-Ra, increasingly aware of the ThunderCats’ growing influence, summoned a variety of monstrous minions to destabilize Third Earth. Despite his dark magic, the ThunderCats’ courage repeatedly thwarted his plans. Through each challenge, the ThunderCats adapted, using the environment to their advantage and refining their teamwork, unique abilities, and mastery of the Sword of Omens. Although they often prevailed, the ThunderCats quickly learned that their enemies were relentless, demanding constant vigilance.

### ThunderCats, Ho!

Lion-O was haunted by guilt over fleeing Thundera before its destruction, but in communing with Jaga, he learned that many Thunderians had survived. Using her Sixth Sense, Cheetara discovered they had been rescued by the berbils and stranded on a deserted island on Third Earth. Determined to find them, Lion-O and the ThunderCats began preparations for a rescue. Unbeknownst to them, Mumm-Ra also learned of the survivors and hired the cyborg mercenaries known as the Berserkers to capture them and the berbils who had rescued them. The Berserkers succeeded, delivering the prisoners into the hands of the mu’tants, who transported them to Fire Rock Mountain, a place rich in thundrainium, a substance that weakens Thunderians. While the ThunderCats struggled to locate their people, they also faced attacks from the mu’tants and their new war machines. Lion-O’s attempts to save his allies were repeatedly thwarted until he gained unexpected help from Snow Knight of Hook Mountain and the samurai warrior Hachiman, who overcame Mumm-Ra’s deception just in time to join the fight. Meanwhile, Snarf allied with the tuskas of Tuskania, and together they disrupted Mumm-Ra’s forces and freed the berbils. Despite these efforts, the rescued Thunderians remained trapped on Fire Rock Mountain, separated from the ThunderCats by the mountain’s thundrainium fields. To counter this, Jaga appeared and offered to seek help from the Star of Thundera, a sentient astral body in space with immense powers. Sending his spirit into astral space, he pleaded for the Star’s help. Mumm-Ra tried to stop him by summoning Grune the Destroyer, the ghostly traitor of Thundera. Grune hurled Jaga into the inescapable gravitational pull of the nearby Black Planet but he was rescued by the Star of Thundera. The Star then changed into a talisman which Jaga gave to Lion-O. Wearing the talisman rendered Lion-O immune to the harmful effects of the thundrainium and he was able to rescue the imprisoned Thunderians. As soon as Lion-O brought the Thunderian refugees to safety, Mumm-Ra swept in and grabbed the talisman. Despite Jaga’s warnings, Mumm-Ra opened the talisman and the ensuing blast seemingly destroyed him. The ThunderCats returned to Cats’ Lair where Lion-O anointed the refugees, making them the newest members of the ThunderCats.

### Mumm-Ra Lives!

Mumm-Ra emerged from the rubble caused by the explosion of the Star of Thundera, severely weakened. Ma-Mutt carried him to his pyramid, where Mumm-Ra began to rest and recover inside his sarcophagus. As his power returned, he devised a new plan to continue his relentless attacks on the ThunderCats. He granted Ma-Mutt his own form and sent him to Castle Plun-Darr, where Ma-Mutt instructed the mu’tants to travel beyond Fire Rock Mountain to Darkside and release the Lunataks, fearsome warlords trapped by Mumm-Ra in molten lava from a previous conquest attempt. Snarf overheard this plan and hurried to warn the ThunderCats, but with the thundrainium of Fire Rock Mountain blocking access, they could only watch as the Lunataks were freed and persuaded to begrudgingly join forces with Mumm-Ra and the mu’tants against the ThunderCats. The ThunderCats soon discovered that the Lunataks had plundered the thundrillium Mine and enslaved the Brutefolk to work it. After freeing the slaves, they learned from locals that additional abductions had occurred in nearby villages. While scouting near Fire Rock Mountain, the ThunderCats encountered Tug-Mug, one of the Lunataks, and several mu’tants with captured villagers. In the battle that followed, Tug-Mug snapped the Sword of Omens, but before further damage could be done, ThunderCat reinforcements arrived. Outnumbered, Tug-Mug and his forces retreated. Fortunately, Bengali, a new ThunderCat and former blacksmith, reforged the sword, restoring it completely. However, the power of this new threat was undeniable. Escaped slaves revealed that the Lunataks were building a massive fortress called Sky-Tomb, and that a path through the unnavigable Forests of Mists bypassed Fire

Rock Mountain. Lynx-O, another of the new ThunderCats, used his special Braille Board to navigate safely through the Forests of Mists and led the team through the treacherous canyons to Darkside. There, they found Sky-Tomb fully operational and mobile. To monitor the Lunataks, the ThunderCats built an outpost called the Tower of Omens at the canyon’s entrance, with assistance from the berbils. Snarfer, Snarf’s nephew, also arrived from the Planet of the Snarfs to help with its operation. The ThunderCats faced constant clashes with the Lunataks, mu’tants, and Mumm-Ra. Despite the growing number of formidable foes, however, they always managed to emerge victorious. The ThunderCats never forgot about those kept under the Lunataks’ thumbs, and slowly but surely more and more were freed.

### New Thundera

Frustrated by the ThunderCats’ continued survival, Mumm-Ra and Ma-Mutt traveled through time and space to the debris of Thundera, seeking the Sword of Plun-Darr and the Treasure of Thundera. Soon after, the ThunderCats discovered that fragments of Thundera were reassembling into a new planet. They were astonished to see a new Onyx Pyramid forming on this “New Thundera,” where Mumm-Ra was forcing the Snarfs to search for the Treasure. Scouts soon confirmed that Mumm-Ra had abandoned the Onyx Pyramid on Third Earth, prompting the ThunderCats to design booster rockets to reach the new world. At Sky-Tomb, the Lunataks expelled the mu’tants after repeated failures. Overhearing the ThunderCats’ plans, the mu’tants aimed to sabotage the mission and earn MummRa’s favor. They headed to Castle Plun-Darr to repair the damaged RatStar. Meanwhile, the ThunderCats learned that New Thundera had formed near some of the Moons of Plun-Darr and pulled them into orbit, corrupting the planet’s landscape with their influence. Preparing for these dangers, the ThunderCats crafted new equipment to explore safely. With their booster rockets completed, the ThunderCats launched toward New Thundera but encountered the mu’tants aboard the RatStar. They successfully blasted the ship, sending it back to Third Earth. As they exited the atmosphere, Lynx-O reported increased volcanic activity, suggesting that Mumm-Ra had obtained the Sword of Plun-Darr. The Ancient Spirits of Evil had indeed drawn the sword from the depths and bestowed it upon him, and Mumm-Ra intensified the Snarfs’ labor to locate the Treasure. Landing carefully on New Thundera, Snarf and Snarfer urged an immediate rescue of the Snarfs, but Lion-O prioritized preventing Mumm-Ra from obtaining the Sword and Treasure. Impatient, Snarf and Snarfer attempted a nighttime rescue but were captured. In the confusion, one Snarf escaped and alerted the ThunderCats to the situation. As they moved to rescue the captives, Lion-O encountered Mumm-Ra disguised as Snarfer. Mumm-Ra tricked him into using the Sword of Omens’ Sight Beyond Sight to locate the Treasure, then revealed his true form and used the Sword of Plun-Darr to capture all the ThunderCats. As Mumm-Ra flew away with the chest, the Sword of Omens destroyed it, scattering the treasure across New Thundera and leaving only the Book of Omens in Mumm-Ra’s possession. Shortly after, the ThunderCats recovered one piece of the Treasure, the Magical Flute, which could charm beasts. Lion-O then learned from the Sword of Omens that the ThunderCats remaining on Third Earth had been captured by the Lunataks. He instructed his team to return to Third Earth and rescue their friends, while he and Snarf stormed Mumm-Ra’s pyramid. On Third Earth, the Lunataks forced the captured ThunderCats and berbils to mine thundrillium. Some managed to escape with Snow Knight’s help, and reinforcements from New Thundera joined them for a daring rescue. They infiltrated Sky-Tomb through ventilation shafts, shut down the power, and used the darkness to free their friends. Back on New Thundera, Lion-O and Snarf entered the Onyx Pyramid and found the Book of Omens. Mumm-Ra rose from the cauldron and fought Lion-O. In a desperate move, Lion-O dodged a strike from the Sword of Plun-Darr, which plunged into a statue of the Ancient Spirits of Evil, collapsing the pyramid. Lion-O seized the Book of Omens as Mumm-Ra fell back into the cauldron, while Snarf hypnotized Ma-Mutt with the Magical Flute to guide them to Mumm-Ra’s transport, the Mumm-Raft. The rest of the ThunderCats and rescued berbils were confronted by the Lunataks while fleeing Sky-Tomb. Just as all seemed lost, Lion-O arrived in the Mumm-Raft and severed the rocky outcropping they stood on, plunging them into the water. Everyone escaped aboard the Mumm-Raft, while Ma-Mutt, freed from the trance, returned to the Onyx Pyramid, where the Ancient Spirits revived Mumm-Ra on Third Earth. The ThunderCats and Mumm-Ra then began racing to recover the scattered pieces of the Treasure of Thundera. Regular excursions from Third Earth to New Thundera allowed them to gradually reclaim the treasure while uncovering the secrets of the Book of Omens. All the while, Mumm-Ra’s summoned creatures patrolled New Thundera, and the Lunataks ravaged Third Earth seeking fuel and technology to transport Sky-Tomb there as well. Over time, the ThunderCats staved off these vile schemes and succeeded in retrieving all pieces of the Treasure of Thundera as well as unraveling the Book of Omens’ mysteries.

### Banishment

Captain Bragg, a ringmaster-style bounty hunter, travels the void of space in his titular spaceship seeking wanted criminals. Arriving at the Tower of Omens, he offered to rid Third Earth of undesirables for a modest fee, including transporting them to his prison on the planetoid of Way Outback. Despite a few setbacks, Bragg, assisted by the ThunderCats, used his deceptive tricks and hypnosis to capture both the mu’tants and Lunataks, imprisoning them aboard his ship. He then transported his captives to the distant planetoid of Way Outback, freeing Third Earth from their menace. Meanwhile, Mumm-Ra faced the wrath of the Ancient Spirits of Evil, who, frustrated with his constant failures and the loss of his allies, gave him only one day to defeat the ThunderCats. To reinforce their command, they banished his beloved pet Ma-Mutt and transformed Mumm-Ra into his most powerful form, destroying his pyramid in the process. Mumm-Ra’s rampage began with violent earthquakes and volcanic eruptions across Third Earth. The ThunderCats, analyzing the geological disturbances, sent out scouts to find him. Having grown to gigantic proportions, Mumm-Ra effortlessly dispatched the first group who confronted him, teleporting them to an unknown dimension. More challengers arrived and fought bravely, only to join the others in captivity. He trapped Lion-O in a prison of stone and molten lava and destroyed the Tower of Omens with a single slash of the Sword of Plun-Darr. Following Lynx-O’s advice, Snarf armed himself with every item from the Treasure of Thundera and entered the dimension that had been discovered within the Book of Omens, seeking guidance on defeating Mumm-Ra. Meanwhile, Jaga used his connection to the spiritual plane to transport himself and the trapped ThunderCats into the Eye of Thundera, granting enough power to free Lion-O from his rocky tomb and deliver him back into the battle. As Lion-O and Mumm-Ra clashed again, Snarf’s image appeared in the Eye of Thundera, instructing Lion-O that he needed only to survive until sundown to triumph. Despite Mumm-Ra’s overwhelming power, Lion-O endured the battle, and when the day ended, the Ancient Spirits of Evil punished Mumm-Ra as they had Ma-Mutt. Lion-O then reunited with his friends using the Sword of Omens, and the ThunderCats celebrated the apparent defeat of their formidable foe.

### Return to Thundera

With the mu’tants and Lunataks imprisoned and Mumm-Ra banished from Third Earth, the ThunderCats decided the time had come to return home to their restored planet, New Thundera. Before departing, they destroyed Mumm-Ra’s Onyx Pyramid and appointed the League of Third Earth, a group of Third Earth inhabitants and a representative of the Interplanetary Control Force, to safeguard the world in their absence. A small contingent of ThunderCats remained behind to watch over the Tower of Omens and Cats’ Lair. Their departure, however, allowed the Ancient Spirits of Evil to reform their statues from the rubble. Realizing that evil would fade as long as the ThunderCats endured, they released Mumm-Ra from his banishment and raised an Onyx Pyramid for him on New Thundera. Traveling there aboard the MummRaft, Mumm-Ra plotted his revenge as the ThunderCats arrived in the Valley of the Stone Giants. The great statues came to life and attacked, but Lion-O’s words calmed them, convincing the giants that the ThunderCats were allies. With their help, and that of the ever-faithful Snarfs, the ThunderCats began building a new Cats’ Lair in the valley, now guarded by these gargantuan statues. Watching from his cauldron and communing with the Ancient Spirits of Evil, Mumm-Ra learned of the planet’s restoration. The ancient thunderian sorceress Jagara had constructed a Mighty Gyroscope in the Great Beneath at the core of the world. Using her creation, the Mighty Gyroscope, Jagara stabilized the remnants of Thundera and generated a new gravitational core. This device drew together the fragments of Thundera, not only binding them together but also reforming them into the renewed world of New Thundera. Seizing upon this knowledge, Mumm-Ra devised a new plan. Disguising himself as Jaga the Wise, Mumm-Ra tricked Jagara into believing the ThunderCats were impostors, even forging a false Book of Omens as proof. Under his influence, Jagara tampered with the Gyroscope, reducing gravity across the valley. As the ThunderCats and their unfinished lair began to drift skyward, Lion-O used the Sword of Omens to send a desperate signal that freed Jagara from Mumm-Ra’s control. Though she managed to stabilize the planet temporarily, Mumm-Ra escaped with the Gyroscope’s regulator, leaving New Thundera doomed once more. Unstable gravity and violent tremors plagued the planet, hampering construction of the new Cats’ Lair. During their first council meeting in the half-built structure, the ThunderCats debated the cause of the quakes and responded to a distress signal from Thunderian refugees seeking to return home. One team set out to investigate the signal, while another deployed the Whisker, a new tracking vehicle whose probes could analyze seismic activity. The second team soon spotted Mumm-Ra at the Churning Rocks. Realizing he had returned, they concluded he must be behind the disturbances. As the refugees were recovered and escorted to New Cats’ Lair, Lion-O led a pursuit to Churning Canyon. Mumm-Ra, irate that his plan to destroy the ThunderCats had not worked, once again took Jaga’s form to deceive Jagara. He again convinced her that the ThunderCats were indeed false and, under his enchantment, she started to dismantle the controls of the Mighty Gyroscope, hoping to destroy the “false ThunderCats” and their lair. The ThunderCats arrived in the Great Beneath just in time to intervene. Lion-O used the Sword of Omens to reveal Mumm-Ra’s true form, breaking his hold on Jagara. Defeated, Mumm-Ra fled, leaving the ThunderCats to stabilize the damaged Gyroscope. Though they managed to restore partial control using a temporary regulator, it wouldn’t last long and they had to seek out the real regulator before it was too late. However, Mumm-Ra destroyed the regulator by throwing it in the magical bubbling waters of his cauldron. As quakes worsened and gravity fluctuated wildly, the ThunderCats considered abandoning New Thundera. Yet, with refugees still arriving, they resolved to persevere. Mumm-Ra, deciding to speed up his evil scheme, descended into the Great Beneath and used his magic to severely damage the Gyroscope, sending catastrophic tremors across the world. Though Jagara fought valiantly to stop him, Mumm-Ra severed the Gyroscope’s main support cable before retreating, causing the massive device to plummet into an abyss. All seemed lost until Lion-O called upon the Eye of Thundera, transforming the Sword of Omens into a temporary Gyroscope to stabilize the planet. With the crisis contained, the ThunderCats constructed a new Mighty Gyroscope to replace the fallen one, which Lion-O activated through the sword’s power. With New Thundera finally secure and Jagara joining their ranks as a steadfast ally, the ThunderCats began resettling their restored homeworld. Though Mumm-Ra remained a constant threat and environmental instability continued to challenge them, the Thunderians pressed on, rebuilding their civilization one stone at a time as New Thundera’s future slowly took shape.

### Reclamation

Even though the Mighty Gyroscope had reformed New Thundera, the corruption of Plun-Darr’s moons continued to plague the planet. Over time, it intensified, triggering violent geological disturbances that threatened to tear the world apart. Seeking a solution, the ThunderCats consulted the Book of Omens. Using the Key of Thundera to unlock it, they learned that stopping the planet’s destruction required presenting specific pieces of the Treasure of Thundera to designated locations within a set time. The final step was to present the Key of Thundera to the Guardian within the Book of Omens himself.

Success would transform the corrupted regions into peaceful, productive lands, while failure would freeze the ThunderCats in time as the planet self-destructed. While the ThunderCats made their preparations, MummRa, who had been spying on the ThunderCats as a scarab, changed back to his true form. He then took the Key of Thundera and the Book of Omens and flew off to his pyramid with them. The ThunderCats split into teams to deliver the treasures. One journeyed to the Caverns of Cold carrying a tile from the fountain of healing water that once flowed when the area was the Gardens of Thundera. Another took the Gem Light that once crowned the signal tower of what was now the Mountains of the Moon. A third carried the Golden Oar of the Sacred Barge to the Baleful Swamp, and a final team transported the Ancient ThunderCat Flag to the Iron Glades. When Lion-O went to prepare his task of presenting the Key of Thundera to the Guardian, he discovered both it and the Book of Omens were missing. Suspecting Mumm-Ra, he raced to the New Onyx Pyramid. Inside, Mumm-Ra used the Key of Thundera to enter the book, defying the Ancient Spirits of Evil, who had forbidden him, fearing the ThunderCats’ ritual would weaken their power on New Thundera. Lion-O, who had just arrived at the scene, also entered the book using the Sword of Omens. Within the Book, the Guardian froze all the ThunderCats in time and declared that Lion-O must defeat Mumm-Ra in unarmed combat to reclaim the Key. After a fierce battle, Lion-O triumphed, only for Mumm-Ra to summon the Ancient Spirits of Evil’s champion, Pyron. The mysterious warrior proved formidable, but Lion-O called upon the dragons of the Book of Omens, who incinerated Pyron with their fiery breath. Having rightfully reclaimed the Key of Thundera, Lion-O presented it to the Guardian at the final hour. The Guardian summoned the ThunderCats, unfreezing them, and they delivered their artifacts. The once hostile regions of New Thundera were transformed to peaceful areas. New Thundera has been fully restored and all corrupting influence has been removed. Refugees continue to arrive regularly, bringing hope for the future. Yet even as the influence of the Ancient Spirits of Evil wanes, Mumm-Ra endures, plotting in the shadows while threats from beyond Thundera’s skies begin to stir. Many see the reborn world as vulnerable, prompting the ThunderCats to form the ThunderGuard, training a new generation of heroes to protect what they have rebuilt.

### Eras of Adventure

The saga of the ThunderCats unfolds across five distinct eras, each offering its own tone, challenges, and opportunities for heroism. Campaigns may focus on a single period or span several eras to tell a sweeping, interconnected story.

### Exodus

The ThunderCats have crash-landed on Third Earth and struggle to establish a foothold in this strange new world. Mumm-Ra and the mu’tants constantly threaten their efforts, while the inhabitants of Third Earth remain uncertain whether to be allies or foes. Thundrillium Availability. Thundrillum is very rare and must be located. Player Characters. Players take on the roles of additional ThunderCats who survived the crash or newfound allies encountered on this untamed world.

### ThunderCats Ho!

The ThunderCats have secured a strong presence on Third Earth and formed many alliances. Their progress leads to the discovery of more survivors from Thundera, stranded on a remote island after their rescue ship was damaged during the planet’s destruction. Complicating matters further, Mumm-Ra has unleashed the Lunataks, creating a new threat to face. Thundrillium Availability. Thundrillium is rare, reserved for buildings and vehicles as the Lunataks are stealing it and fighting for control of all available mines. Player Characters. Players assume the roles of newly found Thunderians or trusted allies of Third Earth who have been earned through the ThunderCats’ heroic deeds.

### Return to Thundera

Miraculously, Thundera has begun to reform, though Mumm-Ra has already entrenched himself on the reborn world. Upgraded Thunderian vehicles now allow travel between Third Earth and New Thundera, setting the stage for a desperate struggle. Mumm-Ra wields the dreaded Sword of Plun-Darr in his hunt for the legendary Treasure of Thundera, whose pieces now lie scattered across the planet. Both sides

race to reclaim them while the Lunataks engineer their own way to New Thundera. Thundrillium Availability. Thundrillium is still rare on Third Earth, but uncommon on New Thundera. Player Characters. Players assume the roles of Thunderians who survived the cataclysm and are now eking out a living on New Thundera, or allies from Third Earth aiding in the quest for the Treasure of Thundera.

### League of Third Earth

With the mu’tants and Lunataks imprisoned and Mumm-Ra banished from Third Earth, the ThunderCats turn their attention toward reclaiming New Thundera. To ensure peace in their absence, they establish the League of Third Earth, composed of Third Earth inhabitants and a representative of the Interplanetary Control Force. Some ThunderCats remain behind to guide the League, while others lead the resettlement of their homeworld. Yet Mumm-Ra, ever persistent, has already begun his schemes on New Thundera. Thundrillium Availability. With the Lunataks’ mines liberated, thundrillium is now uncommon on Third Earth as well as New Thundera. Player Characters. Players assume the roles of ThunderCats who stayed behind on Third Earth or representatives of the League of Third Earth. Alternatively, they assume the roles of Thunderians returning to New Thundera or new settlers from Third Earth.

### ThunderGuard

New Thundera has been fully restored and all corrupting influence has been removed. Refugees continue to arrive daily, bringing hope for the future. Yet even as the influence of the

### Ancient Spirits

the shadows while threats from beyond Thundera’s skies begin to stir. Many see the reborn world as vulnerable, prompting the ThunderCats to form the ThunderGuard, training a new generation of heroes to protect what they have rebuilt. Thundrillium Availability. Thundrillium is common, but well regulated to preserve its abundance. Player Characters. Players assume the roles of ThunderGuard members, whether proud Thunderians or settlers from Third Earth, united in defense of their shared home. Ancient Spirits As old as existence itself, the Ancient Spirits are eldritch beings who embody the pure forces of good and evil. They dwell upon a spiritual plane separate from the mortal realm, and though their power is vast, they cannot directly influence the material world. To act within it, they must use conduits such as statues carved in their likeness or mortal champions who serve as vessels for their power. The Ancient Spirits are driven by an eternal desire to expand their influence wherever they can. The Spirits of Evil work ceaselessly to spread corruption and cruelty, while the Spirits of Good strive to nurture virtue and harmony. At times, their opposing aims bring them into direct conflict, yet on other occasions, they may pursue their goals across entirely different worlds from one another.

### Sign of Four

There exist four Ancient Spirits of Evil and four of Good, and the number four holds significance in all of their manifestations. Their places of power take the form of four-sided pyramids surrounded by four obelisks and adorned with four statues. Each statue represents a humanoid form of a different animal. The Ancient Spirits of Evil appear as a crocodile, bull, wild boar, and vulture. The Ancient Spirits of Good appear as an eagle, bear, goat, and crane. The fourfold structure extends beyond appearance as well. Despite the influence the Ancient Spirits exert, they remain largely unknown to most folk. Though fragments of their names echo through eldritch lore, names like Nemesis, Slitherweed the Many-Legged, and Vulture King, their true identities are shrouded in mystery, even to the champions who serve them.

What is known is that each Spirit embodies one of four distinct aspects, offering a rare glimpse of differentiation among them. Both the Good and Evil Spirits share these aspects, though the way they personify them varies with their alignment. War. Embodies unbridled energy and the fire of ambition. It inspires courage while driving destruction against foes, granting fleeting glimpses of what lies ahead to those who heed its call. Spirit. Carries an air of authority, acting as an arbiter among its peers. It embodies the delicate balance of life and death, protecting the living and providing access to the departed. Tech. Commands the power of invention and discovery. It fuels technological advancement and grants knowledge to create extraordinary devices Soul. Weaves the bonds that connect allies and adversaries alike, shaping relationships that benefit the Spirits’ goals.

### Power Stones

While desperate pleas may sometimes draw the attention of the Ancient Spirits, true communion with these entities requires four power stones, each serving as a tether between the spiritual and mortal realms. Only by arranging these stones precisely and reciting the proper incantations can one make contact. The Power Stones are as old as creation itself, concentrated manifestations of their respective aspects. Each stone corresponds to a specific Ancient Spirit and contains a tiny fragment of that spirit’s power. Some mortals have even channeled these stones into weapons, wielding them with devastating effect. Whispers suggest that the Eye of Thundera may be Thundera’s own war stone among the Ancient Spirits of Good. The stones are scattered, making them difficult to locate. Once gathered, however, they allow mortals to commune with the Ancient Spirits at will, enabling deeper negotiations and the potential for more potent bargains. Every world is said to contain its own set, though whether stones from different worlds can be combined remains unknown.

### Champions

Once the connection is established, a bargain may be struck and a champion chosen. Yet the Ancient Spirits do not accept just anyone. The offering must intrigue them, and the mortal must possess potential worthy of their attention. Those who succeed gain forbidden knowledge, longevity beyond mortal years, and the ability to call upon their patrons for further aid, transforming into more powerful forms when the need arises. Though their strength flows from the Ancient Spirits, these champions retain a measure of independence. They may pursue their own desires so long as they do not directly oppose the agenda of their patrons. Their power, however, waxes and wanes with the prevalence of their aligned force. A champion of Evil grows stronger as wickedness spreads across a world, while one of Good gains might from the presence of virtue. Because their fortunes are intertwined, disagreements between spirit and champion are rare, and the Ancient Spirits are often quick to grant their chosen whatever assistance they request without debate.

### Power and Influence

Champions replenish their strength by resting within their pyramids, drawing upon the ambient energies of good or evil that permeate the world around them. On worlds where their aligned force is scarce, they must act with restraint. On Third Earth, for instance, evil runs rampant while good is faint and fragile. Mumm-Rana, champion of the Ancient Spirits of Good, is far more vulnerable than her counterpart, Mumm-Ra, the champion of Evil. The scarcity of good energy limits her reach, and venturing too far from her Opal Pyramid leaves both herself and her sanctuary exposed. Should an enemy trace her back to her lair, the consequences could be catastrophic. Champions also channel more power in places steeped in their embodied force. For example, while Mumm-Ra is most powerful in his Onyx Pyramid where evil has saturated the very air itself, he also has a lot of power in many other locations on Third Earth, as it is a planet ravaged by centuries of evil acts. Consequently, Mumm-Rana is most powerful in her Opal Pyramid, but also at Cats’ Lair, where the ThunderCats’ adherence to the Code of Thundera has imbued the land with goodness. However, this increase in power can’t make up for the overall permeation of an embodied force, as even within her Opal Pyramid, Mumm-Rana is no match for Mumm-Ra. When good prevails across a world, the Ancient Spirits of Evil bide their time, letting corruption slowly fester in mortal hearts. Conversely, when evil dominates, the Ancient Spirits of Good struggle to maintain their foothold. They often rely on patience, waiting for evil to consume itself, knowing that malice inevitably breeds discord, only taking action when allowing evil to fight itself would lead to worse outcomes. One such moment came when Queen Luna enslaved the people of Third Earth with her ancient magical belt. The Spirits of Good foresaw that if evil triumphed over her, it would claim the belt and grow unstoppable. Despite the risks, Mumm-Rana confronted Luna herself, ultimately banishing her and securing the belt within her pyramid for safekeeping.

### Currency

On Third Earth, there is little sense of a unified currency. Trade exists, but it is exactly that. Exchange by barter. Most civilizations have developed their own local systems of value, such as the snowfolk’s fire bricks or the berbils’ shnekkels, yet these hold worth only within their own communities. Before Thundera’s destruction, the Thunderians used a form of currency called credits, but in the aftermath of that catastrophe, survival takes precedence over commerce. As a result, coins and currency rarely play a role in ThunderCats adventures, with goods and favors traded through agreements, promises, and mutual need instead. However, if you as the GM prefer to include traditional Fifth Edition currency, it can be explained as a result of the ThunderCats’ growing influence. Over time, their leadership may have inspired a unified standard of trade among the peoples of Third Earth, represented by familiar copper, silver, and gold coins. The same principle can apply to New Thundera, where order and civilization are slowly being rebuilt. ThunderCats

### Conditions

The following are new conditions that may affect the creatures of ThunderCats adventures in a variety of ways, in addition to existing conditions found in the core game system. These conditions can be caused by both creatures and player character options found in this book. Each condition lasts until it is either removed, or for the duration specified by the feature that caused the condition. If multiple effects impose the same condition more than once, track the duration of each source separately, but the effects are not cumulative. If a condition is removed from a creature, all instances of that condition are removed. The following descriptions explain what effects happen to a creature that is subjected to one of these new conditions.

### Empowered

While you have the Empowered condition, you experience the following effects. Heroic Inspiration. You gain Heroic Inspiration when you gain this condition if you lack it. Proficiency and Speed Bonus. You add 1 to your effective Proficiency Bonus and 5 feet to your Speed.

### Pacified

While you have the Pacified condition, you experience the following effects. Attacks Affected. You have Disadvantage on attack rolls. Natural 1 Penalty. You gain 1 Exhaustion level each time you roll a 1 on the d20 of an attack roll or saving throw.

### Weakened

While you have the Weakened condition, you experience the following effects. Healing Affected. When you would regain Hit Points, you instead gain Temporary Hit Points equal to the amount of Hit Points you would have regained. These Temporary Hit Points last until you regain at least 1 Hit Point. Save DCs Affected. When you cause another creature to make a saving throw, the DC of that saving throw is reduced by 2.

## Chapter 1: Character Options

“You are brave, warrior. We are kindred, in that way. But experience has taught me that pride comes before a fall. A warrior must know when to fight, and when to retreat. When to fight another day. A lesson I wish I had learned earlier.” —Slithe, Lord of the Reptilians, Warlord of All Mu’Tants, and Master of Plun-Darr

### Classes

All of the classes found in the Fifth Edition gaming rules can be used in ThunderCats adventures, but presented here are new subclasses. There is also an entirely new class, with new features and powers. Whether you take up the tools of the

### Mechanist

options open a number of additional ways to experience the world of ThunderCats. Mechanist An Inventor of Mechanical Marvels The Mechanist lives in the hum and hiss of steam, the whir of gears, and the spark of invention. Whether tinkering at camp or sprinting into battle aboard a clanking contraption of their own craft, they are always at work, endlessly adjusting, refining, and experimenting with their apparatus. This intricate blend of technology and ingenuity serves as both weapon and tool, shaped for combat, exploration, and utility alike. Each device reflects its creator, from rideable, vehicle-like marvels to mobile constructs, and every Mechanist’s apparatus is as distinctive and stylized as the inventor who built it. A Mechanist uses ingenuity to tip the odds. They retool their gear, swap functions, deploy mechanical apparatuses, and adapt to the moment’s need. Their greatest triumphs come not through brute force, but through clever design. They use what they can efficiently and effectively, but always strive for improvement of their designs. Every battle leaves them wiser, their blueprints refined, and their next version already taking shape. In a world of spells and swords, the mechanist shapes the future by pushing pistons, wiring circuits, and dreaming of the perfect invention. CORE MECHANIST TRAITS Primary Ability Intelligence Hit Point Die D8 per Mechanist level Saving Throw Proficiencies Constitution and Intelligence Skill Proficiencies Choose 2: Acrobatics, Deception, Investigation, Medicine, Nature, Perception, or Sleight of Hand Weapon Proficiencies Simple weapons, Shortswords, Scimitars, Hand Crossbows Tool Proficiencies Navigator’s Tools, Tinker’s Tools Armor Training Light armor Starting Equipment Choose A or B: (A) Leather Armor, Any Simple Weapon, 10 Darts, Net, Scholar’s Pack, and 15 GP; or (B) 75 GP

### Becoming a Mechanist…

#### AS A LEVEL 1 CHARACTER

• Gain all the traits in the Core Mechanist Traits table. • Gain the Mechanist’s level 1 features, which are listed in the Mechanist Features table.

#### AS A MULTICLASS CHARACTER

• Gain the following traits from the Core Mechanist Traits table: Hit Point Die, proficiency in one skill of your choice from the Mechanist’s skill list, and training with Light armor. • Gain the Mechanist’s level 1 features, which are listed in the Mechanist Features table.

### Mechanist Class

### Features

As a Mechanist, you gain the following class features when you reach the specified Mechanist levels. These features are listed in the Mechanist Features table.

### Level 1: Efficient Crafting

You are the epitome of crafting, gaining the following features: • When crafting nonmagical items, you do so in half the normal time (round up) using a quarter of the item’s cost in materials, instead of half (round down). MECHANIST FEATURES Level Proficiency Bonus Charge Capacity Functions 1 +2 Class Features Efficient Crafting, Apparatus, Functions (Journeyman) 2 4 2 +2 Equipment Expert, Signature Functions 3 5 3 +2 Mechanist Subclass 6 6 4 +2 Ability Score Improvement 7 7 5 +3 Apparatus Size Increase (Medium), Functions (Expert), Hard Rese 9 9 6 +3 Emergency Power 10 10 7 +3 Subclass feature 11 11 8 +3 Ability Score Improvement 12 12 9 +4 Apparatus Size Increase (Large), Functions (Illustrious) 14 14 10 +4 Basic Material Alchemy 15 15 11 +4 Nicks n’ Scrapes 16 16 12 +4 Ability Score Improvement 16 16 13 +5 Apparatus Size (Huge), Functions (Genius) 17 17 14 +5 Subclass feature 17 18 15 +5 Sturdy Construction 18 19 16 +5 Ability Score Improvement 18 21 17 +6 Apparatus Size Increase (Gargantuan), Functions (Super Genius) 19 22 18 +6 Advanced Material Alchemy 20 23 19 +6 Epic Boon 21 24 20 +6 Effortless Engineering 22 25

• You can spend 10 minutes and 10 GP worth of materials to create a Tiny device (AC 5, 1 HP). The device ceases to function after 24 hours (unless you spend 1 hour repairing it to keep the device functioning), or when you dismantle it with a Utilize action; at that time, you can reclaim the materials used to create it. You can have up to three such devices active at a time. When you create the device, you determine its function by choosing one effect from the options below; the device produces that effect whenever you or another creature takes a Bonus Action to activate it with a touch. • Amplifier. The device amplifies the voice of a creature holding it to be up to three times as loud as normal for 1 minute. For the duration, the creature has Advantage on Charisma (Intimidation) checks. • Holo-Projection. An image no larger than a 5-foot Cube is projected from the device. It lasts until the end of your next turn. • Lighter. The device instantaneously lights or snuffs out a candle, a torch, or a small campfire. • Marker. The device makes a small mark appear on an object or a surface. • Pulsing. The device causes harmless tremors for 1 minute. • Speaker. The device creates an instantaneous sound that originates from itself.

### Level 1: Apparatus

When you take a Short or Long Rest with Tinker’s Tools, you can target a group of objects not being worn or carried that fill a 5 foot Cube within 5 feet of you, and use them to create an Apparatus. You can only have one Apparatus created at a time. An Apparatus uses the following rules. Basic Statistics. An Apparatus counts as a Construct as well as an object that is neither an ally or enemy, has Immunity to Poison and Psychic damage, Immunity to all conditions other than Grappled, Prone, and Restrained, and has an Armor Class based off of the material that primarily makes up its construction, as referenced in the Apparatus Material table below. An Apparatus can’t be primarily made out of mithral or adamantine. You can choose to wear your Apparatus or ride it as a mount, so long as its size is appropriate for such. If it is forced to make an ability check or a saving throw, treat all its ability scores as equal to your own. When the Apparatus is subjected to an effect that allows it to make a saving throw to take only half damage, it instead takes no damage if it succeeds on the saving throw. Instead of having Hit Points, when the Apparatus takes damage, it loses one of its Functions (see “Functions” below). If it no longer has any Functions, the Apparatus breaks apart and is no longer operational. You can restore a lost Function or repair a broken Apparatus by spending a Utilize action with Tinker’s Tools in hand while within 5 feet of the Apparatus. A repaired Apparatus still has no Functions, and must still have each lost Function restored individually. During a Short or Long Rest, you can choose to disassemble your Apparatus and use its materials to create a new Apparatus. Actions. Your Apparatus can only take an action, Bonus Action, or Reaction if directed by you using the same type of action, usually via one of its Functions from your Functions feature. The exception to this is the Attack action, which it can’t take. Instead, it can make attacks in place of your own (see “Attacks” below). Shared Speed. The Apparatus shares your walking Speed, splitting the movement up between both you and it. For example, if your Speed was 30 feet, you could choose to move 10 feet and your Apparatus to move the remaining 20 feet. If your Apparatus gains extra movement, it only affects the movement it can make that turn. For example, if your Apparatus uses 10 feet of your Speed on your turn and takes the Dash action, it gains an additional 10 feet of movement that turn. If it otherwise gains a special Speed, such as a Fly Speed, it can use that full special Speed in addition to the

movement you give it for that turn from your own Speed. For example, if it gains a Fly Speed of 30 feet from the Propellers Function and you give it 20 feet from your own Speed, it can fly up to 50 feet that turn. Ammunition. You can load ammunition and other items in the Adventuring Gear section (see Fifth Edition gaming rules or chapter 3 of this book), such as Caltrops or Ball Bearings, into the Apparatus to be held onto until used. The Apparatus can hold up to 20 of any type of ammunition and a number of other adventuring gear items equal to your Proficiency Bonus. Attacks. You can choose to have the Apparatus make melee attacks with a reach of 5 feet or ranged attacks using ammunition in place of one or all of your attacks. The Apparatus uses your Intelligence modifier and Proficiency Bonus for its attack rolls, and deals Force damage equal to 1d8 plus your Intelligence modifier on a hit. The damage of your Apparatus is increased by a number of dice when you reach Mechanist level 6 (2d8), 9 (3d8), 13 (4d8), and 17 (5d8). Utilize Items. As an action, you can have the Apparatus take the Utilize action to use an item it has access to, such as Caltrops or Ball Bearings as referenced above. Size. Your Apparatus can only be Small (i.e. utility backpack or belt, cybernetic limb, robot), but at later levels it can become Medium (Ex. bike, scooter, wheelchair), Large (Ex. cart, mechanical horse, chariot), Huge (tank, schooner), and then Gargantuan (moving fortress, warbot). The size of the Cube including the materials used to create an Apparatus must be larger when creating Apparatus’ with a size larger than Small (Medium: 10 feet, Large: 15 feet, Huge: 20 feet, Gargantuan: 25 feet). An Apparatus can be worn or ridden by a number of creatures of different sizes as per the Apparatus Size chart below. APPARATUS MATERIAL Substance Armor Class Cloth, paper, rope 11 Crystal, glass, ice 13 Wood, bone 15 Stone 17 Iron, steel 19

### Level 1: Functions

You have developed functions for your Apparatus, allowing it to accomplish several different tasks. You gain two Functions of your choice, such as Whirling Chains and Hyper Boost. Functions are described in the “Functions” section. Replacing and Gaining Functions. Once per Long Rest, you may swap out one Function during a Short Rest. You may swap out a number of Functions equal to your Proficiency Bonus during a Long Rest. Most Functions cost 1 Power Charge, but some cost more. Some also allow you to spend more Power Charges for upgraded or elongated effects. When you gain certain Mechanist levels, you gain more Functions of your choice, as shown in the Functions column of the Mechanist Features table. You can’t pick the same Function more than once. Tiers. Functions are divided into five tiers: Journeyman, Expert, Illustrious, Genius, and Super Genius. You only have access to Journeyman Functions, but gain access to later tiers when you gain certain Mechanist levels as seen on the Mechanist Features table. Function Save DC. Some Functions require your target to make a saving throw. The save DC equals 8 plus your Intelligence modifier and Proficiency Bonus. Power Charges. Functions cost Power Charges. You have 4 Power Charges, and you gain more as you reach higher levels, as shown in the Charge Capacity column of the Mechanist Features table. You can’t have more Power Charges than the number shown in the table for your level. You can only expend a number of Power Charges equal to 1 plus your Proficiency Bonus per turn.
