For example, a Lightning Bolt spell cast into one portal space will continue out the other portal space in any direction you choose.

#### LEVEL 14: THE PRESTIGE

The greatest trick you can pull is making everyone think you’re no longer a threat. After taking damage from an effect originating from a creature, you can take a Reaction to wrap yourself in a powerful illusion that makes it seem as though you’ve been dealt a mortal blow. The triggering creature must succeed on a Wisdom saving throw against your spell save DC or completely believe that it somehow killed you. This saving throw is made at Disadvantage if the damage caused you to become Bloodied. This belief lasts until you leave its line of sight for any reason, at which point you can cast any of the following spells without expending a spell slot if you have it prepared, as a Bonus Action: Dimension Door, Invisibility, Misty Step, or Teleport. Once you use this feature, you can’t use it again until you finish a Long Rest.

### Backgrounds

Characters can hail from a wide variety of different origins in ThunderCats, but there are some specific instances that appear in a number of the iconic personalities’ histories. The following Backgrounds are designed to represent some of those unique narratives, or to help players create some of their own, evocative of the setting and story.

### Crashed

Ability Scores: Strength, Constitution, Wisdom Feat: Die-hard Skill Proficiencies: Athletics and Survival Tool Proficiency: Choose one kind of Other Tools Equipment: Choose A or B: (A) Explorer’s Pack, Lantern (Bullseye), Quarterstaff, Traveler’s Clothes, 28 GP; or (B) 50 GP You were just another colonist, crew member, or stowaway on board a vessel headed to a completely different planet, but fate had different plans for you and your shipmates. Along the way, your ship ran into trouble and was sent hurtling down toward this new planet you call home. This wasn’t the life you were expecting, but you are ready to rebuild what was lost to you nonetheless.

### Destined

Ability Scores: Strength, Wisdom, Charisma Feat: Pack Leader Skill Proficiencies: Insight and Performance Tool Proficiency: Choose one Gaming Set or Musical Instrument Equipment: Choose A or B: (A) Any Gaming Set or Musical Instrument, Diplomat’s Pack, 10 GP; or (B) 50 GP You are on a path to leadership, to greatness, and to glory that was laid out before you were even part of this world. Your past, your lineage, or perhaps even just how the stars were aligned when you came to be instilled in you the call to be part of something greater, whatever that might be.

### Nomad

Ability Scores: Constitution, Wisdom, Charisma Feat: Unshaken Skill Proficiencies: Nature and Survival Tool Proficiency: Navigator’s Tools Equipment: Choose A or B: (A) Crowbar, Explorer’s Pack, Hooded Cloak, Navigator’s Tools, Traveler’s Clothes, 9 GP; or (B) 50 GP You never call any one place home for long, and feel most at ease when the proverbial road is beneath your feet. Your life is fluid, always in motion, and you stand defiantly against stagnation. In all your travels you have learned many lessons, but one stands out: you can survive wherever your travels take you.

### Reclaimer

Ability Scores: Dexterity, Intelligence, Wisdom Feat: Object Understanding Skill Proficiencies: History and Insight Tool Proficiency: Choose one type of Artisan’s Tools Equipment: Choose A or B: (A) Burglar’s Pack, Chest, 10 Pages of Parchment, Traveler’s Clothes, 26 GP; or (B) 50 GP You lived a somewhat normal life, but a trinket or piece of your history that was lost has been returned to you, and now everything is strange and new. It was like this slice of the past you didn’t know was missing had been reclaimed, setting yourself up for a future of adventure.

### Tinkerer

Ability Scores: Strength, Intelligence, Wisdom Feat: Mechanical Aptitude Skill Proficiencies: Perception and Investigation Tool Proficiency: Tinker’s Tools Equipment: Choose A or B: (A) Hammer, Tinker’s Tools, Traveler’s Clothes, 2 GP; or (B) 50 GP You have a mind for the technical and the mechanical. For as long as you can remember, you have been taking things apart and putting them back together again; perhaps better than they were before. In a world where technology is an important part of everyday life, you are key to understanding and commanding it.

### Species

There are dozens of sapient species found in the ThunderCats universe, whether on Third Earth, New Thundera, or beyond. The following are some of the most notable of these species, giving players and GMs alike the chance to build characters that are rooted in ThunderCats lore and legends. While this chapter presents many species, it does not cover every one the ThunderCats have encountered. Some, such as trolls, under-earthfolk, or unicorn keepers, can easily be represented using existing species from the Fifth Edition core rules like gnomes, dwarves, or elves respectively.

### Thunderian

### Human

Found throughout the galaxy, not solely on Third Earth, humans are as varied as the planets they appear upon. Whether they can trace their lineage back to the original Earth of ancient times, or simply believe they came solely from their corner of existence, humans form a significant portion of the galaxy’s spacefarers. Their resourcefulness and willingness to confront a universe of diversity are to be commended, accepted, and possibly even envied on many of the worlds they call home. On Third Earth, there are pockets of humanity that stretch back to long before the first Thunderian ever set foot there. An ancient society of honorbound dynasties came to settle Third Earth from their distant homeworld under a Red Sun, and the warrior maidens of the Treetop Kingdom seem to be one of the oldest cultures to thrive here. With Third Earth’s rich atmosphere and diverse climate, humans from all over the galaxy can come and survive well enough. Humans vary in appearance, especially if they grew up off-planet. There are even some strange or unique skin pigments that range the colors of the rainbow, but at their core, humans are biologically similar to one another. It is their cultural differences that strengthen them as a species more than any evolutionary traits. Before the cataclysmic shattering of the planet Thundera, it was teeming with advanced feline humanoids—the Thunderians. Culturally, most Thunderians have traditionally followed the Code of Thundera: “Justice. Truth. Honor. Loyalty.” This has helped forge tightly connected families, communities, and eventually led to the creation of the highest honor amongst Thunderians; the noble ThunderCats. Thunderians governed their homeworld for eons in relative peace, allowing them to focus on advancing their people’s science, art, architecture, and culture. Their expansion into the stars introduced them to numerous other species and cultures, but none directly affected them as much as discovering the nearby planet of Plun-darr and its inhabitants, the mu’tants. The two collective peoples immediately clashed in every way. The Thunderians were immediately forced to defend themselves from the aggressive territoriality of their new neighbors, their conquest-minded leaders, and their penchant for violence. When Thundera exploded—some say due to an attempt to incarcerate the Sword of Plun-darr in hidden vaults—the remaining Thunderians boarded escape ships and blasted off to the distant corners of the galaxy. Even though all Thunderians share a number of basic physical traits, like their natural claws and sharp eyes, they also inherit specific traits from the feline bloodlines their ancestors were closest to. Their appearance and general physique connect on some level to those ancient cats, showing up in the coloration and markings of their fur, but also grants them additional benefits that spiritually connect them to their forebears.

#### STRONG CATS

#### CLEVER CATS

Strong Cat Thunderians have thick musculature, and stand taller and wider than other Thunderians. This solid frame is perfect for building powerful physiques and fostering a remarkably resilient core. When a Strong Cat Thunderian needs to do so, they can draw upon an immensely deep reserve of raw physical power. Clever Cat Thunderians have keen minds that help them see their surroundings in different shades of problem solving requirements. They are fast and creative thinkers, coming up with inventive solutions or designs to overcome threats. Clever Cat Thunderians come up with the ways to make things work for them and their allies using their ideas, with a mind as sharp as their claws.

#### FAST CATS

Fast Cat Thunderians are more lithe and fleet of foot, easily outpacing their fellows without breaking a sweat. They use their flexibility and uncanny sense of balance to use the world as their own gymnasium, never letting any obstacle slow them down. Fast Cat Thunderians are always ready to be where they are needed, and can get there in the blink of an eye.

#### TOUGH CATS

Tough Cat Thunderians are the most resilient and stalwart of their kind, refusing to let anything lay them low without a fight. They tend to be the most adaptive to their surroundings, shrugging off situations that others would shy away from. Tough Cat Thunderians stand in the path of danger and do not budge.

#### WISE CATS

Wise Cat Thunderians use their mental fortitude and instincts to enforce their will on their environment. Whether seeing things others cannot or teaching their allies aspects of the world they have been open to learn themselves, Wise Cat Thunderians are the most perceptive and cunning of their kind. They have helped shape much of their ancient traditions and lessons to be learned by future generations.

#### PROUD CATS

Proud Cat Thunderians are the born leaders and most personally resolute examples of the species. Their greatest strength is an ironclad belief in themselves and their allies, cementing them as the pillar their team can find support from. Proud Cat Thunderians stand as examples of Thunderian nobility and can embody everything the Code of Thundera teaches.

THUNDERIAN BLOODLINES Bloodline ThunderCats Examples Benefits Strong Cats Grune, Panthro • • • You have proficiency in Strength saving throws. You have Advantage on Strength (Athletics) checks. #### THUNDERIAN TRAITS

Creature Type: Humanoid Size: Medium (About 5-6 1/2 feet tall) Speed: 30 feet As a Thunderian, you have these special traits. Claws. You have claws that you can use to make an Unarmed Strike. If you hit with this Unarmed Strike and deal damage, you can deal Slashing damage equal to 1d6 plus your Strength or Dexterity modifier (your choice), instead of the normal damage of an Unarmed Strike. Darkvision. You have Darkvision with a range of 90 feet. Natural Climbers. You have a Climbing speed equal to your Speed minus 10 feet. Thunderian Bloodline. You must choose a bloodline that grants you physical abilities. Choose a bloodline from the Thunderian Bloodlines table. You gain the benefits listed for that bloodline.

### Snarf

Snarfs are a species of small, quadrupedal, thickly furred felines that cohabitated Thundera alongside the Thunderians. The two species have lived in concert for so long, they are intrinsically linked on a social and seemingly spiritual level. Even after the destruction of their homeworld, when most snarfs relocated to a nearby planetoid cleverly renamed Planet Snarf, they maintained a psychic tether to the surviving Thunderians.

Snarfs are three to four feet long from whiskered nose to the end of their muscular, semi-prehensile tail. They are quadrupeds, but are comfortable walking around on their hind legs and manipulate their surroundings with the dexterous handlike paws of their front legs. The coloration of their fur generally appears striped with large sections ranging in the “warmer” hues; reds, oranges, yellows, and some tans. Snarfs are fiercely loyal and, despite their strong self-survival instincts, they are brave warriors when called to the defense of others. The most interesting feature of the snarfs as a species is their almost mystical connection to good fortune, with some ancient Thunderian texts claiming that snarfs are intrinsically incapable of doing evil acts, which is why so many noble Thunderian families invited snarfs to live in their homes, watch over their cubs, and help steer their moral compass.

#### SNARF TRAITS

Creature Type: Beast Size: Small (About 3-4 feet long/tall) Speed: 30 feet As a Snarf, you have these special traits. Blessings of Thundera. Snarfs and Thunderians have lived together for longer than any of the tales in the historic texts, and are often considered to be one and the same. Any beneficial effect that affects Thunderians will also affect you. Good Fortune. You can rely on your good nature and better fortune to get you through most things. When you roll a 1 on the d20 of an ability check or saving throw, you can reroll the die, and must use the new roll. On All Fours. When you need to move faster, you can run quadrupedally. Your walking Speed is increased by 10 feet while you are not carrying anything in your forepaws. Semi-Prehensile Tail. Your tail is remarkably strong and dexterous, allowing you to hold items in it. In addition, once on your turn you can take the Utilize action using your tail (no action required). Wily. Snarfs are known for their wit and wily natures, making their minds difficult to rein in. You have Advantage on saving throws to avoid or end the Charmed condition and against effects that would force you to perform an action or change your behavior, such as the Command or Confusion spells.

### Berbil

The Ro-Bear berbils, more commonly referred to as just berbils, are a partly organic, partly cybernetic species that crash landed on Third Earth long ago. They have existed in the fringes of the forests, dwelling in agricultural communities as long as anyone can remember. Even their own oldest of folktales name Third Earth as their homeworld. They live peaceful lives tending their orchards and gardens, and maintaining a number of unique kinds of fruits that sustain their organic and inorganic bodily components. They are personable and intelligent, most often choosing to build relationships with outsiders over trying to keep them away, but are also hardy enough to defend themselves if necessary. Berbils are short, ursine or marsupial-like humanoids with large cybernetic portions of their bodies—head, torso, and limbs alike. Their organics are covered in thick fur, often ranging in brown, tan, or grey, but blue and violet berbils appear occasionally. Their hands and feet end in thick claws useful for climbing and digging, and are not used as anything but tools. Once considered to be pacifists, recent generations of berbils have learned how to stand up for themselves and defend their ways of life through their interactions with Lion-O and his ThunderCats.

#### BERBIL TRAITS

#### SNOWFOLK TRAITS

Creature Type: Humanoid Size: Small (About 3-3 1/2 feet tall) Speed: 25 feet Creature Type: Humanoid Size: Medium (About 6 1/2-8 feet tall) Speed: 30 feet As a Berbil, you have these special traits. Cybernetic Organism. You are a unique mixture of organic and inorganic components. This allows you to be “repaired” by the Mending spell, regaining 2d8 Hit Points. Any other form of healing targeting you is reduced by 1 per die (minimum 1). Once you regain Hit Points from the Mending spell in this way you must finish a Short Rest before you can do so again. Industrious. You are proficient in three tools of your choice. Metal Body. Your internal organs are all protected or replaced by metallic components. You gain a +1 to your Armor Class and have a Damage Threshold of 3. When you reach character level 5, your Damage Threshold increases to 5. When you reach character level 9, your Damage Threshold increases to 7. Peaceful Nature. You are often hesitant to initiate combat, and can be slow to react when it is thrust upon you. You have Disadvantage on Initiative rolls. Utiliclaws. You can climb rough surfaces without expending extra movement, and have Advantage on Strength (Athletics) checks to dig through loose stone or soil. As a Snowfolk, you have these special traits. At Home in the Cold. You have Resistance to Cold damage and don’t suffer negative effects from cold weather or environments (such as Difficult Terrain in snow or ice). Powerful Build. You have Advantage on any ability check you make to end the Grappled condition. You also count as one size larger when determining your carrying capacity. Vulnerable to Heat. You have Disadvantage on saving throws made to mitigate or avoid Fire damage, and you must succeed in a DC 14 Constitution saving throw to benefit from a Long Rest while in an environment of extreme heat. Wintered Weapons. You can wrap any weapon in a coating of magical hoarfrost as a Bonus Action. The next time the weapon hits a target, it inflicts extra Cold damage equal to 1d6 plus your Proficiency Bonus. You can use this trait a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest.

### Snowfolk

The snowfolk are native to the northern mountains and frozen poles of Third Earth, with their oldest settlement being the community in, around, and under Hook Mountain. They are the stalwart protectors of their frozen territories, and are ironclad in their will when sworn to duty. They were long ago put in charge of protecting Third Earth from tremendous evils, and the oaths of their ancestors are as solid as the mystic ice out of which they build everything. Snowfolk are tall, brown or grey-furred humanoids with wooly muzzles, sharp eyes, and pointed ears near the crown of their heads. They tend to be physically resilient and strong, but tire quickly when subjected to hot environments. The spiritual essence of winter is a part of their core being, and wielding intense cold and ice is second nature to every snowfolk. They are taught early in life to only give their word to those they trust, and to do so carefully, as it binds as much as guides them. A snowfolk ally is an ally for life.

Word Is Bond. You are stubborn and persistent when keeping your word or upholding your duty. You have Advantage on any saving throw made to avoid gaining a condition or being forced to perform an action that dissuades you from your current duty (such as Charmed, Frightened, or an action from the Command spell).

#### TUSKA TRAITS

The tuska are pinniped humanoids living along the coasts and islands across Third Earth. Tuska evolved from other marine mammals and still bear many of the same physical traits as their forebears, all of which helped them to become a dominant species wherever their territories stretch. They tend to foster deep folklore and cultures rooted in family, teamwork, and a rich warrior tradition. Becoming friends, teammates, or crew alongside a tuska frequently means having a trusted ally for life.

### Tuska

in height, weight, and the coloration of their dense pelt of insulated fur. Ranging in tans, browns, and greys, tuska fur gets darker and can look slick or leatherlike when wet, which they often are. All tuska are natural swimmers and possess a layer of blubber under their skin that helps maintain their buoyancy, body temperature, and helps seal up or cushion minor wounds. Added to their dermal resilience, tuska all have two large, dominant dental tusks set in their jaws that serve as their species’ namesake, helping forge the intimidating image of their people. TUSKA TRAITS Creature Type: Humanoid Size: Medium (About 6-7 feet tall) Speed: 30 feet As a Tuska, you have these special traits. Fierce Appearance. You are naturally a fearsome and physically imposing creature. You have proficiency in Intimidation, and may use Strength instead of Charisma when making Intimidation checks. Hold Breath. You can hold your breath for a number of minutes equal to 5 times your Constitution modifier (minimum 5 minutes). Natural Swimmer. You have a Swim Speed equal to your Speed. Thick Skinned. When you take Bludgeoning, Piercing, or Slashing damage you can reduce the damage you take by 1d4 plus your Constitution modifier (no action required). You can use this trait a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest.

### Mu’Tant

The mu’tants of Plun-Darr are a collection of humanoids with distinct strains of animalistic traits who have had the misfortune of being led by a tyrannical few into lives of infamy. Some say it was the mystical corruption of the Sword of Plun-Darr nestled deep in the planet that twisted their leaders into villains, but the reality may lie with the bestial instincts of the mu’tants themselves, and their natural desire to create cultural packs and the pecking orders within. The planet Plun-Darr has always existed in a metaphorical mirrored revolution with Thundera, pitting the inhabitants of both against each other for eons. Thunderians and mu’tants have debated, competed, and battled against each other for as long as the two peoples have been able to cross the space between them. It has always been Thundera versus Plun-Darr. The Thunderians versus the mu’tants. The Sword of Omens versus the Sword of Plun-Darr. There would always be outliers that do not conform to the collective stereotypes of their people, allowing for heroes to rise up from the swamps, jungles, and urban sprawl of Plun-Darr. In fact, a life of excitement and adventure surviving amongst other mu’tants could make legends out of them. mu’tants hail from five primary lineages based upon the root creatures they share many of their traits with. Their collective roots come from surviving on Plun-Darr, and amongst each other, with a leaning toward general tenacity and resilience. mu’tants are often tall, well-muscled, and heal quickly from generations of aggressive physicality. As long as nothing gets in the way unexpectedly, they live long lives; well over a century, sometimes two. Although drastically different from each other, each strain of mu’tant is almost physically identical

at a glance. Closer inspection shows different color variations: plumage, fur, eye shades, and subtle markings, making each one as different as the stripes on a Thunderian tyger!

#### AVIANS

Avian mu’tants are tall, wiry, humanoids with slightly hunched shoulders beneath a slightly extended neck and a head dominated by a large, menacing beak. Their longer limbs end in taloned hands and feet, and most bear little to no plumage except around the shoulders and chest. There are more Vulturemen amongst the families of Avian mu’tants, but Corvidfolk and Raptorians do appear occasionally. Avian mu’tants are known for their powerful natural weaponry, but also particularly strong visual acuity.

#### CANINES

Canine mu’tants are the strain of mu’tant that vary the most due to the myriad of different physical appearances they present, despite all falling under the same genetic background. They are all covered by a layer of fur, but the range of length, wiriness, color, and thickness is vast. They take traits from bulldogs, wolves, and many others, with the extremely numerous jackals being the predominant subspecies. Canine mu’tants have powerful musculature, whether sinewy or thick, and a highly evolved sense of smell.

#### PRIMATES

Primate mu’tants, as a strain, are made up almost solely of monkians. Something in their enduring and dominant genetics have pushed them into not only being the most recognizable Primate mu’tants, but also the most numerous amongst all the mu’tants. There are other, rarer Primates with differing fur patterns or colors, but the vast majority will share the baseline look of the monkian with thick, white or gray fur all over their bodies, a loud and booming voice, and advanced tendon structures that make them intensely athletic jumpers, climbers, and grapplers.

#### REPTILIANS

#### MU’TANT TRAITS

Reptilian mu’tants are the only cold-blooded strain from PlunDarr, and they have somehow managed to position themselves into being considered the top of the social food chain amongst mu’tants. Their round-shouldered bodies are big, tough, and covered in dense scales, and every Reptilian mu’tant also possesses a powerful tail as equally useful as a weapon as it is for balance. Creature Type: Humanoid Size: Medium (About 6-7 feet tall) Speed: 30 feet

#### RODENTIA

Rodentia mu’tants are rarely seen, even on Plun-Darr, but not because of the quantity of their kind, but rather their natural instinct to hide, shy away from unwanted attention, and survive through anything life can throw at them. Their bodies, while still athletic, tend toward flexibility and nimbleness, rather than raw musculature. A Rodent mu’tant’s whiskered muzzle allows them to sense subtle changes around them, even in complete darkness, adding to an enhanced sense of self-preservation that affords them a kind of “danger sense.” As a Mu’Tant, you have these special traits. Darkvision. You have Darkvision with a range of 60 feet. Mu’Tant Strain. You are part of a strain that grants you physical abilities. Choose a strain from the Mu’Tant Strains table. You gain the benefits listed for that strain. Natural Weapons. You have natural weaponry (claws, fangs, beak, etc.) that you can use to make an Unarmed Strike. If you hit with this Unarmed Strike and deal damage, you can deal Piercing or Slashing damage (as appropriate to the natural weaponry used) equal to 1d6 plus your Strength modifier, instead of the normal damage of an Unarmed Strike. Survival of the Fittest. You have proficiency in the Survival skill and have the Tough Origin Feat. ### Feats

In the ThunderCats Roleplaying Game, Feats are a flavorful way of adding special properties and talents to characters that have appeared from time to time in the stories that make up the beloved narrative. Just as Feats from other publications, the following entries are all available to ThunderCats characters that meet their specified requirements (if any). This section’s feats are organized by category—Origin, General, or Fighting Style—and alphabetized in each category. All the feats are listed alphabetically in the Feat List table.

#### INSPIRING DEFENSE

Fighting Style Feat Your friends are better off fighting near you. You gain the following benefits. Defensive Position. At the start of your turn, you may lower your Armor Class by up to your Proficiency Bonus, increasing the Armor Class of your allies within 5 feet of you by the same amount until the start of your next turn.

#### INSPIRING OFFENSE

Fighting Style Feat You know how to trap your foes between you and an ally, focusing both your efforts upon them. You gain the following benefits. Offensive Position. At the start of your turn, choose one ally within 30 feet of you. Draw a Line between you and that ally. Until the start of your next turn, you and that ally gain Advantage on attack rolls on targets within the Line. General Feat (Prerequisite: Level 4+, Charisma 13+) You have been blessed with a voice as enchanting as the song of the Arietta bird. You gain the following benefits. Aptitude for Song: You double your Proficiency Bonus when making Charisma (Performance) checks for singing or public speaking. Powerful Voice: You gain Advantage on any D20 Test specifically involving singing.

### Spells

The following new spells are designed with the ThunderCats universe in mind, but could be used in any setting. As these are new spells to be added to the collective class repertoires, each one will be denoted which classes can choose these spells normally when they have the option to do so. The following spells are presented in alphabetical order.

#### ARIETTA’S SIREN

Level 4 Enchantment (Bard, Druid, Sorcerer) Casting Time: Action Range: Self Components: V Duration: Concentration, up to 1 minute You sing a song of serenity in a 60-foot Emanation for the duration. Allies within the Emanation gain Advantage on Intelligence, Wisdom, and Charisma saving throws. When you cast this spell, you may distribute a number of d4s equal to your spellcasting ability modifier to creatures of your choice within the Emanation. Whenever a creature makes an Intelligence, Wisdom, or Charisma saving throw before the spell ends, the creature can expend one or more of its d4s to add 1d4 to the saving throw. Using a Higher-Level Spell Slot. When you cast this spell using a spell slot of level 6 or higher, the size of the dice distributed increases by one step (d4 to d6, d6 to d8) for every two spell slot levels above 4.

A malfunctioning weapon can’t expend charges until a creature takes the Utilize action to fix the weapon malfunction.

Until the end of your next turn, you have a +5 bonus to AC, including against the triggering attack. You have Resistance to the triggering damage type until the start of your next turn. Also, the first time you hit with a melee attack on your next turn, the target takes an extra 1d6 damage of the triggering type, and the spell ends. You can choose to cast this spell without expending a spell slot, but must immediately succeed on a DC 15 Constitution saving throw or gain 1 Exhaustion level.

Using a Higher-Level Spell Slot. The number of charges expended increases by 1, and the damage increases by 1d10, for each spell slot level above 3.

Until the spell ends, you gain the following benefits: • You gain Resistance to Force, Necrotic, and Radiant damage. • You regain 10 Hit Points at the start of each of your turns if you have at least 1 Hit Point. • You gain Immunity to the Frightened condition. • You emit a 15-foot Emanation. Whenever the Emanation enters a creature’s space and whenever a creature enters the Emanation or ends its turn there, the creature must make a Wisdom saving throw. On a failed save, the creature drops whatever it is holding and has the Frightened condition. If the creature ends its turn in a space outside the Emanation, the creature makes a Wisdom saving throw. On a successful save, the creature no longer has the Frightened condition. • If you are smaller than Large, you become Large, along with anything you are wearing. If you lack the room to become Large, your size increases as much as it can. • You gain Advantage on Strength checks and Strength saving throws. • You gain claws that you can use to make an Unarmed Strike, using your spellcasting ability modifier. If you hit with this Unarmed Strike and deal damage, you can deal Slashing damage equal to 1d6 plus your spellcasting ability modifier, plus 2d6 Necrotic damage, instead of the normal damage of an Unarmed Strike. The target’s Hit Point maximum decreases by an amount equal to the Necrotic damage taken. When you take the Attack action on your turn and attack with a Light weapon, you can make one Unarmed Strike using your claws as a Bonus Action later on

the same turn, choosing the Damage option. • You can attack twice, instead of once, when you take the Attack action on your turn. You ignore this benefit if you already have a feature, like Extra Attack, that gives you extra attacks.

Level 5 Enchantment (Bard, Cleric, Ranger, Warlock) Casting Time: 1 Action Range: 60 ft. Components: V Duration: Concentration, up to 1 minute A number of creatures up to your spellcasting ability modifier in a 30-foot-radius Sphere centered on a point you choose within range that can see and hear you, and that can understand you, must make a Wisdom saving throw. On a failed save, a creature has the Charmed condition for the duration. While the creature is Charmed in this way, it undertakes to perform any services or activities you ask of it in a friendly manner, to the best of its ability. You can set the creature new tasks when a previous task is completed, or if you decide to end its current task. If the service or activity might cause harm to the creature, or if it conflicts with the creature’s normal activities and desires, the creature can make another Wisdom saving throw to try to end the effect. This save is made with Advantage if you or your companions are actively in conflict with the creature. If the activity would result in certain death for the creature, the spell ends. When the spell ends, the creature knows it was Charmed by you. On a successful save, the creature becomes Indifferent about creatures of your choice that it is Hostile toward. This indifference ends if the target takes damage or witnesses its allies taking damage. When the spell ends, the creature’s attitude returns to normal. Each creature that ends this movement in the Line must make a Strength saving throw, taking 4d6 Force damage and gain the Restrained condition until the end of its next turn on a failed save, or half as much damage only on a successful one. Using a Higher-Level Spell Slot. When you cast this spell using a spell slot of level 5 or higher, the size of the object or creature you can effect increases by one size category (Large to Huge, etc.) for every two spell slot levels above 3.

If you choose the past, you can only view events that transpired within 1 year of the present. A creature that sees the sensor (such as a creature benefiting from See Invisibility or Truesight) sees a luminous orb about the size of your fist.

## Chapter 2: Third Earth and Beyond

“Our home has been destroyed. We’ve been hunted across the galaxy. We finally found a place to be, but not without cost. Nor without even new threats. But none of this matters. There is no going back. I wonder, Jaga, was this your plan all along? I thought knowledge would come with age, but I feel I know less with each passing day. Somewhere out there are answers. I must find them.” —Lion-O, Lord of The ThunderCats The once-glorious planet of Thundera, ancestral home of the ThunderCats, was a world defined by feline nobility and peace until tragedy struck. Thundera’s core erupted catastrophically and rented the planet asunder, forcing its inhabitants to flee into the unknown void of space. Though many refugees escaped aboard starships, their home was irrevocably lost. Their flight carried them across the cosmos, landing upon the strange, untamed Third Earth. This mysterious planet, rich in varied geography and inhabited by both native peoples and alien races, became their refuge and battleground. There, amidst forests, deserts, and ancient ruins, they built the Cats’ Lair and formed alliances against the dark sorcery of Mumm-Ra and other lurking threats. While the majority of the ThunderCats’ adventures unfold on Third Earth, there are many other worlds beyond its skies. For instance, Thundera’s sister planet, Plun-Darr, is the war-torn mu’tant homeworld scarred by centuries of conflict and further devastated by Thundera’s destruction. Across the stars, wayward snarfs have found peace on a colorful world they now call the Planet of Snarfs. In another corner of the cosmos, a proud samurai empire flourishes under the light of a red sun. Whether you’re drawn to exploring ancient tombs filled with hidden evils, searching for treasure in shadowy caves, or confronting legendary beasts roaming the wilderness, both Third Earth and the distant worlds beyond it offer endless possibilities. These vivid settings make excellent inspiration for one-shot adventures, convention scenarios, fill-in sessions, or even the start of a grand campaign.

### Space

The immense void of space is home to countless celestial bodies, including suns, planets, moons, and comets. In ThunderCats adventures, however, space behaves a little differently than we might expect. There is no weightlessness beyond celestial objects, and momentum isn’t constant, so anything that stops propulsion simply falls, continuing downward until it propels itself again. The temperature is strangely tolerable, so creatures don’t freeze in the cold expanse. There’s always light, allowing everyone to see completely, and somehow beings can breathe and even speak freely among the stars. These peculiar qualities of ThunderCats space are as mysterious as they are convenient, and it’s probably best not to question them too closely.

### Interplanetary Control Force

The Interplanetary Control Force (ICF) is tasked with upholding law and order across the stars. Its ranks are made up of highly trained specialists who serve as law enforcers, prison guards, and ecological inspectors, each equipped with advanced weapons and reliable transport suited to their duties. While their primary jurisdiction lies in space, the ICF occasionally operates on planetary surfaces, usually to detain prisoners for later pickup by large ships or to pursue wanted criminals. Due to the vastness of space, ICF resources are spread thin, leaving many units small and widely dispersed. Officers frequently operate alone or in small teams, which demands exceptional skills, self-reliance, and discipline. As a result, they tend to be zealous in their commitment to the law and show little tolerance for those who break it, even for something as minor as a speeding violation.

### Thundera

In the seventh quadrant of a distant galaxy orbited a shimmering, bejeweled planet with two moons. Thundera’s atmosphere was rich in oxygen, and its twelve great seas supported a thriving ecosystem, including the noble humanoid feline race known as Thunderians. Presided over by the fabled Cats’ Lair, seat of Imperial power, the Thunderians cultivated a prosperous society of science, architecture, art, and magic. Among them were the ThunderCats, an elite order of noble warriors sworn to defend their civilization. Through their vigilance, Thundera enjoyed relative peace, despite repeated invasions from the mu’tants of its sister planet, Plun-Darr. Thundera was a world of sprawling cities powered by advanced technology and guided by ancient magic, but its splendor was not to endure. A devastating catastrophe shattered the planet, scattering its people across the stars.

### Geography

Thundera was a breathtaking planet filled with fertile meadows of feather-soft grasses, verdant forests, singing rains, rainbow mountains, and glittering crystal canyons. Sky architects controlled the weather and kept it perfect for civilization to thrive. The capital, Grimalkingrad, rose around a dormant volcano where the Eye of Thundera was discovered. Its domes and minarets glimmered in the sun, dwarfed only by the monumental Cats’ Lair, home of the royal family.

### History

Thundera was originally uninhabited until the Great Migration, when peaceful and intelligent cat-like humanoids arrived and settled the planet, calling themselves Thunderians. Their leader discovered the Eye of Thundera in an ancient lava basin atop Mount Grimalkin, the dormant volcano around which the capital would later rise. When the Eye was freed, a scarlet beam shot into the sky, and the leader became the first ThunderCat, establishing a code of Justice, Truth, Honor, and Loyalty. Under the guidance of this mystic king, the newly settled Thundera flourished. Generations later, Thundera faced interplanetary war as the mu’tants of its sister planet, Plun-Darr, sought its rich resources. The ThunderCats, composed of Thundera’s noble families, repeatedly fought back against the invading mu’tants using a new weapon forged with the Eye of Thundera known as the Sword of Omens. Over time, mu’tant attacks grew more brutal. This took a toll on Thundera’s last king, Claudus, Lion-O’s father. Weakened, nearly blind, and mourning his wife, Claudus could no longer care for his infant son or the sacred treasures of Thundera. With his energy spent on ruling from the throne, he entrusted the planet’s future and Lion-O’s safety to his most trusted warrior, Jaga, who would act as Lord of the ThunderCats until Lion-O came of age. During this period, Plun-Darr’s most vicious leader, Ratilla the Terrible, arrived wielding the mighty Sword of Plun-Darr, a weapon nearly rivaling the power of the Sword of Omens. Jaga and the ThunderCats defeated Ratilla, casting his sword deep into Thundera’s canyons in hopes of containing its power. However, that power refused to stay locked away.

#### DESTRUCTION

It was later discovered that the Sword of Plun-Darr’s immense energy triggered catastrophic events that would destroy Thundera. To prevent widespread panic, leaders returned to their provinces to deliver the news personally and guide the exodus. In poorer regions, arks carried as many as possible, but there were never enough ships. In wealthier areas, most

citizens were able to escape with relative ease. The capital had a surplus of transports, which Jaga redirected to the underrepresented regions. Some of these ships reached their destinations in time, while others did not. Jaga put the whole of Thundera before the ThunderCats, even if it only meant saving a few more lives. Once the first leg of the exodus was underway, he turned his attention to the nobles, but the situation quickly worsened. The planet’s destruction accelerated beyond predictions, and its crust began to fracture, sending tremors across Thundera. Realizing survival through the night was unlikely, Jaga prepared to lead the final wave, while ensuring women, children, and the next Lord of the ThunderCats departed first under the protection of Jaga’s inner circle. Claudus, however, insisted that Jaga lead the first wave, fearing the loss of his successor would doom Thundera’s future. He reminded Jaga that his own choice as Lord had been deliberate, and that the safety of the next generation was paramount. Though hesitant, Jaga agreed, understanding that this would be the final time he and Claudus would meet. Though countless ships would still fail to leave the planet, the ThunderCats preserved their culture and their people. By saving what they could, they ensured that their civilization would survive, carrying the promise of a New Thundera for future generations.

### Third Earth

The world known as Third Earth is a small blue-green planet, third in line from its sun. Covered largely with water, Third Earth became the refuge of the ThunderCats when their homeworld, Thundera, was destroyed The planet is one of extremes, where overgrown wilderness and chaotic magic collide to create unpredictable and untamed environments. Among the wilds, ancient remnants of long-forgotten civilizations, both magical and technological, emerge from the overgrowth, holding secrets that can be either dangerous or helpful. Third Earth is home to a great variety of species, some of whom welcomed the ThunderCats, while others resisted them. Indigenous people and visitors from other worlds inhabit its lands, possessing a wide mixture of technology and magic. Most civilizations remain separate, interacting only occasionally, often through small conflicts over resources.

### Geography

Third Earth has been repeatedly reshaped by technological warfare and the unpredictable surge of powerful magic. The result is a hodgepodge of crazy environments blended with traditional wilderness. Normal fields and woods exist alongside forests alive with desires and scorching deserts of phosphorus sands, all concealing crazy creatures and ancient ruins of societies long forgotten. Bottomless ravines cut through the crust, while labyrinthine passages run beneath the surface. Any and all types of environments both imagined and unimagined exist on Third Earth, sometimes right next to each other. Environmental logic has no place here. Many environments beyond the sparse settlements, and even some that are settled, are unbelievably hazardous.

### History

Third Earth is the future incarnation of planet Earth (“First Earth”), long after the decline of human civilization. No-one truly knows the exact cause of its reshaping from First Earth to Second Earth. Some believe the devil-priest Mumm-Ra played a role, while others claim humanity led to its own destruction through world-shattering thundrillium weaponry. Perhaps the truth lies somewhere in between. Second Earth marked the rise of a new age where magic eclipsed science. Though remnants of technology persisted, such as genetically altered creatures, haywire robots, and rusting weaponry, the people who survived slowly began to rebuild into a medieval-like society. Over time, they learned to harness mystical secrets, allowing them to forge powerful artifacts and cultivating new forms of magical power. The shift from Second Earth to Third Earth is similarly unclear. During this era, Mumm-Ra is thought to have grown more active, amassing artifacts and clashing with rival mages for supremacy. Other powerful entities also rose to prominence, and perhaps it was this growing power that led to unstable magics which brought forth Third Earth.

#### EVER-LIVING EVIL

Third Earth is post-apocalyptic. The magical civilizations have vanished, leaving only relics and ruins, while the technological achievements of First Earth exist only as buried remnants. Taking advantage of the new age, surviving magical beings, from Second Earth and other planets, seized opportunities to expand their influence. Evil fought evil, until eventually Third Earth was divided into two domains. The devil-priest Mumm-Ra ruled the northern hemisphere, and the evil Zaxx, a mystically powerful four-armed anthropomorphic bird, claimed the south. Zaxx and Mumm-Ra clashed repeatedly for control of the planet, and during one such battle, Mumm-Ra seized Zaxx’s medallion, trapping his essence and becoming the sole ruler of Third Earth. Yet Mumm-Ra’s dominance did not last, as the great Rammastide uprising saw the oppressed peoples of Third Earth unite against him. Despite his immense power, Mumm-Ra could not withstand the combined might of the rebellion and was ultimately driven back to the Onyx Pyramid. There he rested, allowing the people to grow complacent and believe he had been defeated, waiting patiently for the right moment to return. Over time, the unity of the rebellion dissolved, with tribes and peoples drifting apart, and Mumm-Ra’s evil slowly faded into legend. When the ThunderCats and mu’tants later arrived on Third Earth, Mumm-Ra awoke. Sensing an opportunity to reclaim power, he forged an uneasy alliance with the mu’tants and sought to harness the Eye of Thundera. Together, they posed a formidable threat to both the ThunderCats and the inhabitants of the planet. As the ThunderCats settled on Third Earth, they began exploring their new home and interacting with its residents. While some welcome them as defenders, many see their ongoing battles with the mu’tants as a destabilizing force. The ThunderCats’ code of Thundera is changing these opinions and earning them allies, while the mu’tants’ cruelty leaves them increasingly isolated. From their stronghold, the Cats’ Lair, the ThunderCats now defend Third Earth against Mumm-Ra, the mu’tants, and any other threats, protecting their new home and the friends they have gained.

### Points of Interest

The ThunderCats made planetfall in the northern hemisphere of Third Earth, once dominated by the devil-priest Mumm-Ra. While the planet holds many continents and scattered islands, the lands most significant to the ThunderCats’ adventures in northern Third Earth are detailed below in alphabetical order.

#### ACID LAKE

#### BLACK TOWER

Surrounded by beds of stinging flowers, Acid Lake churns with potent ultrasolvic acid capable of quickly dissolving flesh, organic material, and even the hardest metals. A faint, caustic haze lingers in the air around its surface. Rising from the Forest of Gorgons and Manticores, the Black Tower of Baron Karnor is a tall, narrow, and foreboding structure. The tower served as both the baron’s lair and the vault for his stolen treasures, and is riddled with traps designed to punish any who dare trespass. At the summit, stone gargoyles perch silently, ever watchful. Baron Karnor was a ruthless robber, feared across the land for his greed and cruelty. He preyed on the inhabitants of Third Earth, seizing gold, jewels, and magical artifacts without mercy. Using his arcane powers, Karnor animated stone gargoyles to do his bidding, sending them to acquire more wealth and enforce his dominion. Inside, the Black Tower is a labyrinth of danger, filled with hidden pitfalls, swinging blades, and powerful arcane wards. Karnor spent his life guarding his hoard and devising new traps, and even in death, his corpse remains entwined with the treasures he coveted so fiercely. ADVENTURE HOOK Mumm-Ra has hired a cyborg mercenary known as the Driller to tunnel from the lake to Cats’ Lair, intending to flood the ThunderCats’ home with the deadly acid. The only known substance able to neutralize ultrasolvic acid is sponge fog, a pale pink, highly alkaline growth found only in a misty valley bordering the rockfolk’s territory. Dense and rubbery, sponge fog resists ordinary tools and can be harvested only with expertly forged, razor-sharp blades.

#### BERBIL VILLAGE

Governed by the Berbil High Council, the Berbil Village is the Third Earth home of the berbils. Nestled in a lush field filled with colorful flowers and shrubs, the village is made up of mushroom-shaped houses, all constructed by the berbils themselves. The villagers grow berbil fruit as their primary food source, tending carefully to the orchards that surround their homes. Despite its quaint appearance, Berbil Village is surprisingly technologically sophisticated. The berbils have installed an advanced alarm system that alerts them to any approaching danger, and a nearby aqueduct and reservoir connect the village with Cat’s Lair, ensuring a steady water supply. While outsiders assume the mushroom-shaped houses are a deliberate choice to mask their true capabilities, the real reason is simply that the homes are quick and easy to rebuild. The berbils of the village are master engineers and mechanics, filling the village with tools and equipment used to repair everything from simple gadgets to complex devices. An older-style communicator allows them to maintain contact with Cats’ Lair, ensuring that the village is never isolated from its allies. ADVENTURE HOOK Located near Cats’ Lair and being allied to the ThunderCats, the Berbil Village lives under both protection and peril. Most of Third Earth’s native threats avoid the area, wary of upsetting the ThunderCats. Yet those who oppose the heroes often target the village intentionally, hoping to lure the ThunderCats into traps or distract them from darker schemes. ADVENTURE HOOK Though Baron Karnor has long since died, his animated stone Gargoyles continue to terrorize the people of Third Earth. They steal valuables from nearby villages and return them to the trap-filled Black Tower, keeping the surrounding lands under the dark shadow of the baron’s legacy.

#### BOTTOMLESS CHASM

Stretching for miles across northern Third Earth, the Bottomless Chasm is a vast ravine that seems to cut through the world itself. Its depth is so immense that no explorer has ever reached the bottom, leading many to claim it has none at all. Strange pulses of “Cosmic Rays” sometimes rise from the depths, warping the air and searing those who stray too close. Despite its hostile nature, legends persist of exiled creatures dwelling far below, remnants of those banished by Mumm-Ra in ages past. ADVENTURE HOOK Mumm-Ra has long used the Bottomless Chasm as an inescapable prison, a place where his enemies vanish without a trace. He breaks their will through endless torment until exhaustion dulls their senses, then distorts space itself to open a hidden portal that swallows them whole. Within the Chasm’s depths, many remain trapped, unable to find the invisible gateway that brought them there or the secret key that could set them free.

#### BRIDGE OF LIGHT

#### CASTLE OF THE SNOWFOLK

The Bridge of Light is a shimmering overpass that appears only when a magical crystal, guarded by the Unicorn Keepers, is placed upon a mystic platform at the riverbank. When aligned with the sun’s rays, the crystal’s brilliance solidifies into a radiant span of pure light, creating a temporary path across the River of Despair. The bridge offers safe passage over the river’s treacherous currents and the monstrous creatures lurking beneath, but it is fragile by nature. Any burst of magical energy disrupts its power, causing the bridge to flicker and collapse into the river below. Perched atop Hook Mountain, the Castle of the Snowfolk is a striking palace carved entirely from ice and stone, its surfaces shimmering with a faint blue glow. Within its glittering spires, the snowfolk make their home under the rule of King Hanuman. A deep, treacherous ravine encircles the fortress, leaving only a sturdy drawbridge to grant passage to its gates. Inside, winding, ornate halls echo with the movements and voices of the snowfolk, while grand chambers showcase treasures, intricate carvings, and enchanted frost gardens. ADVENTURE HOOK The River of Despair is perilous, its swirling depths filled with unseen dangers. The Bridge of Light provides the only reliable means of crossing, yet it demands caution. A single errant spell or energy blast made in panic can destabilize the structure, plunging travelers into the deadly waters. To make matters worse, tampering with the crystal, whether through sabotage or careless curiosity, can cause the entire bridge to vanish mid-crossing. ADVENTURE HOOK Beneath the Castle of the Snowfolk, hidden deep within its frozen dungeons, lies a jeweled egg guarded by a fierce dragon with scalding, steamy breath. Inside the egg rests the frozen body of a long-lost princess. Near the Castle, encased in ice, slumbers the giant Ice King, who seeks the egg above all else. Centuries ago, he was a handsome prince whose beloved princess fell victim to a cruel curse, leaving her trapped forever within the jeweled shell. Now, the Ice King desires nothing more than to embrace the egg and be reunited with his princess within its icy prison. He has lost the power of speech, and any who dare stand in his way risk being frozen solid should he ever awaken.

#### BRIDGE OF SLIME

The Bridge of Slime is a treacherous overpass stretching across the River of Despair, its surface slick with glistening ooze that clings to everything it touches. Said to lead toward the Pit of the Nether-Witch, the bridge’s unstable footing makes each step a risk, and few travelers attempt the crossing without magical or mechanical aid. ADVENTURE HOOK Unlike the Bridge of Light, this vile span offers no protection from the horrors below, but is the only viable path to the Nether-Witch. The dark waters churn with relentless motion, home to the fearsome sea monster Gaw-Rak-Rak, which waits for travelers foolish enough to falter.

#### CASTLE PLUN-DARR

The slime that covers Castle Plun’Darr glints sharply in the morning sunlight, oozing down walls. Located in a dreary swamp, it’s the imposing stronghold and base of operations for the mu’tants on Third Earth. Designed in the shape of a bat-like creature, its massive wings can fold inward, forming a double-layered shield over the main entrance. A protective moat surrounds the fortress and supplies its water, however the moat is infused with phosphor-r, a glowing substance that alerts the mu’tants to any disturbances. The bat-shaped head of the castle houses the main control room, its enormous eyes serving as panoramic windows that survey the surrounding lands. The mouth conceals a powerful

cannon capable of firing fireballs, channeling magical energy over great distances, and even projecting a tractor beam. Built from salvaged parts of the mu’tants’ crashed spaceship, the weapon requires a remote spotter to call in trajectory corrections, as it lacks automated targeting. Above the head, the execution chamber known as the Four Winds awaits defiant intruders, using four spring-loaded catapults to tear victims apart with terrifying precision. Inside, escalators connect the upper levels, while the lowest levels hold dungeons for prisoners or experimental subjects. The fortress serves as a garrison, with the four chief officers, Slithe, Monkian, Jackalman, and Vultureman, maintaining their own quarters, while the remaining mu’tants are housed in barracks. The castle also contains a galley and a secret laboratory belonging to Vultureman. ADVENTURE HOOK Infiltrating the mu’tants’ stronghold to rescue captives or sabotage the cannon is challenging. Every corner of the castle is filled with dangers, and the glowing moat ensures that even the stealthiest intruders leave a trail. Those who enter must be clever, resourceful, and ready for a fight against an enemy that is both cunning and cruel.

#### CATS’ LAIR

Nestled within a marble rock formation on the Plains of Fertility, Cats’ Lair is the formidable fortress of the ThunderCats on Third Earth, crafted to resemble a massive sitting cat. Rising 165 feet, the fortress features a head shaped like the ThunderCats’ emblem, enormous paws, and an array of defensive and offensive systems, much of which was salvaged from the ThunderCats’ crashed flagship. Constructed with the berbils’ help, Cats’ Lair is organized across six floors, each filled with specialized chambers and strategic features. At the very top, the Cats Head serves as a swiveling lookout, outfitted with telescopic scanners and laser-firing eyes. The lair’s arms contain powerful laser cannons capable of repelling both aerial and ground attacks. A retractable electronic drawbridge spans the canyon below, controlling access to the fortress, while a giant ramp outside one paw allows flying vehicles to take off with ease. MAIN CONTROL ROOM Located on the sixth floor, the Control Room is the main area of operations. A large main panel manages most of the lair’s functions, including the drawbridge, the Cats’ paws, and power distribution. Three smaller sub-control panels allow the ThunderCats to operate critical systems remotely. The ceiling is open to reveal complex machinery, and a moving lift provides access for maintenance and repairs.

ARMORY This weapons room contains all ThunderCat weaponry. Racks and storage cases line the walls, each labeled and maintained with precision. BARRACK Each ThunderCat has a private bedroom. Each room contains a bed, wardrobe, and occasional small furniture. BASEMENT Beneath the lair, the Basement generates and distributes power. Here, thundrillium is purified and processed through smelting furnaces and conduits, supplying energy to the lair’s systems and fueling the ThunderCats’ vehicles. There is also a smithy present, making use of the smelting furnaces. CONFERENCE ROOM Situated on the third floor, the Conference Room or Council Chamber serves as the ThunderCats’ substantially spacious meeting area and war room. A wide circular table marked with their emblem dominates the space, surrounded by a chair for each member of the team.

DINING ROOM Adjacent to the kitchen, the Dining Room features a large rectangular table capable of seating the entire team. Snarf not only cooks but also serves the ThunderCats’ meals here. GUEST ROOM A spare bedroom for visitors, the Guest Room mirrors the ThunderCats’ own quarters with a bed, wardrobe, and small furnishings. LABORATORY The lab is filled with chemicals, flasks, test tubes, and experimental equipment. It supports a wide range of experiments, including antidotes, fuel development, and other innovations to aid the ThunderCats in their adventures. LIBRARY During their time on Third Earth, the ThunderCats have gathered a small collection of books detailing the planet and its history. Though their busy lives rarely leave time for leisure reading, these volumes prove invaluable whenever they need to research the mysteries of their new home. HANGAR The ground floor Hangar houses the ThunderCats’ vehicles and also functions as both a workshop and a storehouse. Giant cat paw doors lift at a 45-degree angle to allow entry and exit, controlled from both the Main Control Room and inside the hangar. KITCHEN Found on the fourth floor, the kitchen is compact yet wellequipped with a sink, pantry, utensils, and cooking tools. SWORD CHAMBER On the fifth floor lies the Sword Chamber, a secure vault housing the Sword of Omens and Claw Shield. The chamber features a central stand and a secret rock-activated locking mechanism. The chamber’s walls and door are virtually impregnable, and the only alternative entrance is through a ventilation duct. TRAINING AREA On the second floor of the lair, the Training Area serves as an indoor fighting arena where the ThunderCats can hone and perfect their fighting skills as well as their physical prowess.

#### CAVES OF THE TROLLS AND GIANTS

Carved into the side of a great mountain, the Caves of the Trolls and Giants form a sprawling network of caverns of every size. The smaller caves serve as homes to the short, stocky trolls, while the vast chambers belong to the towering giants. Despite their differences, these two unlikely peoples have created a rare peaceful settlement. The trolls can often be seen riding enormous grasshoppers along winding mountain paths, their laughter echoing through the cliffs. The gentle giants, in contrast, move with thunderous grace, draped in simple garments that reflect their calm and patient nature. ADVENTURE HOOK Deep within the cave lies a rich vein of thundrillium. Harvesting it is dangerous, as even a brief stay can rapidly age a miner into frailty or beyond. Clever harvesters may be able to gather thundrillium quickly, but any prolonged effort will almost certainly be fatal. Only the hidden Geyser of Life can reverse this accelerated aging, yet its waters are fiercely guarded by vicious Winged Water Snakes, ensuring the consequences of failure are severe. ADVENTURE HOOK Those who travel through this region must tread carefully. The giants, though friendly and well-meaning, are easily distracted and often fail to notice visitors beneath them. More than one traveler has narrowly escaped being flattened by a wandering foot or a misplaced boulder.

#### CAVE OF TIME

The Cave of Time is a mysterious cavern with the unnatural power to alter the age of those who enter. Any creature that steps inside begins to age rapidly. Minutes spent within can add years to a person’s life, leaving them weaker, slower, or even too weak to leave if they linger too long.

#### CLIFFS OF VERTIGO

#### CRYSTAL KINGDOM

The Crumbling Cliffs of Vertigo rise sharply, their sheer faces leading toward Mount Anguish. The rock is brittle and unstable, making any attempt to scale them a dangerous endeavor. Even experienced climbers risk sudden falls or being trapped by shifting stone. High in the snowy mountain peaks lies the Crystal Kingdom, a city carved entirely from ice and ruled with an iron fist by the tyrannical Queen Tartara. Sunlight catches the towers, palace, and humble homes, making them gleam like living crystal, while cloaked citizens move through the streets in furs and embroidered silks. Onion-shaped domes glint in sapphire and emerald hues, and the palace walls shimmer with intricate frost patterns. Within its countless chambers lie hidden secrets, including a vast treasure room where the queen hoards her wealth and imprisons victims in ice. Queen Tartara dominates her kingdom with cruelty. Her palace is guarded fiercely by loyal soldiers, yet even they are forced to wear electronic blindfolds to prevent them from seeing her treasures. She hoards wealth with obsessive greed, taking pleasure in amassing items of value and beauty for herself alone. Occasionally, she leaves the palace in her flying jet-sleigh, hunting for new treasures to add to her collection. ADVENTURE HOOK Mount Anguish serves as a site of dark rituals for Mumm-Ra. The Cliffs of Vertigo offer the most direct path to the summit, but their treacherous nature turns any ascent into a deadly gauntlet. Heroes seeking to stop the devil-priest must navigate the unstable cliffs, where a single misstep could mean a fatal fall.

#### CRYSTAL CANYON

The Crystal Canyon is as breathtaking as it is perilous. Towering crystal cliffs rise from the canyon floor, their smooth, mirror-like surfaces scattering light in dazzling patterns. Jagged outcrops and narrow passages make travel extremely difficult, especially for larger vehicles, while the reflective surfaces constantly challenge even the most careful navigator. ADVENTURE HOOK Engravings in the form of arrow-like designs cover the canyon walls, scattered and seemingly random. Deeper into the canyon, the patterns grow more regular, forming a trail that guides the way. Following this trail eventually leads to a cave adorned with stalactites, where the legendary Keystone lies. This large green gem radiates pure energy, capable of revitalizing both living beings and mechanical devices. Yet its power comes at a cost. Those who use the Keystone quickly develop an addictive dependence, ultimately becoming enslaved and then destroyed by it . ADVENTURE HOOK The Arietta Bird is a dazzling creature that often visits the Berbil Village. Its glittering plumage rivals even the most magnificent peacock, and its enchanting song brings the promise of a bountiful harvest to the berbils. Yet its beauty is also its greatest danger, as the bird’s radiant feathers and melodious voice attract numerous evil beings, eager to capture it for their own purposes. Over time, these threats have reduced its numbers, and now only a single arietta bird is believed to remain on Third Earth. Among those who covet the bird is Queen Tartara. Obsessed with possessing the arietta, she seeks to imprison it in her icy domain, where its song would echo only for her.

#### DARK SIDE

Dark Side is a dark, desolate, and dangerous region long considered forbidden by the natives of Third Earth. Its landscape is jagged and unforgiving, filled with craggy mountains, sharp rocks, and rivers of flowing lava. Because of its treacherous terrain and cursed legacy, most inhabitants avoid it entirely. Long ago, during Mumm-Ra’s rule over Third Earth, the Lunataks arrived seeking to conquer its lands. Mumm-Ra responded by defeating and encasing them in the molten lava within Dark Side. Many years later, he commanded the mu’tants to free the Lunataks so they could join forces against the ThunderCats. Once freed, the Lunataks established their mobile fortress, Sky-Tomb, within Dark Side. The region is further isolated by the thundrainium pits of Fire Rock Mountain, making access nearly impossible for the ThunderCats due to the dangerous concentrations of thundrainium. Despite this, the ThunderCats eventually discover an alternative route through the Forest of Mists. At the forest’s edge lies a long canyon lined with razor-sharp rock formations, which only the ThunderCat Lynx-O can navigate safely using his specialized Braille Board. ADVENTURE HOOK As a forbidden land, Dark Side conceals ancient secrets removed from modern knowledge. Its ruins hold powerful artifacts from Second Earth, while strange creatures, possibly even remnants from First Earth, roam its shadowy expanse. If the Lunataks were to claim these relics, it could bring disaster, and any journey into Dark Side carries the risk of encountering these lingering threats.

#### DESERT OF LAVA GEYSERS

The Desert of Lava Geysers is a vast wasteland. Full of sand and obelisk-like rocky outcrops, the desert is located near Mumm-Rana the Ever-Good’s Opal Pyramid. While appearing benign, the desert spews hot lava at exactly noon every day. The lava floods the entire desert, making it a very dangerous place to be. Eventually the lava drains off slowly. ADVENTURE HOOK Mumm-Rana at the Opal Pyramid is often a target of evil-doers seeking to attack her while she’s vulnerable. Those who seek ill against her and her pyramid must cross the Desert of Lava Geysers. The intelligent among her enemies know to do so close to noon, so as to impede any interlopers who may be tracking them. Even beyond attempts to breach the Pyramid, this deadly tactic serves as an effective means to shake any pursuers.

#### DESERT OF SINKING SANDS

#### DESTINY ATOLL

The Desert of Sinking Sands is a massive, bone-strewn wasteland that sprawls around Mumm-Ra’s Onyx Pyramid. Few dare to cross its endless dunes, for the land itself seems alive with malice. Hidden pockets of treacherous quicksand lie in wait, swallowing the unwary and delivering their lifeless remains directly to Mumm-Ra’s evil domain for use in his dark rituals. A small, remote tropical island, Destiny Atoll was named by the Thunderian Lynx-O in thanks that the Thunderian refugees’ destiny was to survive. After some berbils rescued them from the dying Thundera, their ship crashed onto Third Earth, leaving them stranded on this lush island. A sparkling lake lies at the island’s heart, spilling into the surrounding sea, while dense jungle trees heavy with fruit and coconuts cover much of the land. A mountainous ridge rises alongside an inactive volcano whose summit churns with foul magic. Though beautiful, Destiny Atoll is also battered by powerful tropical storms and is home to some of the largest creatures on Third Earth. ADVENTURE HOOK The Desert of Sinking Sands serves as both a barrier and a weapon in Mumm-Ra’s defense. Through foul incantations, the devil-priest can command the sands to shift and swallow those who linger in one place too long, whether quicksand lies beneath them or not. Those claimed by the desert are drawn into his lair, where they are reanimated as cursed mummified servants of their new master. ADVENTURE HOOK The dark power that shrouds the volcano draws anything flying overhead into a strange well of lost time, casting them into one of Third Earth’s past incarnations. Rumors also speak of a reclusive mystic dwelling within the volcano’s depths, said to be bound by blood to Mumm-Ra and determined to end his evil once and for all.

#### DISMAL FALLS

At the edge of a jagged cliff, the River of Poison plunges into the depths below, forming the massive cascade known as Dismal Falls. Its toxic waters spill into the Swamp of Serpents, cloaking the surrounding region in a choking, venomous mist. The air burns to breathe, and even the fearless ThunderCats approach only in insulated catsuits designed to withstand the deadly haze. ADVENTURE HOOK Dismal Falls serves as the vital chokepoint between the River of Poison and the Swamp of Serpents. Any disturbance to its flow or stability could have disastrous consequences either lowering the swamp’s water level and stirring the creatures that dwell beneath, or flooding the entire region, sweeping away everything caught in its venomous surge.

#### ENFLAMER’S VOLCANO

Enflamer’s Volcano is a towering, active volcano. Centuries ago, a Dwarf Star crashed into its core, infusing it with star fire and raising its temperature far beyond that of any normal volcano. Within its depths dwells the fiery entity known as the Enflamer. A winding cave on the mountainside leads deep into the heart of the volcano, where treacherous caverns twist around lakes of molten fire, steaming waterfalls, and swarms of energy bats. ADVENTURE HOOK The cosmic heat of the star fire can forge or repair legendary artifacts, yet no living being can endure its intensity. Only the Enflamer, immune to this searing power, can shape such creations, though always in exchange for copious amounts of molten gold. Those who seek him must tread carefully, however, for the Enflamer’s favor is fickle, and a newly forged or reforged artifact may just as easily be turned against its master as serve them.

#### FIELD OF DAGGERS

The Field of Daggers appears at first to be nothing more than a stretch of barren, rocky terrain. In truth, it hides a deadly secret. Whenever someone attempts to cross it, massive stone spikes erupt from the ground at lightning speed, impaling anything in their path. These stony blades strike with such force that they can lift and shatter entire vehicles. ADVENTURE HOOK The Field of Daggers is the shortest land route to Mount Anguish, a site of Mumm-Ra’s dark rituals. It is also the only path accessible to vehicles. Heroes seeking to halt the devil-priest’s rites must brave this lethal ground, where every step risks impalement. The region also serves as a treacherous escape route for villains, making pursuit through its shifting stones a deadly gamble.

#### FIRE ROCK MOUNTAIN

Fire Rock Mountain is a perilous region marked by sharp, jagged, rocky outcrops, and thin rocky bridges and valleys. The deep canyons below glow with fiery veins of thundrainium, called “fire rocks” by locals of Third Earth. This highly radioactive substance is extremely toxic to Thunderians, whose strength falters in its presence. A network of caves run through the mountain, accessible only by a single winding stone bridge spanning over the fiery pits. ADVENTURE HOOK Because thundrainium saps the strength of Thunderians, Fire Rock Mountain makes a perfect prison for captured ThunderCats. Any Thunderian rescue attempt would falter long before reaching its fiery depths, their bodies succumbing to the draining power. Only those unaffected by thundrainium can brave its glowing caverns to attempt a rescue, or uncover what secrets lie hidden within.

#### FOREST OF GIANT INSECTS

The Forest of Giant Insects is a dense woodland of towering trees and tangled vegetation. As its name suggests, the forest teems with enormous insects of every kind. It is a perilous place, especially after nightfall when these creatures emerge to feed. Even the ThunderCats avoid entering its shadowed depths unless absolutely necessary.

ADVENTURE HOOK Few venture willingly into the Forest of Giant Insects. Yet for those ambushed within the nearby rockfolk territories, it may serve as the only escape from the labyrinth of canyons and cliffs where enraged rockfolk hurl boulders at intruders. Rumors also tell of a white flower that blooms deep within the forest’s dark heart, growing amid treacherous quicksand, said to heal even magical memory loss.

#### FOREST OF MISTS

The Forest of Mists is shrouded in a dense, unending fog that gives the region its name. The haze is so thick that even seasoned travelers struggle to find their bearings, and few who enter ever emerge unscathed. The forest lies at the edge of Dark Side, connected by a narrow cavern lined with jagged stone spires that pierce the path. ADVENTURE HOOK Because Dark Side is encircled by Fire Rock Mountain and the thundrainium pits, reaching it is nearly impossible for Thunderians, who are weakened by exposure to thundrainium. The Forest of Mists therefore becomes their only possible route. Yet the path is fraught with danger; visibility is almost nonexistent, and the cavern beyond is deadly in its own right. Only those with heightened senses or powerful energy-draining thundrillium fog lamps stand any chance of making the journey alive.

#### FOREST OF SILENCE

The Forest of Silence is an eerie woodland where no sound can exist. Creatures within the forest cannot make noise, nor can they hear anything around them. This unnatural quiet makes the forest especially dangerous, as even the most desperate calls for help go unheard. ADVENTURE HOOK The Forest of Silence is an ideal place for ambushes or secret schemes. Enemies of the ThunderCats might lure them deep into its depths with captives, using the unnatural quiet to strike without warning. It also serves as a hidden lair for dark plans, such as Mumm-Ra’s secret cocoons containing duploids. Here, the evil replicas of the ThunderCats can grow undisturbed, ready to be unleashed at the perfect moment.

#### FOUR DAY DROP

The Four Day Drop is a vast abyss hidden deep within the forest, serving as a shortcut between Cats’ Lair and the Tree Top Kingdom. This enormous circular pit is said to have formed when a small meteor struck Third Earth. According to the Warrior Maidens, anyone who falls into the Drop will take four days to reach the bottom, which is how it earned its name. The only way to cross safely is by using an old, rotting tree that stretches across the chasm. ADVENTURE HOOK Crossing the Four Day Drop is already a risky undertaking, but the log is also the site of daring duels. Two warriors will often meet at the center, striking at the log until one yields or the log gives way, sending both combatants plummeting into the darkness below. Afterward, the log is replaced for continued passage or the next deadly contest.

#### GARDEN OF DELIGHTS

The Garden of Delights is a utopic place hidden deep underground. It appears as a paradise of vibrant flowers, colorful animals, and fluttering insects, but is actually a carefully crafted illusion. Tending this garden is a talking, human-faced plant known as Silky. ADVENTURE HOOK The garden is the creation of Mumm-Ra, designed to lure his enemies into his schemes. Within this false paradise, Mumm-Ra takes the form of Silky. Mumm-Ra draws intruders to the Garden through a series of mini earthquakes and the appearance of a massive burrowing worm. Once they arrive, the flowers bloom and spread pollen which charms the mind, then Silky offers a yellow melon-like fruit that induces vivid hallucinations and overwhelming euphoria. Those who partake become obsessed with obtaining another fruit, obeying Mumm-Ra’s commands with little question. Only those with exceptionally strong wills can resist the garden’s enchantment and uncover its true danger.

#### GEYSER OF LIFE

Not far from the Cave of Time lies the hidden Geyser of Life, a shimmering spring circled by rose-colored plants and rising stone cliffs. Its waters possess potent magic capable of reversing age itself, restoring youth to those who bathe in it. Yet few can approach its radiant pool, for it is fiercely guarded by a swarm of winged water snakes that strike at any who draw too near. ADVENTURE HOOK Those who have aged prematurely within the Cave of Time may seek the Geyser of Life as their only hope of restoration. Doing so requires contending with the Winged Water Snakes, however.

#### GREAT OCEANIC PLUG

The Great Oceanic Plug is a massive mechanical structure built atop a colossal fissure on the ocean floor of Third Earth. Centuries ago, this fissure opened, threatening to drain the oceans and extinguish the planet’s core. In what is celebrated as the greatest engineering feat in Third Earth’s history, the hole was sealed, preserving the world’s seas and life itself. Since that time, the Plug has remained under constant watch. Its guardian and crew maintain strict neutrality, standing vigilant regardless of politics or war. At the helm is Dr. Dometone, the Plug’s director and principal guardian, who oversees daily operations and maintenance. He is aided by two loyal robots, Hercules and Samson, who patrol the structure and are themselves carefully maintained by the engineering team. brute called Scrape, to Third Earth to pull the Great Oceanic Plug, drain the sea, and harvest the rock.. Dr. Dometone took Hercules ashore for supplies without realizing that Scrape was nearby. Soon after, Samson was attacked by Scrape’s ship, an electric robot eel. Samson didn’t have a chance. He quickly melted down into a frazzled heap of twisted metal. Dr. Dometone believed the same thing would happen to Hercules if he tried to fight Scrape, so he now seeks out external help.

#### GRUNE’S TOMB

Hidden beneath a towering tree on a windswept plain lies Grune’s Tomb, a vast underground vault that imprisons Grune the Destroyer, a Thunderian warrior cast into exile for his betrayal.
