When forged into a weapon, Thundore is lightweight and exceedingly well-balanced. Weapons made from Thundore have the following additional properties. Flexible Construction. The weapon’s weight is halved, and it has the Finesse property if it didn’t already. Well-Balanced. The weapon scores a Critical Hit on a roll of 19 or 20 on the d20.

#### ARMOR

ARMOR Armor coated in thundrainium loses its effect after the wearer takes damage 10 times. Armor coated in thundrainium has the following additional property. Thundera’s Bane. While wearing this armor, Critical Hits from Thunderians against you become normal hits. When forged into Medium or Heavy armor, Thundore can regulate life systems, thus forming a protective exoskeleton around the wearer that helps keep them alive. Armor made from Thundore has the following additional properties. Flexible Construction. The armor’s weight is halved. While wearing Medium armor made of Thundore, you can apply your full Dexterity modifier (instead of a maximum of 2) when determining your Armor Class. In addition, Medium or Heavy armor made of Thundore doesn’t impose Disadvantage on your Dexterity (Stealth) checks if it normally would. Life System Regulation. While wearing armor made of

### Thundore

Thundore Thundrainium can also be smelted, refined, and treated into a strong but flexible material called Thundore, though the process is incredibly dangerous and can easily result in a devastating explosion. If done correctly, however, it can provide incredible martial advantages. These advantages come at a cost for Thunderians, however, as the latent radiation from the original thundrainium still causes some limitations. Nonetheless, the advantages gained are sometimes worth the discomfort. Thunderian Dampening. All Thundore items prevent Thunderians from using or benefitting from any of their Species traits while on their person. ALTERNATIVE CHARGES The universal game term of “charges” is a collective reference to energy or power stored inside an item for future use by that same or another item. Narratively, charges can be referred to as a fuel gauge, power cells, mana batteries, or, in ThunderCats, thundrillium reserves. They are, ultimately, where nearly every technology or magic-adjacent equipment draws their power from in order to function. Most charges in ThunderCats will come from the many states of thundrillium. The GM may decide, however, that there are other sources of charges available as well. Raw mystic power, mana, life-draining necrotech, plasma infusions, and many other forms of energy can be harnessed, condensed, and catalyzed into charges. Within the confines of the ThunderCats setting, these are rare occurrences due to the existence of thundrillium, but it doesn’t mean they couldn’t be a possible solution when charges become scarce.

### Thundrillium Devices

Fueled by the incredible energy of thundrillium, these devices represent the height of advanced technology.

#### BELT INSIGNIA RECORDER

Thundrillium Device The Belt Insignia Recorder is a special device that can be used as a belt buckle. It is a removable recording device controlled by a small remote and it is capable of flying and recording any events. These recordings can then easily be played back on the device. Placing a full Energy Cell in the device gives it 10 charges. Recording. As a Bonus Action, you can expend any number of the recorder’s charges to activate it; the recorder remains active for 1 minute per charge expended. You can use a Bonus Action to deactivate the recorder early, but doing so doesn’t recover any expended charges. While active, the recorder has a Fly Speed of 30 feet and can hover. It records all audio and visual it can see and hear. It can be controlled with a Utilize action to move up to 30 feet or change its facing direction. If the recorder had previously recorded something, the recording is overridden by a new one. The recorder is considered to be an object with AC 24, 10 Hit Points, Immunity to Poison and Psychic damage, and Resistance to all other damage. Playback. While deactivated, you can take a Utilize action to expend 1 of the recorder’s charges and play back what the device recorded on its screen.

#### FLAME THROWER

Thundrillium Device This device projects a wide, high-energy beam of superheated plasma capable of liquefying most materials on contact. Placing a full Energy Cell in the device gives it 6 charges. As a Utilize action, you can expend 1 of its charges to project fire in a 10-foot Cone. Each creature in that area makes a DC 13 Dexterity saving throw. On a failed save, it takes 4d6 Fire damage and starts burning. On a successful save, it takes half as much damage only. A nonmagical object that isn’t being worn or carried also takes the damage and starts burning if it is in the area. Nonmagical metal objects, such as metal weapons or suits of Heavy or Medium metal armor, within the area glow red-hot briefly. The object and any creature in physical contact with it takes 2d6 Fire damage.

#### HOLOGRAPHIC RECORDER

Thundrillium Device The Holographic Recorder is a spherical flying camera that can record images with perfect clarity. These recordings can then easily be played back on any screen, like the one in Cats’ Lair. Placing a full Energy Cell in the device gives it 10 charges. Recording. As a Bonus Action, you can expend any number of the recorder’s charges to activate it; the recorder remains active for 1 minute per charge expended. You can use a Bonus Action to deactivate the recorder early while within 5 feet of it, but doing so doesn’t recover any expended charges. When activated, the recorder floats at an initial distance of 2d10 feet from you. While activated, it records all audio and visual it can see and hear. If you move more than 20 feet away from it, the recorder follows you so that it remains within 20 feet of you. If the recorder had previously recorded something, the recording is overridden by a new one. The recorder is considered to be an object with AC 22, 25 Hit Points, Immunity to Poison and Psychic damage, and Resistance to all other damage Playback. While deactivated, you can use a Utilize action to expend 1 of the recorder’s charges and play back what the device recorded on a screen within 20 feet of it.

#### LIE TRANSLATOR

Thundrillium Device This pocket-sized device is capable of recording what a person says and then play what that person is actually thinking in his or her voice. The device can thus “translate” a person’s lies into what their real thoughts are, and provides some protection against being ensorcelled. Placing a full Energy Cell in the device gives it 3 charges. As a Reaction you take when a creature within 30 feet of you starts to speak while you are holding the Lie Translator, you can expend 1 of its charges to activate it. If what the creature says is a lie, the translator emits what the creature’s true intentions are in its own voice. In addition, as a Reaction you take when a creature within 30 feet of you causes you to make a saving throw to avoid having the Charmed condition while you are holding the Lie Translator, you can expend 1 of its charges to activate it and automatically succeed on your saving throw.

#### LOCATOR DISC

Thundrillium Device This tiny homing device can be adhered to any surface or creature. It comes with a receiver, which remains separate from the disc either in handheld form or mounted to a vehicle or other device. Placing a full Energy Cell in the device gives it 10 charges. As a Utilize action, you can adhere the Locator Disc to a surface or creature. If the creature is unaware, you must succeed on a Dexterity (Sleight of Hand) check against its Passive Perception to do so unnoticed. If the creature is aware, it must succeed on a Dexterity saving throw against your Grapple DC to avoid it. Removing the disc requires a creature to succeed on a DC 13 Wisdom (Perception) check to find it, and then a DC 13 Strength check to remove it. As a Utilize action while within 5 feet of the receiver, you can expend 1 of its charges to have the disc send a signal to the receiver, giving its precise location on a map. The disc and the receiver must be within 25 miles of each other. The signal fails if any thickness of lead blocks a direct path between the disc and the receiver, or if the disc is in an area of extreme heat.

#### PLASMA CANNON

Thundrillium Device, Weapon (Any Ranged Simple or Martial) This weapon is capable of firing blasts of plasma. It is powered by an Energy Cell and placing a full Energy Cell in it gives the weapon 6 charges. It ignores the Loading property if it has it, or any weapon property that would limit the number of attacks you can make with it. Plasma Shot. The weapon can only make attack rolls by expending 1 charge from it, which is used in place of ammunition. On a hit, roll the weapon’s damage dice twice. One roll deals Lightning damage and the other roll deals Fire damage. Then add any relevant modifiers to either roll; not both. If the target is mainly composed of metal or wearing armor made of metal, it suffers the effect of the Heat Metal spell until the end of its next turn.

#### PLASMA BLADE

#### THUNDRAINIUM CANNON

Thundrillium Device, Weapon (Greatsword, Longsword, Scimitar, or Shortsword) Thundrillium Device This weapon generates a blade of plasma instead of having a traditional one. It is powered by an Energy Cell and placing a full Energy Cell in it gives the weapon 10 charges. When it has 0 charges, the blade of plasma dissipates and it becomes a sword handle. Plasma Strike. The weapon can only make attack rolls by expending 1 charge from it. On a hit, roll the weapon’s damage dice twice. One roll deals Lightning damage and the other roll deals Fire damage. Then add any relevant modifiers to either roll; not both. On a hit, you can choose to expend 1 charge to deal an extra 1d6 Lightning damage and 1d6 Fire damage (no action required). This siege weapon features four cannons mounted on a mobile platform, designed to be piloted by a creature of Medium size or smaller. Each cannon fires shells packed with volatile thundrainium, which detonate on impact in a burst of destructive energy and choking thundrainium fumes. Placing a full Energy Cell in the device gives it 4 charges. The Thundrainium Cannon is a Large object with the following statistics: AC 19; HP 75; Speed 30 ft.; Immunity to Poison and Psychic damage. As a Utilize action, you can expend any number of the cannon’s charges; the cannon fires 1 shot per charge expended at different points you can see within 120 feet of it. Each creature in a 5-foot-radius Sphere centered on each of those points makes a DC 15 Dexterity saving throw. A creature takes 2d10 Fire damage and 2d10 Bludgeoning damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one. A creature in the area of more than one Sphere is affected only once. Afterwards, a cloud of thundrainium (see above) fumes lingers in the area for 1 minute or until a strong wind (such as the one created by Gust of Wind) disperses it.

#### THUNDRAINIUM PROJECTOR

Thundrillium Device This siege weapon is capable of shooting a ray of pure thundrainium, harming even non-Thunderians with its radiation. Its precision-aiming rig can shift between focused beam and wide-saturation mode. It also has wheels, allowing a Medium or smaller creature to pilot it as a vehicle. Placing a full Energy Cell in the device gives it 4 charges. The Thundrainium Projector is a Large object with the following statistics: AC 19; HP 75; Speed 30 ft.; Immunity to Poison and Psychic damage. As a Utilize action, you can expend 1 of the projector’s charges to make a ranged attack roll against one creature you can see within 120 feet of it. The device fires a pinpoint ray of pure thundrainium and makes its attack roll with a +7 bonus. On a hit, the target takes 8d10 Radiant damage, gains 1 Exhaustion level, and is affected by thundrainium (see above) until the end of its next turn. Alternatively, as a Utilize action, you can expend 2 of the projector’s charges to saturate a 20-foot-radius Sphere centered on a point within 120 feet of it. Each creature within the area is affected by thundrainium (see above) until the end of its next turn, and must succeed on a DC 13 Constitution saving throw or take 4d10 Radiant damage and gain 1 Exhaustion level.

#### THUNDRILLIUM BLASTER

Thundrillium Device, Weapon (Any Ranged Simple or Martial) This weapon is capable of firing bolts of raw thundrillium energy. It is powered by an Energy Cell and placing a full Energy Cell in it gives the weapon 8 charges. It ignores the Loading property if it has it, or any weapon property that would limit the number of attacks you can make with it. Thundrillium Bolt. The weapon can only make attack rolls by expending 1 charge from it, which is used in place of ammunition. On a hit, roll the weapon’s damage dice twice. One roll deals Thunder damage and the other roll deals Radiant damage. Then add any relevant modifiers to either roll; not both.

#### THUNDROMETER

Thundrillium Device This compact device was designed to detect and distinguish between thundrillium and thundrainium deposits. When it nears a source of thundrillium, it emits a rapid series of clicks. In the presence of thundrainium, however, it produces urgent, warning beeps. The farther the detector is from either source, the slower and more intermittent the sounds become. Placing a full Energy Cell in the device gives it 10 charges. As a Utilize action while holding the Thundrometer, you can expend any number of its charges to activate it; the Thundrometer remains active for 10 minutes per charge expended. You can use a Bonus Action to deactivate the Thundrometer early while holding it, but doing so doesn’t recover any expended charges. When activated, you can sense the direction to the nearest thundrillium and thundrainium within 1 mile of the Thundrometer, and you know how far away they are. The Thundrometer can’t locate thundrillium or thundrainium if any thickness of lead blocks a direct path between the Thundrometer and the substance.

Recording. As a Bonus Action while holding it, you can expend 1 of the device’s charges to record a voice you can hear, adding it to the device’s database. The device can hold 6 voices in its database. If you record a voice and the database already has 6 voices, you must remove one voice from the database. Mimicking. As a Bonus Action while holding the device, you can expend 1 of its charges to activate it, choosing a voice from its database to mimic. The device remains activated until the end of your turn. While activated, anything you speak into the device sounds as if spoken in the chosen voice. A creature that hears you speak with this device can tell it is mimicked only with a successful DC 20 Wisdom (Insight) check.

#### WARP GAS GRENADE

Thundrillium Device

#### TUSKA GUN

Thundrillium Device, Weapon (Musket) This weapon is capable of firing explosive blasts. It is powered by an Energy Cell and placing a full Energy Cell in it gives the weapon 5 charges. It ignores the Loading property if it has it, or any weapon property that would limit the number of attacks you can make with it. Explosive Charge. The weapon can only make attack rolls by expending 1 charge from it, which is used in place of ammunition. On a hit, roll the weapon’s damage dice twice. One roll deals Thunder damage and the other roll deals Fire damage. Then add any relevant modifiers to either roll; not both. Each creature within 5 feet of the hit target takes 1d6 Fire damage.

#### VOICE IMITATOR

Thundrillium Device This special megaphone-like device transforms the voice of anyone who speaks into it, perfectly mimicking another person’s voice. Placing a full Energy Cell in the device gives it 10 charges. This grenade is powered by an Energy Cell and placing a full Energy Cell in it gives the grenade 1 charge. You can take the Utilize action to expend 1 charge from the grenade and throw it to a point up to 60 feet away, where it explodes. Each creature in a 10-foot-radius Sphere centered on that point makes a DC 15 Constitution saving throw, taking 2d6 Psychic damage and 2d6 Poison damage on a failed save or half much damage on a successful one. Warp Gas Cloud. After the grenade explodes, the effect of the Stinking Cloud spell (save DC 15) emerges from the point it explodes for 1d4 rounds. The effect is not considered magical. A creature that has the Poisoned condition from the Stinking Cloud effect has Disadvantage on Constitution saving throws made to maintain its Concentration while Poisoned in this way. In addition, a creature Poisoned in this way has a 50 percent chance of having the Frightened condition (source of fear one random creature within its line of sight) until it is no longer Poisoned. Otherwise, the creature treats all its allies as enemies until it is no longer Poisoned.

### Adventuring Gear

The different landscapes of Third Earth and New Thundera along with technological knowhow have all given way to the creation of unique gear to help traverse and survive many different ecosystems.

#### ANTI-GRAVITY BOOTS

While you wear these boots, your Speed isn’t reduced by you carrying weight in excess of your carrying capacity. In addition, you ignore the first 10 feet of falling. The bulkiness of the boots gives Disadvantage on Stealth checks while worn. You also have Disadvantage on saving throws to prevent being pushed or shoved due to the boots’ lowered gravity effects. Once on each of your turns while wearing these boots, you can jump an additional 5 feet.

#### CLIFF CLIMBERS

While using these climbers, which require both hands, you gain a +4 bonus to Strength (Athletics) checks made to climb.

#### GRAPPLE GUN

#### SHARK SUIT

As a Utilize action, you can launch the Grappling Hook from the gun at a railing, a ledge, or another catch within 30 feet of yourself, and the hook catches on if you succeed on a DC 13 Dexterity (Acrobatics) check. You can then climb the rope attached to the Grappling Hook, or grab hold of the rope and activate the gun as a Bonus Action to have it pull you up at a rate of 15 feet per round.

#### POWER PACK

This shark-shaped suit allows for underwater travel. While wearing the suit, you gain a Swim Speed equal to your Speed and have 1 hour’s worth of air in the suit before you begin suffocating. In addition, a Beast with a Swim Speed ignores you while in the suit so long as no hostile action is taken against it. Due to the cramped nature of the suit, you can’t cast spells requiring Somatic components or make attack rolls while wearing it. This special Energy Cell increases the number of charges it gives a Thundrillium Device by 4.

#### THERMAL CATSUITS

This outfit consists of a lined suit with a hood, boots and gloves. While wearing the catsuit, you automatically succeed on saving throws against the effects of extreme cold. Due to the water-tight properties of the suits, you have Advantage on saving throws against the effects of toxic liquid or any contact poison.

#### UNDERWATER BREATHING

#### DEVICE

While wearing this device, you can breathe normally underwater. However, you are unable to speak or cast spells using Verbal components.

### Magic Items

This section outlines magic items. Some are legendary treasures of Thundera’s legacy, while others are ancient relics of Second Earth. The Magic Items table lists the new magic items, their rarity, and whether or not they require Attunement.

#### BELT OF TRICK PELLETS

Wondrous Item, Uncommon This belt has pouches that contain 3d4 trick pellets made by WilyKat and WilyKit when found. It magically restores 1d4 trick pellets daily at dawn if it has 2 or fewer pellets. As a Utilize action, or in place of one attack when taking the Attack action, you can throw a trick pellet up to a point up to 30 feet away where it immediately causes an effect determined by rolling on the following table. If you succeed on a DC 13 Intelligence check as part of the action used to throw the trick pellet, you can instead choose any effect on the table. d6 Effect 1 Butter Pellet. The effect of the Grease spell originates from that space. 2 Dinosaur Pellet. The pellet inflates where it lands into a realistic portrayal of a Tyrannosaurus Rex. Its size is Large instead of Huge. Each creature within 10 feet of it when it inflates makes a DC 13 Wisdom saving throw. On a failed save, the target has the Frightened condition until the end of its next turn. The tyrannosaurus rex harmlessly deflates if it takes 1 damage or at the start of your next turn. 3 Sharp-Pointed Jacks. The pellet erupts into spikey jacks where it lands, mimicking the effect of Caltrops. 4 Raft Pellet. The pellet inflates into a usable water raft. The raft uses the stats of a Rowboat, but has 10 Hit Points. The raft harmlessly deflates after 1 hour. 5 Smokescreen Pellet. The effect of the Fog Cloud spell originates from that space. At the start of your next turn, the fog disperses. 6 Bubble Pellet. Each creature within 10 feet of where the pellet lands makes a DC 13 Constitution saving throw. On a failed save, the target subtracts 1d4 from attack rolls and damage rolls until the end of its next turn.

#### BENGALI’S HAMMER OF THUNDERA

Weapon (Warhammer), Very Rare (Requires Attunement) You have a +1 bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls made with this magic weapon. Unlike a regular Warhammer, its base damage is 1d12 Bludgeoning. It deals an extra 1d12 Thunder damage on a hit and lacks the Versatile property. If you are proficient with Smith’s Tools, you are proficient with Bengali’s Hammer of Thundera. Master Forging Hammer. This weapon counts as Smith’s Tools. If you have proficiency with Smith’s Tools, you instead have Expertise with Smith’s Tools if you use this weapon when you would make a Smith’s Tools check. Hone Magic Weaponry. Over the course of 1 hour, you can use this weapon to attempt to temporarily improve the effectiveness of a different magic weapon. At the end of the hour, you make a DC 22 Intelligence or Wisdom check using this weapon as Smith’s Tools. On a successful check, the magic weapon you improved has a +1 bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls for 24 hours. If you succeed on the check by 5 or more, the bonus increases to +2. This stacks with other bonuses to attack rolls and damage rolls the magic weapon may have. Shattering Retort. When you take Force, Radiant, or Thunder damage, you can take a Reaction to instead take no damage and cast Shatter (level 4 version; save DC 16) as part of the same Reaction. If you include yourself in the radius of the spell, you take no damage. Once you use this property, it can’t be used again until the next dawn. Energy Bolt. This weapon has 4 charges and only regains charges from an Energy Cell as if it was a Thundrillium Device. When you take the Attack action, you can expend 1 charge to make attack rolls via shooting energy bolts, which have a range of 60 feet. You can replace any number of attacks with these bolts. Apply your Proficiency Bonus and Strength or Wisdom modifier to the attack roll. On a hit, the target takes 4d6 Force damage.

#### BOOK OF OMENS

Wondrous Item, Artifact This golden book has the insignia of the ThunderCats on its cover and is one of the Treasures of Thundera. Its silver pages contain ciphered information on Thundera, its history, and its people. Partnered with this book is an oversized golden key known as the Key of Thundera that may be found with it (at GM discretion). The Key of Thundera counts as a Legendary rarity magic item, but otherwise has no properties or powers of its own. A creature examining the book can make a DC 25 Wisdom (Perception) check to perceive the secret keyhole on its cover. Inserting the Key of Thundera into the keyhole, or using Truesight given by the Sword of Omens specifically to perceive it, immediately activates the book’s Enter the Mysterious Realm property (see below).

Secrets of Thundera. While consulting the book, if you succeed on a DC 20 Intelligence (Investigation) check, you decipher a part of its information. If you take the Study action to recall or learn information regarding Thunderian history, items of any kind created by Thunderians, or specific Thunderian creatures within the next minute, you make the check with Advantage and add 1d10 to the check’s total. You then can’t decipher another part of the book until you finish a Long Rest. Enter the Mysterious Realm. After using the Key of Thundera or Sword of Omens as referenced above, you are immediately transported into a demiplane inside the book. The demiplane is a blue-hued chamber that houses the Guardian of the Book of Omens, an invincible and untargetable spirit of knowledge that takes the shape of a Large cat’s eye. The Guardian asks you to present the Key of Thundera. If you don’t show it the key within 6 seconds of arriving, it animates two pillars into draconic forms, immediately breathing a torrent of flame upon you. You make a DC 22 Dexterity saving throw, taking 20d6 Fire damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one. At the start of each of your turns, the draconic forms continue to breathe flame, causing you to repeat this saving throw. The draconic forms stop only when you die, or produce the Key of Thundera to the Guardian. If you have the Key of Thundera, you can choose to manifest the effect of the Contact Other Plane spell while inside the demiplane, or harmlessly leave it. The spell has the Guardian answer the questions, you automatically succeed on the spell’s saving throw, and the Guardian answers in sentences instead of the usual one word answers of the spell. The Guardian has almost all information regarding Thundera and Thunderians, but may have other extraordinary extraplanar knowledge as well. After asking the questions, you are ejected from the demiplane. You are also ejected if you hold the Key of Thundera, but choose not to manifest the effect of Contact Other Plane for 1 minute. Once you benefit from Contact Other Plane in this way, you can’t do so again until the next dawn. Destroying the Book and Key. The Book of Thundera and Key of Thundera are indestructible, save for being hit by a magic weapon capable of dealing Piercing or Slashing damage of Artifact rarity. Both book and key have AC 23 and 10 Hit Points.

#### BOOK OF NORVOG NOSZH

Wondrous Item, Artifact This huge, ancient, and powerful tome of necromantic rites, spells, and incantations is tied to the primordial energies of Third Earth. Opening the Book of Norvog Noszh doesn’t require Attunement, but after finishing each Long Rest, when the book is first opened to new pages, trapped spiritual forces are released that inflict 2d6 Necrotic damage on the closest creature to the book. Once opened, the book’s features can be accessed. Ancient Lore. If you use the opened Book of Norvog Noszh for research or reference, you gain Advantage on Intelligence (Arcana and History) checks made while it is opened to a page. Necromantic Spellbook. The Book of Norvog Noszh can be used as a Wizard’s Spellbook and an Arcane Focus, containing the following spells: Animate Dead, Astral Projection, Blight, Circle of Death, Create Undead, False Life, Feign Death, Ray of Sickness, Speak with Dead, and Vampiric Touch. While holding the book, you can take a Magic action to cast one of the above spells, without expending a spell slot or using any Material components. Once used, this property of the book can’t be used again until the next dawn. Summoning the Gorgon. While reading the book, you can choose to attune to the Book of Norvog Noszh. If you do so, you learn of a special ritual that requires 1 hour, the Mask of Gorgon (see the Child of Gorgon stat block in chapter 4), and a successful DC 18 Intelligence (Arcana) check to summon the Child of Gorgon. The Child of Gorgon is initially under your control, responding favorably to your verbal commands. For every command given, there is a cumulative 5 percent chance of the Child of Gorgon breaking free of the ritual’s hold and turning on you. The Child of Gorgon disappears after 8 hours or when you have the Incapacitated condition, whichever happens first. Destroying the Book. The Book of Norvog Noszh is indestructible, save for being hit by a magic weapon capable of dealing Radiant damage of Artifact rarity by a Good aligned creature. The book has AC 20 and 30 Hit Points. If it is destroyed, each Good or Neutral aligned creature within 60 feet of it takes 10d6 Necrotic damage.

#### BRACELET OF POWER

Wondrous Item, Very Rare (Requires Attunement) This golden bracelet is set with two green gems and clasps together to become a solid ring worn around the wrist when attuned. It is one of the Treasures of Thundera. As a Magic action, you can cast a modified version of the Dominate Person spell (save DC 16). The spell doesn’t require spell components or Concentration, has a duration of 8 hours, and can target a number of creatures equal to your Charisma modifier (minimum 1) within 30 feet of you. Thunderians have

Disadvantage on the saving throw made to avoid the effects of the spell. Once you cast Dominate Person in this way, you can’t do so again until you finish a Short or Long Rest.

#### CHEETARA’S EXTENDING STAFF

Weapon (Quarterstaff), Rare (Requires Attunement) This magic weapon doesn’t require an object interaction to draw or stow. Once on your turn, you can collapse into a compact size of a 6-inch rod, or extend to its full length (no action required). Any further alterations to the weapon’s length during your turn must use the Utilize action. The weapon can only be used to make attack rolls while fully extended. While fully extended, the weapon has the Finesse property and you add your Dexterity score to the distance of your High Jumps. Toppling Swings. You can use the Topple mastery property with this weapon, even if you don’t have a feature that lets you use it. A creature hit by this weapon has Disadvantage on its Constitution saving throw against the Topple mastery property. Energy Bolt. This weapon has 6 charges and only regains charges from an Energy Cell as if it was a Thundrillium Device. When you take the Attack action, you can expend 1 charge to make attack rolls via shooting energy bolts, which have a range of 60 feet. You can replace any number of attacks with these bolts. Apply your Proficiency Bonus and Dexterity or Wisdom modifier to the attack roll. On a hit, the target takes 4d6 Force damage. a ledge, pull yourself up onto it (no action required). While attached to a surface, you can harmlessly detach yourself as a Bonus Action. Claw Deflection. When you are hit by an attack roll, you can take a Reaction to add 3 to your AC against that attack, possibly causing it to miss. You can use this property a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and regain all expended uses daily at dawn. Reflective. As a Utilize action, you can use the surface of the Shield as a reflective surface in the same way as you would a Mirror.

#### DOUBLE HEADED DRAGON OF DOOM

Wondrous Item, Very Rare This two-headed jade dragon from the ancient times of Thundera, despite not being from that planet, is a powerful, extradimensional prison. When used, the figurine grows to immense size, rearing back to snap its draconic heads down on your enemies. While it is not technically one of the Treasures of Thundera, it was kept with them.

#### CLAW SHIELD

Armor (Shield), Rare This Shield is worn on your arm like a gauntlet. If you make an Unarmed Strike with the arm wearing the Shield and deal damage, it deals Slashing damage equal to 1d8 plus your Strength modifier, instead of the normal damage of an Unarmed Strike. The Shield has the following additional properties that you can use while wearing it. Claw Lines. As a Bonus Action, you can fire cables from the Shield to a surface within 30 feet of you, pulling you to that surface. You can stay attached to that surface, or if it is

Call the Jade Dragon. As a Magic action, you can target two Large or smaller creatures within 60 feet of you that are within 30 feet of each other, to be potentially “swallowed” by the figurine. Targets of the figurine must succeed on a DC 18 Charisma saving throw or be drawn into a hostile pocket dimension to be tormented. The figurine shrinks back to normal size after use. While trapped in the pocket dimension, creatures take 1d4 Psychic damage at the end of each hour and can’t benefit from Long Rests. Once this property is used, the figurine must regenerate fully before being used again (see Figurine Regeneration below). Freeing the Dragon’s Prisoners. There are three ways to release creatures imprisoned within the figurine, which each appear in the nearest unoccupied spaces within 10 feet of the figurine. • You choose to end the effects (no action required). • The figurine is shattered. The figurine has AC 13, 1 Hit Point, and a Damage Threshold of 19. • The figurine is targeted by a Dispel Magic spell cast at level 6+. Figurine Regeneration. Unless the figurine is currently imprisoning any creatures, it fully restores itself for use when infused with 10 charges from other sources, which it immediately expends. If the figurine was shattered, all of the pieces must be stored in the same container when these charges are infused in order to reassemble successfully.

#### EGORA TALISMAN

Wondrous Item, Legendary (Requires Attunement) This twisted relic from the ancient past of the Onyx Pyramid itself instills magically enhanced self confidence and mental invincibility to its user. Legend claims its power is increased exponentially every thousand years, but remains a noteworthy boon when in its unaugmented state. The Egora Talisman has 3 charges and regains all expended charges daily at dusk, but it can have a maximum of 13 charges in certain situations (see Enhanced Power below). Egora’s Soothing Voice. You have Advantage on Insight and Persuasion checks while the talisman is on your person. Mental Fortitude. As a Magic action, you can expend 1 charge from the talisman to give you Immunity to the Charmed condition and become immune to effects that would force you to perform an action or change your behavior, such as the Command or Confusion spells, for 1 hour. Enhanced Power. When bathed in full moonlight that has been filtered through a dark prismatic stone (like that at the peak of Mumm-Ra’s Onyx Pyramid), the talisman gains 10 charges. In addition, you gain Immunity to Psychic damage and the Stunned and Pacified conditions while you are attuned to the talisman. The maximum number of charges the talisman can have becomes 13 until you end your Attunement to it.

#### EYE OF THUNDERA

Wondrous Item, Artifact This mystical red gemstone, shaped like a cat’s eye, embodies the power of the ThunderCats and is tied to their collective life-forces. It wields monumental magical potency, channeled through the Sword of Omens by, traditionally, the Lord of the ThunderCats. It is believed to have been a gift from the great Thunderian god Catamountain, unearthed in the early days of Thundera’s settlement by Lion-O’s ancestor, Leonus. This discovery marked the founding of the ThunderCats and the establishment of the Code of Thundera. The Eye of Thundera is usually found embedded in the hilt of the Sword of Omens. While it is effortless to embed the gemstone into the sword’s hilt, removing it requires a successful DC 30 Strength check. Sentience. The Eye of Thundera is a sentient Lawful Good gemstone with an Intelligence of 13, a Wisdom of 16, and a Charisma of 20. It utterly refuses to willingly allow a creature of any Evil alignment to benefit from any of its properties or powers. At the eye’s discretion, it can use its Wish of Thundera property of its own volition, but can’t do so again until the next dawn. It only does so to protect Thunderians on a grand scale, or to attempt to protect itself. Warning of Danger. While the eye is on your person, it additionally functions as a Weapon of Warning (see Fifth Edition gaming rules). In addition, you can designate a number of creatures equal to double your Proficiency Bonus as being warded by the eye (no action required). You can change any number of these creatures each dawn. If a warded creature gains a detrimental condition or takes at least 10 damage total, the eye awakens and growls, alerting you to that creature being in danger. It doesn’t give any further information about that creature, such as its location. Wish of Thundera. While holding the Sword of Omens with the eye embedded in it, you can cast Wish from it. You can only choose to use its basic use of duplicating any other spell

of level 8 or lower, or its Reshape Reality effect. The Reshape Reality effect must beneficially target a Thunderian in some way, or a catastrophe that endangers Thunderians on a grand scale. If the effect targets a Thunderian, any benefits granted to that Thunderian last a maximum of 1 hour. Casting Wish in this way doesn’t weaken you as the spell references. However, you gain 1 Exhaustion level after casting the spell. Once you use this property, it can’t be used again until the next dawn. Destroying the Eye. The Eye of Thundera is indestructible, save for being hit by a magic weapon capable of dealing Piercing or Slashing damage of Artifact rarity. The eye has AC 23 and 10 Hit Points. If the eye is destroyed, each Thunderian in all realms has its Strength and Dexterity scores reduced by 1d4 + 1 and has the Pacified condition. The ability score reduction, Pacified condition, and any Exhaustion levels the Thunderian has can’t be removed by any means while the eye remains destroyed. If the eye is restored, it regains all its properties and powers, and the detrimental effects to all Thunderians referenced above immediately end.

#### GOLDEN HELMET OF PHAR-NOOR

Wondrous Item, Legendary (Requires Attunement) The Golden Helmet of Phar-Noor is an enchanted headdress belonging to the Egyptian sorcerer Wizz-Ra. Crafted in the likeness of a Pharaoh’s ceremonial crown, the helmet radiates ancient power and mystic authority. When worn, it grants the bearer the ability to command and control the minds of others completely. As a Magic action, you can cast a modified version of the Dominate Person spell (save DC 20). The spell does not require spell components or Concentration and lasts while you are attuned to the helmet. The helmet weighs 5 pounds. The weight of the helmet increases with each creature under its control. For each creature affected by a Dominate Person spell cast from this helmet, the helmet’s weight doubles. You can end a Dominate Person spell cast from this helmet at any time (no action required).

#### GOLDEN SPHERE OF SETI

Wondrous Item, Legendary (Requires Attunement) The Golden Sphere of Seti is a solid gold orb, about the size of a large sports ball. Possession of the sphere amplifies the wielder’s power fourfold. While attuned to the sphere, your Proficiency Bonus is quadrupled. As a Magic action while attuned to the sphere, you can increase your size category by two—from Medium to Huge, for example—until your Attunement ends. Curse. This item is cursed, and becoming attuned to it extends the curse to you. As long as you are cursed, you are unwilling to part with the sphere, keeping it within reach at all times. You also are compelled to gain possession of anything more valuable than 10 GP within sight, so long as the acquisition will not end in certain death. You will do whatever you can to attain such objects, no matter how heinous, despicable, or against your personality it is. In addition, after finishing 1d4 Long Rests, you must succeed on a DC 15 Charisma saving throw or treat all your allies as enemies until your Attunement ends. On a successful save, this saving throw is repeated each time you finish a Long Rest. Your Attunement to the sphere can’t be ended in any way unless the effect that would do so targets you while you have 0 Hit Points and have at least one Death Saving Throw failure. Obsession. Any creature with an Intelligence of 10 or higher that is informed, assumes, or otherwise knows the value or history of the Golden Sphere of Seti must immediately succeed on a DC 15 Wisdom saving throw or become obsessed with trying to acquire it. On a successful save, this saving throw is only repeated if the creature sees the Golden Sphere of Seti in another creature’s possession. If the creature succeeds on this repeated saving throw, it is immune to the Obsession property of the sphere for 365 days.

#### KEYSTONE

Wondrous Item, Rare (Requires Attunement) The Keystone is an ancient and immensely powerful crystal, glowing with a deep emerald light. Its surface is etched with mysterious runes and symbols whose meanings have long been forgotten. The crystal radiates pure, limitless energy capable of restoring life to the dying and bringing motion to lifeless machines. Yet its power comes at a terrible cost. Those who draw upon the Keystone soon find themselves bound to it, consumed by their dependence until it destroys them entirely. As soon as you are attuned to the Keystone, and upon finishing each Long Rest, you gain the effects of the Greater Restoration spell. While physically holding the Keystone in one or more hands, you gain access to its benefits. Firm Grasp. In any instance where you would lose your grip on the Keystone (Ex. getting disarmed of it, having the Incapacitated condition, or falling asleep), you can make a DC 15 Constitution saving throw to maintain your hold until it is physically removed from your grip. Magical Vitality. You automatically succeed on all Death Saving Throws that are not inflicted by taking damage while at 0 Hit Points. Vital Infusions. As a Magic action, you can expend any number of your Hit Dice to replenish an equal number of charges in any object—magical or nonmagical—within 10 feet of you that is currently missing charges.

Admiration and Reliance. During your first turn in any combat, you must take your action and Bonus Action to simply stare and admire the Keystone. Withdrawal. You must succeed on a DC 24 Wisdom saving throw to willingly break your Attunement with the Keystone, which you can only attempt once every 24 hours. If you are otherwise forced to drop or lose the Keystone, including if it is stolen from you, you immediately gain 1d4 Exhaustion levels.

#### LYNX-O’S LIGHT SHIELD

Armor (Shield), Rare This reflective Shield is worn on your wrist. When exposed to a source of Bright Light that isn’t natural daylight, you can angle the Shield to reflect a 20-foot Cone of the same color and quality of light (no action required). The Shield has the following additional properties that you can use while wearing it. Luminous Bolt. This weapon has 4 charges and only regains charges from an Energy Cell as if it was a Thundrillium Device. When you take the Attack action, you can expend 1 charge to make attack rolls via shooting bolts of light, which have a range of 60 feet. You can replace any number of attacks with these bolts. Apply your Proficiency Bonus and Dexterity or Wisdom modifier to the attack roll. On a hit, the target takes 4d6 Radiant damage. Light Shield Reflection. When you take Force or Radiant damage originating from a creature, you can take a Reaction to instead take no damage, and the triggering creature takes the Force or Radiant damage you would have taken.

#### MAGIC BELT OF THE ANCIENTS

Wondrous Item, Legendary (Requires Attunement) The Magic Belt of the Ancients is an artifact once worn by Queen Luna, grandmother of Luna, the leader of the Lunataks. Forged in ancient days, this golden belt channels immense mystical energy, granting its bearer extraordinary magical power. The belt must be worn openly in order to be attuned and used. It taps into the powerful energies permeating Third Earth, and was once used by Queen Luna to subjugate the peoples forced to build the Lunataks’ Sky-Tomb. Impressive Stature. While wearing the belt, you are subject to the Enlarge effects of the Enlarge/Reduce spell (no Concentration required). Control the Masses. You can produce a tremendously powerful imposition of your will on all creatures with an Intelligence score of 6 or higher within 60 feet of you. Each creature must succeed on a DC 16 Wisdom saving throw or have the Charmed condition. The Charmed condition lasts until the belt is no longer being worn, you choose to end the effects, or have the Incapacitated condition. A Charmed creature can repeat the saving throw after finishing each Long Rest, ending the effect on itself on a success. You can use this property a number of times equal to your Charisma modifier (minimum 1), and regain all expended uses after finishing a Short or Long Rest. Removing the Belt. You can remove the belt at any time, but others can try to do so by force. This can be done physically by a creature you are Grappled by succeeding on a DC 18 Strength (Athletics) or Dexterity (Sleight of Hand) check as a Utilize action, allowing it to take the belt from you. This can also be achieved magically through a directed Dispel Magic spell cast at level 5+, breaking the Attunement and causing the belt to unclasp and fall to the ground.

#### MAGICAL FLUTE

Wondrous Item, Uncommon The Magical Flute is one of the Treasures of Thundera, and bears the unmistakable ThunderCats insignia. At first glance, it appears to be an ordinary flute, but it splits gracefully in the middle into two elegant tubes crafted from gleaming silver metal. The flute has 3 charges and regains its expended charges daily at dawn. As a Magic action, you can cast Animal Friendship by playing the flute, targeting one Beast within 30 feet of you for each charge expended. The spell save DC against the effects of the flute is equal to a Charisma (Performance) check made to play the instrument as part of the same action.

#### MINIATURIZATION GAS

Wondrous Item, Uncommon Found in a small spritzing vial, this liquid can be sprayed out the nozzle as a gas. There is enough of it for one use. The concoction was invented by an ancient pharaoh to whom history only remembers as “Tutan-Tiny.” As a Utilize action, the gas can be sprayed in a 5-foot-radius Sphere within 10 feet of you. The gas lingers in the air for 1 minute or until a strong wind (such as the one created by Gust of Wind) disperses it. Each creature in the Sphere makes a DC 13 Constitution saving throw. If the creature fails the saving throw, it shrinks in size as though under the Reduce effect of the Enlarge/Reduce spell for 1 hour (no Concentration required). The effect can be ended with a Greater Restoration spell or similar magic, or if the creature is magically enlarged to its normal size (using the Enlarge effect of an Enlarge/Reduce spell, for example). A creature must also make this save when the Sphere moves into its space and when it enters the Sphere or ends its turn there. A creature makes this save only once per turn.

#### MIRROR OF TRUTH

Wondrous Item, Legendary This golden-framed mirror has the insignia of the ThunderCats on the back and is one of the Treasures of Thundera. Reveal All Truths. Reflections in the mirror are shown as if seen with Truesight. Energy Reflection. When you take Force, Lightning, Radiant, or Thunder damage originating from a creature, you can take a Reaction to instead take no damage, and the triggering creature takes the Force, Lightning, Radiant or Thunder damage you would have taken. makes a DC 16 Dexterity saving throw. On a failed save, it takes 3d8 Force damage and has the Blinded condition until the end of your next turn. On a successful save, it takes half as much damage only.

#### PUMYRA’S CAT’S TAIL

Weapon (Whip), Rare (Requires Attunement) Unlike a regular Whip, this magic weapon’s base damage is 2d6 Slashing. If you are proficient with Slings, you are proficient with Pumyra’s Cat’s Tail. Elastic Cord. This weapon can function in all ways as 30 feet of Rope. However, it can’t be burst by a successful Strength (Athletics) check. Explosive Pellet. This weapon has 3d4 explosive pellets when found. It magically regains 1d4 pellets daily at dawn if it has 2 or fewer pellets. When you take the Attack action, you can fling an explosive pellet using this weapon as an attack roll, which has a range of 90 feet. You can replace any number of attacks with these explosive pellets. Apply your Proficiency Bonus and Dexterity modifier to the attack roll and damage roll. On a hit, the target takes 3d6 Fire damage.

#### PANTHRO’S NUNCHAKU

Weapon (Mace), Rare (Requires Attunement) This magic weapon takes the appearance of two claw-ended battle sticks connected by a chain, and has the Reach property. When you hit a creature wielding a weapon with an attack roll using this weapon, you can choose to have it drop one weapon it is wielding instead of dealing damage. Nunchaku Blast. This weapon has 2 charges and only regains charges from an Energy Cell as if it was a Thundrillium Device. When you take the Attack action, you can expend 1 charge to use one of the following properties in place of one of your attacks. Expel Flame. You expel fiery gas from the red battle stick. Each creature in a 30-foot Cone makes a DC 16 Dexterity saving throw, taking 6d6 Fire damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one. Expel Energy Pellets. You expel pellets of energy from the blue battle stick. One creature you can see within 60 feet

#### RATAR-O’S RAT’S EYES

Weapon (Daggers), Very Rare (Requires Attunement) You have a +2 bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls made with each of this pair of magic weapons, which count as a single magic item for the purposes of Attunement. When you hit a target with an attack using either of these magic weapons, the target takes an extra 1d6 Force damage. While holding one of these magic weapons, you can cause its pair to telekinetically fly to your opposite hand if it is on the same plane as you, and immediately wield it if you have a hand free (no action required). If its pair is over 1,000 feet away, it arrives in 1 minute. Levitating Strikes. When you hit a Medium or smaller creature with an attack using either of these magic weapons, you can cause it to begin levitating up to 20 feet straight into the air until the end of its next turn. Creatures without a Fly Speed have Disadvantage on attack rolls while levitating in this way. You can use this property a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus and regain all expended uses daily at dawn. Swift Telekinesis. You can cast Telekinesis from either of these magic weapons as a Bonus Action (save DC 18). In addition, you can choose to take the Magic action of the spell as a Bonus Action. Once you cast Telekinesis, you can’t cast it from either weapon again until the next dawn. Energy Beam. Both weapons have 6 charges collectively and only regain charges from an Energy Cell as if they were Thundrillium Devices. When you take the Attack action, you can expend 1 charge to make attack rolls via shooting energy beams, which have a range of 90 feet. You can replace any number of attacks with these beams. Apply your Proficiency Bonus and Dexterity or Intelligence modifier to the attack roll. On a hit, the target takes 4d8 Force damage.

#### SANDS OF SLEEP

Potion, Uncommon This sparkling, alchemical powder resembles fine sand. There is enough of it for 1d6 + 4 uses. As a Utilize action, you can sprinkle a pinch of the powder over a 5-foot radius Sphere within 5 feet of you. Each creature in that area must succeed on a DC 15 Wisdom saving throw or have the Unconscious condition for 1 hour. The condition ends on a creature early if the creature takes damage or if another creature uses an action to shake it awake.

#### SPHERES OF POWER

Wondrous Item, Very Rare These tiny blue orbs are extremely powerful and grant all kinds of abilities. This is a collection of 1d6 + 2 spheres. You can take a Magic action to activate one of the Spheres of Power, choosing one of their effects. Each sphere is consumed when activated. Casting Spheres. You can cast one of the following spells from the sphere (spell save DC 16): Dimension Door, Enlarge/ Reduce (Enlarge only), Greater Invisibility, Magic Missile (level 4 version). Mirror Copy. You focus on one piece of equipment or magic item of up to Rare rarity you can see. The sphere vanishes in a dazzling display of energy in your hand and becomes a perfect copy of the weapon or item. You automatically treat the copied item as being attuned to you. The copy vanishes after 1 minute. Oubliette. You can pull an Incapacitated creature within 30 feet into the activated Sphere of Power, holding it in a featureless, prison-like, pocket dimension. Creatures contained within the pocket dimension stay incarcerated in this way until the remaining Spheres of Power are used, you choose to release them, or the Oubliette sphere is targeted by a Dispel Magic spell cast at level 6+. When any of the above occurs, the Oubliette sphere shatters.

#### SWORD OF OMENS

Weapon (Dagger/Greatsword), Legendary (Requires Attunement by a Creature of Any Good or Neutral Alignment) Forged by Tigris, the finest blacksmith in all of Thundera, for the ThunderCats’ then-lord, Cougartha, the Sword of Omens became the cornerstone of Thundera’s might. Passed down from Lord to Lord, it exists as the enduring symbol of the ThunderCats’ leadership. While the sword itself is a masterpiece of craftsmanship, its true power lies in the Eye of Thundera embedded within its hilt, making it the most formidable weapon in the galaxy.

You have a +2 bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls made with this magic weapon while it is retracted into a Dagger. While extended into a Greatsword, this bonus increases to +3 (see Invoke the Eye of Thundera below). In either form, the weapon deals an extra 2d6 Force damage on a hit and deals double damage to objects and structures. This weapon has the following properties, some of which only function when the Eye of Thundera is embedded in the hilt. Sentience. The Sword of Omens is a sentient Lawful Good weapon with an Intelligence of 13, a Wisdom of 14, and a Charisma of 16. The weapon can roar and give general warning to its wielder only when combined with the Eye of Thundera. It utterly refuses to willingly allow a creature of any Evil alignment to benefit from any of its properties or powers, and such a creature otherwise has Disadvantage on all attack rolls made with the weapon. Recall. You can take a Bonus Action and use a command word to cause the weapon to fly back to your hand, so long as it is on the same plane as you, and immediately wield it if you have a hand free (no action required). If the weapon is over 1,000 feet away, it arrives in 1 minute. Invoke the Eye of Thundera (Dagger Only; Requires Eye of Thundera). As a Magic action, you can use a command word to cause the weapon to extend into a Greatsword and cast The Call of Thundera (a spell in chapter 1) from it as part of the same action. The spell can only target Thunderians, and each one has the Empowered condition for 1 minute immediately after it arrives within 60 feet of you. If the weapon isn’t used to make an attack roll against a creature for 10 minutes, or when it is sheathed, it retracts back into the form of a Dagger. Once you use this property, you can’t do so again until the next dawn. Charges (Greatsword Only; Requires Eye of Thundera). The weapon has 10 charges and regains 1d8 + 2 expended charges daily at dawn. You can expend its charges to use the properties below. Energy Deflection. While holding the weapon, you can expend 1 charge to cast Energy Pact (a spell in Chapter 1). Grant Sight Beyond Sight. While holding the weapon, you can expend 1 charge to cast Sight Beyond Sight, or 6 charges to cast Greater Sight Beyond Sight (spells in chapter 1). Grant Truesight. While holding the weapon, you can take a Magic action to expend 2 charges and gain Truesight out to 30 feet, or 3 charges to gain Truesight out to 60 feet, for 1 minute. Omen Bolt. When you take the Attack action, you can expend 1 charge to make attack rolls via shooting bolts of mystical energy, which have a range of 60 feet. You can replace any number of attacks with these bolts. Apply your Proficiency Bonus and Strength or Charisma modifier to the attack roll. On a hit, the target takes 4d6 Force, Lightning, or Radiant damage (your choice).

#### SWORD OF PLUN-DARR

Weapon (Glaive), Legendary (Requires Attunement) You have a +3 bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls made with this magic weapon, which takes the appearance of a double-ended blade. Unlike a regular Glaive, its base damage is 2d12 Slashing. It deals an extra 1d10 Force damage on a hit, has the Finesse property, and deals double damage to objects and structures. If you are proficient with Greatswords or Longswords, you are proficient with the Sword of Plun-Darr. Dark Intent of Plun-Darr. You gain proficiency and Expertise in Intimidation, and have Advantage on attack rolls with this magic weapon against Good aligned creatures. Blade of Iniquity. When you hit a creature with an attack roll using this magic weapon, you can force it to make a DC 16 Constitution saving throw. On a failed save, the creature takes an extra 1d10 Force damage and has the Stunned condition until the end of your next turn. If your alignment is Evil, you can use this property at will. Otherwise, you can use this property a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and regain all expended uses daily at dawn.

#### THUNDERSCOPE

Wondrous Item, Rare (Requires Attunement) You can use this magical spyglass to seek out items of importance or power by looking through it, taking a Utilize action, and expending charges. The spyglass has 6 charges and regains 1d6 charges daily at dawn. Far Seeing (0 Charge). The Thunderscope functions like a normal Spyglass. Energy Detection (1 Charge). You cast Detect Magic as part of the Utilize action. Metallurgical Identification (1 Charge). You focus on a specific item you can see through the Thunderscope and learn of any material-based properties it possesses. Thunderian Signature (0 Charge). You focus on a specific item and know if it comes from Thundera or is Thunderian-made. Treasure Hunt (3 Charges). You know the compass direction in which you would need to travel to encounter the nearest magic item of Very Rare or Legendary rarity not currently attuned to any creature.

#### TOTEM OF DERA

Wondrous Item, Legendary This large blue jewel held in an ornate golden handle is one of the Treasures of Thundera. The jewel has 12 charges and regains 1d10 + 2 charges daily at dawn. While you hold it, you can cast one of the following spells from it: Animate Objects (expends 4 charges), Greater Restoration (expends 3 charges; additionally cures target of all magical contagions), or Lesser Restoration (expends 0 charges).

#### TYGRA’S BOLA WHIP

Weapon (Whip), Very Rare (Requires Attunement) You have a +1 bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls made with this magic weapon. It deals Bludgeoning damage instead of Slashing damage, and deals an extra 2d4 Bludgeoning damage on a hit. When you hit a solid surface or creature with this magic weapon while it isn’t ablaze, you can cause flames to engulf the bolas at the end of the weapon. These flames shed Bright Light in a 10-foot radius and Dim Light for an additional 10 feet. While the bolas are ablaze, the weapon deals an extra 3d6 Fire damage on a hit. The flames last until you hit a target or until you drop, stow, or sheathe the weapon. Invisibility Cloak. While holding this whip, you can cast Greater Invisibility (targeting yourself only), requiring only Somatic components. The spell’s duration changes to 1 hour (the spell still requires Concentration), and you gain a Swim Speed equal to your Speed while you have the Invisible condition from the spell. If you drop the whip, the spell ends. Once you use this property, it can’t be used again until the next dawn.

### Scientific Inventions

Not all incredible items depend on thundrillium or magic. Some stand as proof of what science can achieve on its own.

#### BOOSTER JUICE

Scientific Invention As a Utilize action, can be poured on living organics (plants, fungus, etc.) They grow by one size category—from Small to Medium, for example. May be used as a fuel source in vehicles in place of other fuel.

#### ENZYME CATALYZER

Scientific Invention The Enzyme Catalyzer is one of the most powerful weapons in the arsenal of the Intergalactic Control Force. It has 6 uses before it needs to be replenished. As a Utilize action, you can discharge the catalyzer into a 15-foot Cube of nonmagical liquid within 10 feet of you to remove poison, acid, and rot in a 5-foot-radius Sphere centered on the liquid. As a Utilize action, you can spray the catalyzer on an Ooze within 10 feet of you. The Ooze makes a DC 14 Constitution saving throw, taking 10d6 Necrotic damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one. As a Utilize action, you can shoot out 1 pint of enzymatic, soapy foam in a 30-foot-long, 5-foot-wide Line. Creatures in that area must succeed on a DC 14 Dexterity saving throw or have the Prone condition, and its Speed is 0 until it is moved from the space where it failed the saving throw. A target can repeat the saving throw at the beginning of each of its turns, ending the Speed reduction on a success. In addition, succeed or fail, a creature and everything it is wearing or holding in the Line is subjected to the Clean or Soil effect (cleaning only) of the Prestidigitation spell.

#### SUPERPOWER CONCOCTION

Scientific Invention The Superpower Concoction is a powerful serum devised by Vultureman in his Castle Plun-Darr laboratory. Fed up with the mu’tants’ endless failures, he created the serum to amplify his own abilities and finally achieve victory through intellect alone. As a Utilize action, you drink this serum and gain the following benefits for 1 hour: • You gain a Fly Speed equal to your Speed. • You can shoot laser beams from your eyes as an action, choosing one target you can see within 90 feet of you. The target makes a Dexterity saving throw (DC 8 plus your Dexterity and Proficiency Bonus), taking 4d10 Radiant damage on a failed save and half as much damage on a successful one. • You can hypnotize one Humanoid you can see within 30 feet of you that can also see you. The target must succeed on a Wisdom saving throw (DC 8 plus your Charisma modifier and Proficiency Bonus) or have the Charmed condition. The Charmed target regards you as a trusted friend to be heeded and protected. Although the target isn’t under your control, it takes your requests or actions in the most favorable way it can. Each time you or one of your companions does anything harmful to the target, it can repeat the saving throw, ending the effect on itself on a success. Otherwise, the effect lasts as long as you benefit from the Superpower Concoction. This benefit can only be used once per dose of Superpower Concoction. 10 minutes before the effects end, you feel a tingling sensation, at which point you can sustain the effects for an additional hour by drinking another Superpower Concoction.

### Vehicles

The use of highly engineered vehicles is commonplace in ThunderCats adventures. From overland to space travel, diving into the depths of Third Earth’s seas and subterranean abyss, or just blasting headlong into conflict, vehicles can fill a number of important roles. Whether a team of player characters all manning a single mechanical centerpiece to their strategies or a much smaller creation designed for one user, using a vehicle in ThunderCats adventures can be an important element to gameplay. Vehicles vary greatly in their design and function, but tend to be designed or modified to serve a specific purpose or concept. Many of these vehicles hail from previously engineered designs seen in ThunderCats media, but new and unique vehicle inventions are infinitely possible. A clever Mechanist or Operator could figure out a new way to use technology to come up with the next best thing since the Thundertank! • Your Strength is set to 19 (unless it was already higher) and you have Advantage on Strength checks.

### Base Attributes

Cargo. Vehicles can carry the listed weight without any penalty. Carrying more than the listed amount, but not double, causes the vehicle to move at half Speed. A vehicle carrying more than twice the listed weight has the Restrained condition. Speed. Vehicles have a listed base Speed. Unless noted specifically, this is a ground Speed. A vehicle can choose to have a different movement type (Burrow, Fly, etc.) as its primary Speed, making it immobile when it can’t access that movement type. Ram. All vehicles have the capacity to inflict damage on targets by ramming into them. The damage for this special Ram attack, as well as its reach, is initially based on the vehicle’s size category. The vehicle uses an amount of movement equal to the reach of this special Ram attack each time it makes one. If it can’t spend the requisite amount of movement, the Ram attack automatically misses. Note: This doesn’t count as a Melee Weapon System for the purposes of a Melee System Enhancer Vehicle Component (see Vehicle Components below). Charges. All vehicles require energy to operate as well as perform useful actions and special abilities; this energy can come from many different sources, but most commonly thundrillium in the form of Energy Cells. Each Energy Cell gives a number of charges depending on the vehicle’s size and any modifications made to it, as shown on the appropriate column in the Vehicle Base Attributes table below. Modifications made to vehicles can alter their normal maximum charges, as can other game effects (concentrated thundrillium, magical power from an artifact, enhanced fuel efficiency, etc.), but the values on the table are the vehicle standards for ThunderCats adventures. While a vehicle has charges remaining, its Energy Cell can’t be removed. Once the vehicle has 0 charges, the Energy Cell can be replaced with a new cell by taking a Utilize action. All vehicles in ThunderCats adventures share these common attributes. Size Category. Vehicles are classified by their Size. Exactly as with creatures, they will be noted as being Medium, Large, Huge, or Gargantuan. Immunities. Vehicles have Immunity to Poison and Psychic damage, as well as all conditions except Grappled, Paralyzed, Prone, Restrained, and Weakened. Armor Class. Vehicles have an Armor Class in the same fashion as creatures and characters. This shows how difficult the vehicle is to hit by Attack rolls. A vehicle’s Armor Class is calculated using the base value listed on the Vehicle Base Attributes table, modified by the vehicle’s Dexterity modifier. Damage Threshold. Vehicles have a Damage Threshold, which appears after their Hit Points; a vehicle has Immunity to all damage unless it takes an amount of damage that equals or exceeds its threshold, in which case it takes all damage as normal. Damage that fails to bypass the threshold is considered superficial and doesn’t damage the vehicle. Ability Scores. Vehicles have a Strength, Dexterity, and Constitution score based on their Size Category (see Vehicle Base Attributes table below), and an Intelligence, Wisdom, and Charisma score of 0. They automatically fail Intelligence, Wisdom, and Charisma saving throws. Crew. Vehicles have two numbers listed representing the crew it needs in order to operate. The number before the slash is how many Small or Medium-size creatures (Large-size creatures count as two Crew/Passengers) are required to function, while the number after the slash is the maximum number of Crew and/or Passengers it can carry normally. If a vehicle is being operated by fewer Crew than its minimum requirement (but more than none), or if it is carrying more Crew or Passengers than its maximum allowance, it has Disadvantage on all D20 Tests. VEHICLE BASE ATTRIBUTES Vehicle Size Category Medium Large Huge Gargantuan 11 + Dex modifier (max 6) 13 + Dex modifier (max 3) 15 26 Hit Points/Damage Threshold 50/5 100/10 300/15 500/20 Strength 12 16 20 24 Dexterity 18 14 12 8 Constitution 14 16 18 20 Crew 1/1 1/4 2/8 6/24 Cargo 1,000 pounds 5 tons 25 tons 125 tons Speed 60 ft. 50 ft. 40 ft. 30 ft. Ram 1d8, 5 ft. reach 2d8, 5 ft. reach 4d8, 10 ft. reach 4d10, 10 ft. reach Maximum Charges 5 10 20 40 Component Slots 3 6 12 24 Armor Class

Component Slots. Unlike a basic wagon or sailing ship, the advanced vehicles in ThunderCats adventures are designed with additional modularity in mind to allow their creators to add and remove utility as the need arises. Vehicles come standard with a number of Component Slots that can be fitted with a number of vehicle components that alter the vehicle’s attributes, allow it to possess new and unique abilities, and perform specific functions that pertain to the chosen components. The Vehicle Base Attribute table shows the number of Component Slots a standard vehicle possesses.

### Personal Vehicles

Many ThunderCats vehicles function similarly to standard, commonplace vehicles such as wagons, carriages, rowboats, and the like, as shown in Fifth Edition gaming rules. Some are designed to be more durable and combat capable as Personal Vehicles, forging a unique experience of use between it and the vehicle’s owner. Characters can turn any vehicle with Component Slots into a Personal Vehicle by spending at least one Long Rest attuning to it in the same fashion as attuning with a magic item (this doesn’t count against the number of items the character can have attuned). During that time, the character should be cleaning, performing minor maintenance, and generally becoming familiar with the vehicle. When the Long Rest ends, the vehicle becomes the character’s Personal Vehicle. A character can only attune to a number of Personal Vehicles equal to their Proficiency Bonus at any given time. There are a number of benefits a character gains when crewing one of their Personal Vehicles, detailed as follows: • The character ignores one source of Disadvantage when making ability checks on behalf of their Personal Vehicle. • The character can take Short or Long Rests in their Personal Vehicle (if possible) as if it were always a safe environment. • The character reduces any Critical Damage Effect rolls made by their Personal Vehicle by 1 (see Vehicle Damage Rolls below). • The character always adds their Proficiency Bonus when making Crew Checks and attack rolls with the vehicle. • The character can swap one Vehicle Component in their Personal Vehicle with another Vehicle Component they possess over the course of a Short Rest with a successful DC 12 Intelligence check. The character adds their Proficiency Bonus to the check if they have proficiency with Tinker’s Tools. The vehicle must still have enough unused Component Slots to swap the Vehicle Components. There are two primary uses characters will be able to count on their vehicles to perform—travel and combat. The use of vehicles highlights and enhances these important aspects of any roleplaying game, and plays a significant role in ThunderCats tales.

#### VEHICLE TRAVEL

### Vehicles in Combat

All ThunderCats vehicles require fuel of some kind, translated as charges. When involved in rapid or aggressive maneuvers, like those used in combat, these charges can be used up rapidly, but basic transportation is much more charge-efficient. Just moving around in a vehicle as a source of common transit outside of combat, in the same way a character might with a wagon or carriage, doesn’t use up any charges, but does require the vehicle to possess at least 1 charge. A vehicle with 0 charges outside of a combat situation has the Paralyzed condition. A vehicle being used for longer-distance transportation will use a number of its charges making the journey based on the category of distance travelled and the size of the vehicle doing the travelling. These numbers can be modified by the GM, but the suggested values can be found on the table below. While the charges used for transportation are narratively spent over the course of the journey, the actual charges being used for this kind of travel are spent when the journey reaches its destination. The GM can choose to impose small charge expenditures along the way when a vehicle makes numerous stops or detours, but collectively the expenditures should stay within the above values. If a vehicle is called upon to make some kind of driving-based check, often referred to as a Crew Check, the standard ability used is Dexterity, but the GM could substitute Intelligence when it feels more appropriate. Personal Vehicles get hurled into combat situations frequently in ThunderCats. In an effort to make their use more exciting and narratively attached to the overall use of charges for technological elements of the ThunderCats universe, vehicles follow their own kinds of rules in combat. Vehicles function like a mount or individual creature in combat, following all the standard rules for Initiative order, action economy, D20 Tests, and so forth, but follow a few specific rules of their own: • A vehicle that is not actively being crewed is considered to have the Paralyzed condition. • A vehicle must expend 1 charge to enter Combat Mode when it is called to roll Initiative. Combat Mode lasts up to 10 minutes. • While in Combat Mode, the vehicle can expend 1 charge to gain Advantage on any D20 Test it is supposed to make (no action required). • On its point in the Initiative order, the vehicle is allowed to perform one Maneuver (Action), one Maneuver (Bonus), and a number of Maneuvers (Crew) equal to the number of Crew the vehicle is being manned by. It may also make a single Maneuver (Reaction) between the end of its own turn and the beginning of its next turn. A vehicle can’t take the same Maneuver twice in the same turn. Several standard Maneuvers are detailed in the section below. TRANSPORTATION CHARGE USE Distance Travelled Charge Used (by Vehicle Size Category) Medium Large Huge Gargantuan Less than 30 miles 1 1 1 1 31-100 miles 2 1 1 1 101-1,000 miles 3 2 1 1 2 1 1,001+ miles (same planet) 5 3 Interplanetary N/A Vehicle’s Maximum Charges ½ Maximum Charge Capacity

#### COMBAT MODE MANEUVERS

In ThunderCats, specific Vehicle Components added to a vehicle will grant them new and detailed Maneuvers the vehicle is able to perform, but there are a number of standard Maneuvers that all vehicles have at their disposal while in Combat Mode. ATTACK (ACTION) – 0 CHARGES The vehicle moves up to its Speed and makes a single attack roll with one Weapon System. EVASIVE MANEUVERS (ACTION) – CHARGES VARY (SEE DESCRIPTION) The vehicle expends a number of charges equal to 3 minus its Dexterity modifier (minimum 0 charges). A negative Dexterity modifier is added to the base number of 3 instead (no maximum). Once expended, the vehicle moves up to its Speed. In addition, all attack rolls targeting the vehicle have Disadvantage until the beginning of its next turn. RAMMING SPEED (ACTION) – 1 CHARGE The vehicle moves up to its Speed in a straight line. At any point along this movement, the vehicle is allowed to make a Ram attack against a target that comes within the vehicle’s reach. ALL OUT ATTACK (ACTION) – 2 CHARGES The vehicle moves up to half its Speed and makes a number of attacks with its Weapon Systems equal to the highest Proficiency Bonus of one of the vehicle’s Crew. One of these attacks must be a Ram. DAMAGE CONTROL (BONUS) – 0 CHARGES One Crew makes a DC 14 Intelligence check, adding its Proficiency Bonus if it possesses the proper tools to make repairs. On a successful check, the vehicle removes one lasting Critical Damage effect or regains Hit Points equal to twice its Damage Threshold. SIDESWIPE (BONUS) – 1 CHARGE This Maneuver must be made immediately before making a Ramming Speed or All Out Attack Maneuver. The vehicle is allowed to make a number of extra Ram attack rolls at different targets equal to the highest Proficiency Bonus of one of the vehicle’s Crew during that Maneuver’s movement. TACTICAL SPEED (CREW) – 1 CHARGE The vehicle moves up to half its Speed. EMBARK/DISEMBARK (CREW) – 0 CHARGE A single Crew disembarks, immediately rolling Initiative, or a single creature embarks, removing itself from the Initiative order and becoming a Crew/Passenger of the vehicle. ADDITIONAL MANEUVERS… There are more Combat Mode Maneuvers available to vehicles based on their own unique designs and the Vehicle Components that are integrated into them. These are covered under the example vehicles’ Vehicle Components section found later in this chapter.

### Vehicle Damage Rolls

Vehicles suffer damage much in the same way as creatures and other targets; game effects such as attacks, traps, or environmental circumstances reduce the vehicle’s current Hit Points (as long as the damage is equal to or greater than the vehicle’s Damage Threshold). Unlike characters, which are Incapacitated at 0 Hit Points and begin making Death Saving Throws, vehicles are more difficult to destroy. This is translated as vehicles suffering Critical Damage instead. Critical Damage are caused by Vehicle Damage Rolls, and represent specific parts of a vehicle becoming damaged or inoperable, eventually leading up to the complete destruction of the vehicle itself. A vehicle is forced to make a Vehicle Damage Roll whenever it suffers any amount of damage while at 0 Hit Points and whenever it suffers a Critical Hit from any attack roll that inflicts damage. Whenever called upon to make a Vehicle Damage Roll, roll 1d8 and consult the table below. It details each type of Critical Damage Effect the vehicle suffers, which are explained after the table. Some of the below Critical Damage Effects are instantaneous, having a singular effect when suffered. Others are noted as being “Lasting,” which will continue to negatively affect the vehicle until repaired.

VEHICLE DAMAGE ROLL d8 Critical Damage Effect 1 Dents & Scratches 2 Locomotion Damaged 3 Weapon System Damaged 4 Component Damaged 5 Hull Weakened 6 Crew/Passenger Damage 7 Fuel Rupture 8 Massive Damage 9 Wrecked 10 Catastrophic Damage Dents & Scratches. The vehicle suffers minimal body damage. The vehicle’s Damage Threshold value is reduced to 0 against the next source of damage inflicted upon it. Locomotion Damaged. Until this Lasting Critical Damage is repaired, the vehicle’s Speed is reduced by 10 feet. Weapon System Damaged. One random Weapon System (non-Ram) is damaged. Until this Lasting Critical Damage is repaired, attacks with this Weapon System have Disadvantage. If a damaged Weapon System suffers this Critical Damage again, it is destroyed and will need to be fully replaced before it can be used again. Component Damaged. One random (non-Weapon System) Vehicle Component is damaged. Until this Lasting Critical Damage is repaired, it does not offer its benefits, Maneuvers, etc. If a damaged Vehicle Component suffers this Critical Damage again, it is destroyed and will need to be fully replaced. Hull Weakened. Until this Lasting Critical Damage is repaired, the vehicle’s Damage Threshold is reduced by half (round down). Crew/Passenger Damage. A random Crew or Passenger currently embarked on the vehicle takes damage equal to half the damage the vehicle suffered to inflict this Critical Damage Effect result (round down). Fuel Rupture.
