The vehicle’s current charges are halved (round down). Massive Damage. Until this Lasting Critical Damage is repaired, you roll twice for all future Vehicle Damage Rolls and take both results. Wrecked. The vehicle is immediately reduced to 0 Hit Points and has the Incapacitated condition. Catastrophic Damage. The vehicle explodes in a shower of debris and arcs of fire and energy. The vehicle is destroyed and all embarked Crew and Passengers take 1d4 times the vehicle’s Damage Threshold in Force damage. Everything within twice the vehicle’s reach for its Ram attack makes a DC 15 Dexterity saving throw, taking half that Force damage on a failed save (round down).

### Vehicle Components

Due to the nature of invention, there are effectively countless Vehicle Components possible, but there are a number of them seen as commonplace or unique to the ThunderCats universe. The following section details a wide variety of Vehicle Component options to use. When adding a Vehicle Component to a vehicle, it is suggested to give it a flair, style, and name of your own design to cement the nature of your own vehicle. After adding an Adaptive Mobility Vehicle Component to the ThunderTank to add a Burrow Speed, Panthro calls them “digging claws,” while the Lunataks called the Star Engines for the Sky-Tomb a “Thundrillium Drive.” As long as the Vehicle Component functions as its rules detail, the name and physical description is just cosmetic flavor. Each Vehicle Component entry details the narrative behind the component, how many Component Slots it requires to integrate into a vehicle, and any pertinent game information it adds to the vehicle. Adaptive Mobility (1 Slot). Some kind of additional equipment is added to allow the vehicle to move in different ways. This adds a new type of movement (Fly, Burrow, Climb, etc.) to the vehicle at a base Speed determined by its Size Category. Armor Plating (1 Slot). The vehicle’s protective layering is increased, adding +2 to the vehicle’s Armor Class. This component can be integrated into the same vehicle a maximum of twice. Assisted Controls (1 Slot). The vehicle’s piloting or drive controls are designed to be easier for one person to use. One creature crewing this vehicle counts as a number of Crew equal to its Proficiency Bonus for Minimum Crew purposes. Atmo-Bubble (1 Slot). The vehicle projects and contains breathable life support for its Crew and Passengers, allowing them to breathe normally underwater, in a poisoned atmosphere, or even a vacuum. This Vehicle Component costs 0 Slots if the vehicle has a Swim Speed. Autopilot Suite (1+ Slots). The vehicle is fitted with automated software that can operate it on a very basic level. The vehicle is considered to always have a single Crew with a Wisdom, Intelligence, and Charisma of 10, and a Proficiency Bonus equal to the number of Slots used in integrating this Vehicle Component. The Autopilot Suite may only ever perform one Maneuver per turn, regardless of type. Cargo Pod (1 Slot; Large-size or larger). The vehicle mounts a dedicated space to hold additional cargo, doubling its current Cargo value. Corporeal Steerage (1 Slot). The vehicle requires the Crew to steer the vehicle with their whole body instead of using hand-based controls. The crewing creature uses its own Strength (Athletics) or Dexterity (Acrobatics) for Crew Checks in this vehicle. Engine Efficiency (1 Slot). The vehicle’s engines, rotors, or other locomotive generation systems are enhanced, increasing its Speed by 20 feet. Enhanced Functionality (1 Slot). The mechanisms of the vehicle are upgraded and enhanced, increasing the vehicle’s Strength or Dexterity by 2 (your choice). Frame Reinforcement (1 Slot). The internal structures of the vehicle are reinforced, increasing the vehicle’s maximum Hit Points by 10 percent. Grapnel Cableshot (1 Slot). The vehicle has launchable grappling hooks, gravity tether, or something similar that it can fire at a solid object up to 200 feet away. When launched as a Maneuver (Bonus), the vehicle becomes an anchor for climbing, repelling, etc. between it and the object in question. In addition, the object the cableshot is attached to can be considered as being “carried” by the vehicle for pulling or dragging purposes. Melee Weapon System (1 Slot). Choose a style of melee weapon (claw, drill, piston-hammer, etc.). This adds a Weapon System to the vehicle using the reach of its Ram. It uses the damage dice of its Ram attack (ignoring the Enhanced Ram component if it has it) plus two additional dice of the same size. For example, a Melee Weapon System on a Huge vehicle would deal 6d8 damage (2d8 more than its Ram’s attack’s usual 4d8). The Weapon System can deal either Bludgeoning, Piercing, or Slashing damage, whatever is appropriate to the style of weapon chosen. Melee Energy Weapon System (2 Slots). Choose a style of melee energy weapon (fusion spike, flamethrower, etc.). This adds a Weapon System to the vehicle using the damage dice and reach of its Ram (ignoring the Enhanced Ram component if it has it). The Weapon System can deal either Acid, Cold, Fire, Lightning, or Thunder damage, whatever is appropriate to the style of weapon chosen. Melee System Enhancer (1 Slot). One of the vehicle’s melee weapon systems is augmented and updated. Choose a Melee Weapon System to receive one of the following benefits, which can be chosen more than once unless listed otherwise: • Increase its reach by 5 feet. • Increase its damage by one damage die of the same type. • Choose up to half the dice rolled for its damage rolls (round down). The damage type of those dice becomes Acid, Cold, Fire, Lightning, or Thunder damage (Ex. a fusion saw system on a Huge vehicle could deal 2d8 Slashing damage and 2d8 Fire damage instead of the usual 4d8 Slashing damage). This augment can only be chosen once per Ranged Weapon System. • Inflicts one of the following conditions when it scores a Critical Hit: Blinded, Deafened, Pacified, Poisoned, Prone, Restrained, Stunned, or Weakened. The Prone condition can’t be inflicted on a Gargantuan target. Each condition other than Prone lasts until the end of the vehicle’s next turn.

Ranged Weapon System (1 Slot). Choose a style of ranged weapon (cannon, harpoon launcher, blade flinger). This adds a Weapon System to the vehicle with a normal range of 60 feet and a long range of 120 feet using the damage dice of its Ram (ignoring the Enhanced Ram component if it has it). The Weapon System can deal either Bludgeoning, Piercing, or Slashing damage, whatever is appropriate to the style of weapon chosen. Ranged Energy Weapon System (2 Slots). Choose a style of ranged weapon (energy blast, missile, plasma harpoon, cryoblaster, etc.). This adds a Weapon System to the vehicle with a normal range of 60 feet and a long range of 120 feet using the damage dice of its Ram (ignoring the Enhanced Ram component if it has it). The Weapon System can deal either Acid, Cold, Fire, Lightning, or Thunder damage, whatever is appropriate to the style of weapon chosen. Ranged System Enhancer (1 Slot). One of the vehicle’s ranged weapon systems is augmented and updated. Choose a Ranged Weapon System to receive one of the following benefits, which can be chosen more than once unless listed otherwise: • • • • • Increase its attack roll bonus by +2. Increase its range by 30/60 feet. Double the distance values of its area of effect (if any). Increase its damage dice by a single die of the same type. Choose up to half the dice rolled for its damage rolls (round down). The damage type of those dice becomes Acid, Cold, Fire, Lightning, or Thunder damage (Ex. an acid flechette system on a Huge vehicle could deal 2d8 Piercing damage and 2d8 Acid damage instead of the usual 4d8 Piercing damage). This augment can only be chosen once per Ranged Weapon System. • Inflicts one of the following conditions when it scores a Critical Hit: Blinded, Deafened, Pacified, Poisoned, Prone, Restrained, Stunned, or Weakened. The Prone condition can’t be inflicted on a Gargantuan target. Each condition other than Prone lasts until the end of the vehicle’s next turn. Turret Weapon (1 Slot). One of the vehicle’s weapons is mounted to a retractable turret. Choose one of the vehicle’s Ranged Weapon Systems worth 3 Slots or less (after any other Vehicle Components augmenting it) to mount to the turret. Attacking with the chosen Weapon System becomes a Maneuver (Crew).

Ejection Pods (2 Slots). The vehicle is fitted with an ejection system to safely hurl the Crew or Passengers away from the vehicle in case of emergency. As a Maneuver (Reaction) used when the vehicle suffers a Critical Damage Effect, the Crew or Passengers can automatically disembark and move up to 60 feet away as if each had a Fly Speed. If this leaves the Crew or Passenger in mid-air, additional equipment (parachute, grav-buffer, etc.) allows each to float safely to the ground as per the Feather Fall spell. Engine Booster (2 Slots). The vehicle can funnel additional power into its mobility. As a Maneuver (Bonus) that expends 1 charge, the vehicle doubles its Speed, and gains Advantage on all Dexterity checks and Dexterity saving throws until the start of its next turn. Enhanced Ram (2 Slots). The vehicle’s contact points are augmented and reinforced to inflict additional damage upon impact. The vehicle’s Ram attack damage dice are increased by one die type (Ex. D8s become D10s, and D10s become D12s) and its damage dice is increased by a single die of the same type. Evasion Booster (2 Slots). The vehicle has additional thrusters or other mobilization efforts that cause it to juke and jink out of the way of attacks. As a Maneuver (Reaction) that expends 1 charge when the vehicle makes a saving throw, it may move up to half its Speed without provoking Opportunity Attacks. If its new position moves it out of range or otherwise makes it impossible to be targeted by the effect, the vehicle avoids the effect entirely. Manipulation Arrays (2 Slots). The vehicle is fitted with arms, claws, tentacles, or some other form of appendages that allow it to interact with their environment. The vehicle gains a special Melee Weapon System that can be used to lift, open, carry, etc., but also can be used to grapple a target of a smaller size than it using the vehicle’s Strength and the highest Proficiency Bonus of one of the vehicle’s Crew when determining the save DC. Overpower Cells (2 Slots). The vehicle can draw on a deeper well of power when it needs to. The vehicle can expend 1 additional charge to utilize any Maneuver (Action) as a Maneuver (Bonus). Passenger Quarters (2 Slots; Huge Only). The vehicle is modified to hold additional creatures, doubling its current Maximum Crew value. Protective Coating (2 Slots). The vehicle receives a special chassis layering of alloys and chemicals that make it resistant to a specific kind of non-physical damage. The vehicle gains Resistance to one of the following damage types: Acid, Cold, Fire, Lightning, Necrotic, Radiant, and Thunder. If 3 Slots are used when integrating this Vehicle Component, one additional damage type may be chosen. Self-Repair Node (2 Slots). The vehicle has automated repair systems that can be triggered to mend systems quickly. By expending 1 charge, the vehicle can make two Damage Control (Bonus) actions as a single Maneuver (Crew). Shield Projector (2 Slots). The vehicle is mounted with a temporary forcefield projection system. As a Maneuver (Reaction) that expends 2 charges, the vehicle can raise its shields to gain a +5 bonus to AC and Immunity to Force damage until the beginning of its next turn. Thundrillium Catalyzer (2 Slots). The vehicle gains a chemical or energy catalyst that helps maintain thundrillium supplies, adding 5 to its Maximum Charges. Capture Beam (3 Slots; Large, Huge, or Gargantuan Only). The vehicle has a limited range, one-way teleportation beam that allows it to forcibly pull creatures and objects into it, storing them in a dedicated place. As a Maneuver (Action) that expends 3 charges, the vehicle can make a ranged attack roll against a smaller sized creature or object within 60 feet. If the attack hits, a creature can choose to make a Dexterity saving throw (DC equal to the attack roll result) to avoid the beam. On a failed save (or the target is an object), the target is teleported instantly into a secured holding location (DC 22 Strength check to force open) within the vehicle, counting as Cargo for carrying purposes. Salvo Ranged Weapon System (3 Slots). Choose a style of ranged weapon with an area of effect payload ram(swarm of rockets, flamethrower, shrapnel grenades, solar flare beam, etc.). This adds a Ranged Weapon System to the vehicle with using the damage damage of its Ram (ignoring the Enhanced Ram component if it has it), and deals the damage type appropriate to the style of weapon chosen, as if it was a Ranged Energy Weapon System, in one of the following areas of effect: • A 20-foot-radius Sphere within 120 feet. • A 45-foot-long, 5-foot-wide Line projected from the vehicle. • A 30-foot Cone projected from the vehicle. • A 15-foot Emanation projected from the vehicle. Each creature in the area of effect makes a DC 16 Dexterity saving throw, taking the damage referenced above on a failed save, or half as much damage on a successful one. Each time this Ranged Weapon System is used twice, it requires a Maneuver (Action) or three Maneuver (Crew) actions total to properly reload it for usage. Secondary Control Pods (3 Slots; Huge or Gargantuan Only). The vehicle has two additional cockpit-like pods that can be used to aid in piloting the vehicle, but are also capable of launching as miniature flyers that may reattach later. Launching or reattaching the flyers requires the expenditure of 1 charge from the main vehicle and a Maneuver (Action), in the same fashion as the Embark/Disembark Maneuver. The detached flyers are Large vehicles with a Fly Speed of 60 feet and no Vehicle Components of their own. For each additional Slot above 3 used when integrating this Vehicle Component, one additional flyer is available.

Stealth Cloak (3 Slots). The vehicle can project a field of energy that hides it from view and mutes its presence. As a Maneuver (Action) that expends 2 charges, the vehicle has the Invisible condition and gains Advantage on Dexterity (Stealth) checks for up to 10 minutes, until it makes any kind of attack roll, causes a target to make a saving throw, or until it loses Hit Points for any reason. Universal Prism (3 Slots). The vehicle has been fitted with an arcane focusing device able to unleash the crews’ magical abilities. As a Maneuver (Action) that expends 1 charge, one of the Crew or Passengers can cast a spell through the Universal Prism, as if it and the vehicle were the same creature. The spell cast this way counts the vehicle as “Self” if necessary for the spell, and is considered as being cast with a spell slot two levels higher than what was used to cast the spell. Workshop (3 Slots; Huge or Gargantuan Only). The vehicle has a specialized space for housing and utilizing the tools and implements for a dedicated skill or talent, such as a scientific laboratory, metalsmithing forge, or arcane sanctum. Choose any Intelligence or Wisdom skill to dedicate the Workshop to. A Crew or Passenger who uses that skill while embarked adds +1 to its Proficiency Bonus and gains Advantage while making ability checks using that skill. Assault Mode (4 Slots). The vehicle is modified to be able to shift into a specialized configuration built for close combat. Ranged weapons are withdrawn into the vehicle and their power reserves are redirected to stability and hydraulics, melee weapons are extended and reinforced, and hull hardpoints are adjusted to better protect the internal mechanisms. The vehicle can shift into Assault Mode as a Maneuver (Action) that expends 3 charges. While in Assault Mode, the following adjustments are made to the vehicle’s profile: • Its Speed is reduced by half (round down). • Its Armor Class increases by 2. • Its Damage Threshold increases by the vehicle’s Constitution modifier. • It makes one additional melee attack roll the first time it makes a melee attack roll each turn. • It can’t make ranged attack rolls or use any Vehicle Component that creates an area of effect. The vehicle can remain in Assault Mode for up to 1 minute, but can expend 1 charge to extend that duration for another minute each time that duration ends (no action required). Star Engines (4 Slots). The vehicle’s propulsion systems and outer hull have been modified to achieve extra-planetary speeds, make interplanetary travels (provided it has a Fly Speed), and insulate itself from the freezing void of outer space. Aside from needing this modification to make these kinds of journeys, the vehicle also gains Immunity to Cold damage and has the ability to operate in a vacuum. Thundrillium Siphon (5 Slots). The vehicle possesses a powerful tool for absorbing energy from thundrillium. Whether it is an augur, a spike, or even a tentacle-like appendage, the vehicle can draw charges out from other objects or vehicles it maintains contact with. As a Maneuver (Action), the vehicle can grapple another object. At the start of the vehicle’s turn, if an object containing charges is Grappled by it, the vehicle steals 1d3 charges from the object. Titan Blaster (8 Slots). The vehicle is modified to house a tremendously powerful beam weapon that can incinerate all but the toughest targets in seconds, but at great cost to energy reserves. The Titan Blaster counts as a Weapon System and a Vehicle Component for Critical Damage Effects, expends 6 charges to fire, and uses the following profile on its stat block: Titan Blaster (6 Charges; Recharge 5 – 6). Dexterity Saving Throw: DC 16, each target in a 120-foot-long, 15-footwide Line. Failure: 33 (6d10) Lightning damage plus 33 (6d10) Fire damage. Success: Half damage. Failure or Success: If the damage from this action results in a target vehicle having half its Hit Points or fewer remaining, the target immediately makes a Vehicle Damage Roll.

### Example

There are a multitude of vehicles found in the ThunderCats universe. The following entries detail a number of the most iconic and unique examples of them, including their most common arrays of Vehicle Components they have been known to incorporate. It should be noted that the statistics below do not take any specific Crew talents or proficiencies into account, and list only the base values for the vehicle in question. Additionally, the Speed, AC, or other components of these unique vehicles may not accurately match the usual, due to their fantastical construction by various ThunderCats factions. If you see a Vehicle Component in these stat blocks marked with “Special,” this is an indicator of such a component that has been augmented in such a fashion.

#### BEDLAM

Captain Hammerhand’s massive sailing ship, the Bedlam, serves as the pirates’ infamous headquarters and main conveyance around the seas of Third Earth. As a combination of stolen technologies and ancient traditions, the Bedlam uses a standard sail for pushing this giant construction around to bring powder-packed cannons to bear, but also supports several booster rockets for bursts of much needed speed and a masthead laser capable of crippling targets with a single blast. #### ELECTRO-CHARGER

Designed and operated by Captain Scrape, a salvager and resource-pirate of the highest caliber, the Electric Eelship is a massive interplanetary vessel used to cross the stars and snatch up targets into its wide maw. Its design is based on a flexible, serpentlike fish, allowing it to bend around, constrict, or even electrocute objects, equally useful for crushing resources to be claimed or enemies standing between Scrape and his quarry! The standard patrol vehicle of the Interplanetary Control Force, the Electro-Charger is an advanced single-pilot vessel made for ground, air, and interplanetary travel. Each ICF Evil-Chaser

#### FELINER

The chief interplanetary vessel of the ThunderCats, the Feliner was designed much in the same fashion as the escape ships that fled Thundera as it broke apart. Pared down and streamlined to better survive against the odds with all of Third Earth’s new dangers, and not as roomy or comfortable as the Thunderian transport cruisers, the Feliner is perfect for the ThunderCats’ fast transit.

#### FISTPOUNDER

This mu’tant-invented tank takes the concept of subtlety and throws it out the nearest window… and then drops a ten ton boulder onto it. The Fistpounder is a land-based siege vehicle designed to bring a pair of titanic, hydraulic fist-rams crushing down onto anything that gets too near. Alternatively, until the distance is closed, an elevated mounted array of energy cannons wear a target down or dissuade airborne threats. With the right pilot in the driver’s seat, little can withstand the onslaught of a marauding Fistpounder. #### HERCULES THE FROG TITAN

Hercules, or Herkie for short, is one of two amphibious robotic frog vehicles used to protect the Great Plug on the ocean floor of Third Earth. Capable of operating on land as easily as underwater, Hercules moves around primarily by walking on its rear legs, looking out at the world from behind the pilots’ cockpit-eyes. This leaves the forelimbs free to serve as powerful tools, or as weapons when the need arises.

#### ICE RUNNER

Made infamous by the Lunataks’ icy sorceress, Chilla, the Ice Runner is a one-person flying vehicle with blade-like wings that can fold down to serve as landing runners to skate along the ground as well. It uses a cryokinetic beam as its primary weapon which avoids dipping into the greater thundrillium reserves fueling the Ice Runner, especially when it is forced to activate its star drive to achieve extra-planetary travels.

#### MUTANK

The Mutank is an older, somewhat rodent-themed design from the battle-torn sprawls of Plun-Darr, brought to Third Earth to serve the mu’tants as a heavily armored fast-attack vehicle. Its internal design elements are built to be simple and easily repaired, focusing primarily on protecting the mechanisms surrounding the Mutank’s powerful main weapon. The vortex cannon launches shells packed with concentrated kinetic energy, blasting anything unfortunate enough to get caught in its swirling, explosive payload.

It is capable of rapid maneuverability in the air on repulsor wings or racing along on retractable wheels, bringing its nose-mounted laser cannon to bear on any foes. If the SkyCutter cannot deal with enemies at range, it leans into its namesake using two wing-mounted buzzsaws capable of ripping through most materials with ease. Built to be their dark and sinister home away from home, this towering hulk of stone, alloy, and salvaged technology was assembled through forced labor. Bolkins, Brutemen, and Wollos toiled for months under Luna’s harsh oversight, and the malicious direction of the other Lunataks. The final result is a slow moving warship capable of laying waste to swathes of foes, but the later addition of a Thundrillium Drive in an attempt to travel away from Third Earth revealed a massive flaw in the Sky-Tomb’s design—an unbearably deep requirement for fuel beyond normal vehicular limitations. #### SPACEBOARD

#### THUNDERCLAW

This modified hoverboard is a favorite sports-leisure device used by Thunderian youth, but was quickly multipurposed by the “ThunderKittens” when they first came to Third Earth. Not terribly effective as combat assets themselves, spaceboards are primarily useful as fast modes of transport that leave the rider’s hands free to wield weapons, operate devices, and so on. A small and maneuverable flyer used as a reliable form of transport and moving cargo around, the ThunderClaw is most well known for its “energic tether projector,” an energy-based towing and hauling beam emitter. As it is not designed to be a combat asset, most ThunderClaw pilots will likely use the vehicle’s speed to escape conflict rather than fighting. Spaceboard A flyer designed with a small team in mind, the ThunderStrike is among the newest of the ThunderCats’ designs built for planetary travel. It was a dangerous time on Third Earth when it was invented, which gave good cause for enhanced weaponry to be mounted to the triple-cockpit design. The

THUNDERTANK One of the most iconic of Panthro’s designs on Third Earth, the ThunderTank is a universally useful land vehicle in the ThunderCats’ motor pool. It is the quintessential ThunderCat vehicle, and occupies most of Panthro’s own tinkering time, as he loves to switch out its modular components often. In addition to its extremely durable chassis, it is known for its all-terrain claw housings, grinding jaws, energy beam array, and a turreted blaster system. The ThunderTank is always ready for a quick swap of some of its gear when a new mission calls; climbing sheer walls, diving under the sea, and even putting out forest fires have all been some of its uses before! It should be noted that the ThunderTank is Panthro’s favorite vehicle, and he regularly tests out new Vehicle Components with it. Depending on the mission or Panthro’s curious ingenuity, the ThunderTank is often able to perform unique actions and Maneuvers due to his own Personal Modification abilities gained from the Operator Fighter subclass. In addition, due to Panthro’s tinkering prowess, its Maximum Charges and Component Slots are increased by +3. Blaster Turret. Dexterity Saving Throw: DC 16, each target in a 20-foot-radius Sphere within 120 feet. Failure: 27 (5d10) Fire damage. Success: Half damage. Each time this Ranged Weapon System is used twice, it requires a Maneuver (Action) or three Maneuver (Crew) actions total before it can be used twice again. 175

## Chapter 4: Friends and Foes

176

“You cannot conceive the various dangers of Third Earth. But I can, for I am one of them. Danger envelops this planet. There is no refuge for you Thunderians here. The land itself turns against you. It obeys me.” —Mumm-Ra the Ever-Living This section outlines both the prominent non-player characters (NPCs) in ThunderCats adventures, as well as the varied creatures of Third Earth and beyond. Each includes a description as well as a full stat block for ease of implementation in your campaign. If an NPC or creature has player classes listed in parentheses next to its creature type as a tag, it is considered to have levels in those classes for the purpose of attuning to items, or in reference to other traits, actions, or abilities. Although this chapter presents many stat blocks, not every creature the ThunderCats have been known to encounter is included. Some, like dragons, gargoyles, or mu’tant soldiers, can already be represented using existing stat blocks from the Fifth Edition core rules.

### ThunderCats

The ThunderCats are a group of warriors formed of Thunderian nobles from the doomed planet Thundera. Their ranks include Lord Lion-O, Panthro the Deadly, Cheetara the Fast, Tygra the Stealthy, Jaga the Wise, the mischievous ThunderKittens, and the loyal Snarf. After Thundera’s destruction, they fled aboard their ship, only to be attacked by a group of mu’tants. Jaga piloted the ship to safety on Third Earth, which became the ThunderCats’ temporary home. Though Jaga died during the journey, his ghost continued to guide Lion-O. Later, three new Thunderian refugees joined their ranks: Bengali the Blacksmith, Pumyra the Healer, and Lynx-O the Seer. Like the others, they swore to protect their young lord Lion-O and uphold the virtues of the Code of Thundera: justice, truth, honor, and loyalty. Eventually, most of the ThunderCats, except Tygra and Pumyra, returned to the restored Thundera, leaving Third Earth behind.

### Characters of Note

The following NPCs are those with noteworthy reputations among the ThunderCats, either as members or staunch enemies. Some hold great power and sway, while others are merely interesting individuals with stories to share. Regardless, all can add intriguing encounters to a ThunderCats adventure. If an NPC references a magic item or other special equipment, it can be found within chapter 3 of this book, unless otherwise noted in that NPC’s stat block. 177

#### BENGALI THE BLACKSMITH

#### ACTIONS

Bengali is a descendant of the legendary Thunderian blacksmith Tigris, the master who forged the Sword of Omens. Following in his ancestor’s footsteps, he became a skilled craftsman on Thundera and has even reforged the damaged Sword of Omens through his own expert handiwork. He wields his Hammer of Thundera with equal skill as a weapon and as a tool. A longtime rival of Tygra, Bengali can sometimes act on impulse, yet he is a deft fighter, expert pilot, and a vital member of the team. Once engaged to Cheetara, their relationship suffered under the weight of duty and responsibility. Multiattack. Bengali makes two attacks, using Hammer of Thundera or Energy Bolt in any combination. #### CHEETARA THE FAST

Cheetara is a warrior of speed and agility, yet she is far more than a fast runner. Her athleticism, honed in the Feather Meadows of Thundera, allowed her to survive the deadly Night Eagles of Arriokh, giving her the strength, skill, and reflexes that make her one of the toughest ThunderCats. She can run at speeds exceeding 170 miles per hour for up to two hours, her leg muscles and tendons possessing the tensile strength of spring steel, turning her into a blur across any landscape. Beyond her physical gifts, Cheetara possesses a potent sixth sense that borders on the precognitive. While not fully able to see the future, she can enter a trance-like state to witness events of the past, perceive hidden truths in the present, and glimpse potential future occurrences. These psychic abilities, though powerful, drain her greatly, so she uses them sparingly. It was this sense that allowed her to warn Claudus of Thundera’s impending destruction and give them time to prepare for exodus. 179

Unlike many of her comrades, Cheetara was not born into nobility. She earned her place among the ThunderCats through the Great Race of Thundera, an annual contest that granted the victor entry into the Thunderian court, regardless of origin. During this race, her sixth sense saved a young Lion-O from a deadly fall, cementing her status among the ThunderCats. Her weapon of choice is a telescoping bo-staff, clipped to her left gauntlet. With a flick of her wrist, it extends into a multi-purpose quarterstaff, equally effective for melee combat, javelin throws, or vaulting. In battle, she moves with grace and precision, combining her incredible speed with her staff to devastating effect. Beyond her combat abilities, Cheetara is admired for her beauty, friendliness, and calm temperament, making her the most even-keeled of the ThunderCats. Her upbringing also instilled a deep reverence for the Thunderian gods, and she often visited their temples alongside young Lion-O. Cheetara shared a brief romance with Tygra on Thundera, which ended due to his obsession with work, leading her into a serious relationship with Tygra’s younger rival Bengali. That relationship also failed, however, and with Thundera lost, she and Tygra have begun to rekindle their old flame. Cheetara Medium Humanoid (Thunderian), Neutral Good Thunderian Swiftness. Cheetara has Advantage on Dexterity (Acrobatics) checks. AC 20 Initiative +11 (21) HP 104 (16d8 + 32) Speed 45 ft., Climb 35 ft. #### TRAITS

Super Speed. Outside of combat, Cheetara’s Speed is effectively 1,495 feet (170 mph). She can maintain that Speed for 2 hours total, which resets after she finishes a Long Rest. In addition, at the start of her turn in combat, Cheetara can choose to double her Speed for that turn. If she does so, it counts as 15 minutes toward the 2 hour total of her out of combat Speed. At the end of a turn where she has used all 2 hours of her out of combat Speed, her Speed changes to 20 feet and she has a -5 penalty to AC until she finishes a Short or Long Rest. Collapsible Staff. Cheetara’s staff doesn’t require an object interaction to draw or stow. It can collapse into a compact size to attach to a cuff on her arm, or extend to its full length. The staff can only be used to make attack rolls while fully extended. While fully extended, Cheetara adds her Dexterity score to the distance of her High Jumps. 180

#### JAGA THE WISE

Jaga is the legendary former Lord of the ThunderCats and ghostly mentor to Lion-O. Chosen by King Claudus, Lion-O’s father, he earned the love and respect of all Thunderians through his wisdom, courage, and compassion. As head of the ThunderCat council and closest adviser to King Claudus, Jaga embodied the ideals of justice, honor, and loyalty, serving as both friend and protector to his king through Thundera’s final days. A master of combat and guardian of the Sword of Omens, Jaga led the ThunderCats in devotion to Thundera’s sacred code, holding its ideals as the cornerstone of their civilization. Yet when duty demanded it, Jaga waged secret wars in defense of his world. Quiet battles fought in shadows, which might not have been met with pride had they ever come to light. Tragically, Jaga was indirectly responsible for Thundera’s destruction. In defeating the mu’tant warlord Ratilla, he seized the Sword of Plun-Darr and cast it into the planet’s deepest canyon. The sword’s violent energies destabilized Thundera, setting in motion the tremors that would one day destroy it. As disaster loomed, Jaga stood beside Claudus, now weakened and blind from battle, to secure the future of Lion-O and their people. Recognizing his limits, Claudus entrusted Jaga with the sacred duty of guiding the young heir. When Thundera fell, Jaga led the ThunderCats’ exodus aboard the Royal Flagship, accompanied by Cheetara, Tygra, Panthro, WilyKit and WilyKat, Snarf, and the young Lion-O. After the fleet was crippled by the mu’tants of Plun-Darr, Jaga took the helm himself, piloting the flagship alone while the others slept in suspended animation. He perished just before reaching Third Earth, sacrificing himself for the survival of his people. Jaga’s ghost endures, bound to the Eye of Thundera, and he continues to guide Lion-O from the Astral Realm. In this form, he roams the Astral Realm seeking knowledge and offering counsel to Lion-O during the most critical tests of leadership. Through his guidance, the legacy of Thundera and the ThunderCats’ sacred code lives on, preserving justice, truth, honor, and loyalty across generations. Jaga Medium Undead (Thunderian), Lawful Good AC 13 Initiative +3 (13) HP 45 (10d8) Speed 5 ft., Fly 30 ft. #### TRAITS

Bound to the Ethereal. Jaga can’t leave the Ethereal Plane and can only be affected by creatures on the Ethereal Plane. He can always be perceived by Lion-O, but otherwise has the Invisible condition. Creatures that can see into the Ethereal Plane (with the exception of the See Invisibility spell) can see Jaga as if he was visible, but other effects that would allow a creature to see Invisible creatures or objects can’t perceive Jaga. Ghostly Sight. Jaga can see 90 feet into the Material Plane. Incorporeal Movement. Jaga can move through other creatures and objects as if they were Difficult Terrain. He takes 5 (1d10) Force damage if he ends his turn inside an object. Thunderian Wisdom. Jaga has Advantage on Wisdom (Insight) checks. In addition, his Passive Perception is increased by 4 (accounted for in his statistics).

#### BONUS ACTIONS

Aid From Beyond. Jaga takes the Help action. The ally in either effect of the Help action must be a Thunderian.

Parry. Trigger: Jaga is hit by a melee attack roll while holding a weapon. Response: Jaga adds 5 to his AC against that attack, possibly causing it to miss. 181

#### LION-O, LORD OF THE THUNDERCATS

Born heir to the throne of Thundera, Lion-O always knew he was destined to become Lord of the ThunderCats. Under the guidance of Jaga the Wise, he studied the Code of Thundera though its full weight was beyond his young understanding. When Thundera was destroyed, Lion-O fled with his people, still only a child. During their voyage to a new world, a malfunction in his suspension chamber caused his body to mature into adulthood while his mind remained that of a boy. Awakening on Third Earth, Lion-O found himself a king unprepared for his crown. Immature and impulsive, he struggled with the responsibilities of leadership, often acting in haste or letting curiosity lead him into danger. Yet through spectral guidance from Jaga’s ghost and lessons learned from his fellow ThunderCats, he began to grow, learning patience, wisdom, and compassion in equal measure. Over time, he transformed from a reckless youth into a noble leader worthy

#### -o

Lion-O’s courage and virtue are matched only by his strength and agility. Nearly as strong as Panthro, he can lift massive objects and fight with formidable skill, though he prefers cunning and strategy to brute force. He often seeks to resolve conflicts without violence, using his intelligence and heart to outwit foes rather than destroy them. Haunted by the memory of Thundera’s destruction, Lion-O carries both the pain of loss and the determination to rebuild. His greatest ambition is to restore his homeland and lead his people into a new age of unity. His primary weapon is the all-powerful Sword of Omens, which holds the Eye of Thundera in its hilt. When not in use, it rests in Lion-O’s Claw Shield, which he keeps attached to his thigh. Lion-O Medium Humanoid (Thunderian), Neutral Good Omen Bolt. Ranged Attack Roll: +7, range 120 ft. Hit: 14 (4d6) Force, Lightning, or Radiant damage (Lion-O’s choice). AC 19 Initiative +8 (18) HP 157 (21d8 + 63) Speed 30 ft., Climb 20 ft. The spell can only target Thunderians, and each one has the Empowered condition for 1 minute immediately after it arrives within 60 feet of Lion-O.

#### LYNX-O THE SEER

Lynx-O is the eldest of the ThunderCats and serves as the team’s mystic and scholar. A devout custodian of Thundera’s temples, he shared a close friendship with Lion-O’s father, Claudus, and was a devoted pupil of Jaga. Beginning his training in early adolescence, Lynx-O honed his mental and spiritual abilities to the point of achieving direct mind-contact with his mentor. His dedication also positioned him as the next in line to succeed Jaga as Cardinal of The Wise, the council of six elder ThunderCats charged with safeguarding the sacred Chronicles of Thundera. Lynx-O also acted as guardian to Pumyra, an orphan whose parents fell during a mu’tant invasion shortly after her birth. His own life was forever altered during Thundera’s destruction, when falling debris blinded him as he escaped the planet’s ruin. Though deprived of his sight, Lynx-O’s other senses sharpened, allowing him to remain a formidable warrior and indispensable member of the team. To overcome his blindness, Lynx-O developed extraordinary abilities and ingenious devices. He can project his voice at ultrasonic frequencies, shattering objects with precision. He crafted the Light Shield, a wrist-mounted device that deflects energy blasts and stores sunlight to be released in blinding bursts while leaving him unaffected. He also invented a Braille board that allows him to “see,” operate the Tower of Omens, and pilot complex vehicles with ease. #### PANTHRO THE DEADLY

Panthro is both the strongest and most intelligent of the ThunderCats, blending formidable physical power with unmatched technical skill. As the team’s engineer, he built all of their vehicles and devices, often enhancing them with his own inventive modifications. Despite his love of tinkering, Panthro is also a fierce warrior. Though smaller than many of his companions, he possesses superhuman strength and extraordinary endurance, able to even lift the massive Thundertank. 185

His combat prowess extends beyond brute force. Panthro is a master of Thunderan martial arts, earning the prestigious Order of the Red Sash in Kottr Kwan-Do, the highest discipline of self-defense, a distinction no other Thundercat has achieved in five generations. His skill makes him a heavy hitter, and he wields his signature battlesticks with deadly precision. Panthro often augments them with inventions that release gases or perform other attacks, and his shoulder spikes can extend or spring out along wires, serving both offensive and defensive purposes. Even his Thundercats logo belt buckle doubles as a multi-use tool, such as a remote-controlled observation disc. Panthro is the most volatile and quick-tempered of the ThunderCats, yet his age and experience allow him to guide and temper the impetuous young Lion-O. Fiercely loyal, Panthro would willingly sacrifice himself for his friends. However, even his strength and skill cannot overcome his one weakness: bats. For reasons unknown, Panthro is terrified of them, often freezing at the sight of a single bat. Enemies such as Mumm-Ra and Safari Joe have exploited this fear to challenge him on numerous occasions. #### PUMYRA THE HEALER

Pumyra is a young Thunderian renowned for her healing skills, with deep knowledge of medicines and herbs that makes her an indispensable ally. She grew up with Lynx-O and Bengali, forming a close bond with both. Though youngest of the adult ThunderCats, she is among the most nimble of Thunderians, moving with the precision and grace of a leopard. Her agility allows her to leap great distances and heights effortlessly. Pumyra carries a tail-like belt that serves multiple purposes: it can sling projectiles stored in a leather pouch at her waist, or be wielded as a whip. Despite her youth, she is headstrong and quick-tempered, but balances these traits with Lynx-O’s guidance, her guardian since birth. Quiet and self-assured, her personality mirrors Tygra’s, which may explain why she chose to remain behind with him when the others returned to Thundera. Healing Expert. Whenever Pumyra rolls a die to determine the number of Hit Points a creature restores, she can reroll the die if it rolls a 1 or 2. She rerolls the die until the result is higher than 2. Thunderian Swiftness. Pumyra has Advantage on Dexterity (Acrobatics) checks.

Multiattack. Pumyra makes three attacks, using Cat’s Tail Whip or Explosive Pellet in any combination. Cat’s Tail Whip. Melee Attack Roll: +7, reach 10 ft. Hit: 11 (2d6 + 4) Slashing damage. Explosive Pellet. Ranged Attack Roll: +7, range 90 ft. Hit: 14 (3d6 + 4) Fire damage. Field Medic (10/Day). Pumyra uses her Healer’s Kit on a creature she can see within 5 feet of her. If the creature has 0 Hit Points, it regains 1 Hit Point. Otherwise, the creature regains 10 (2d6 + 3) Hit Points.

#### BONUS ACTIONS

Leap. Pumyra jumps up to 25 feet by spending 10 feet of movement. 187

#### SNARF OSBERT

Snarf, whose real name is Osbert, is among the oldest of the ThunderCats, though his true age is anyone’s guess. From Lion-O’s earliest days, he served as the young lord’s constant companion and official nursemaid, a title he proudly rephrases as “Guardian.” During Lion-O’s youth in the royal palace, Snarf also managed household duties as the family’s butler, caring for both the estate and the boy he considered his greatest responsibility. Even now that Lion-O has come of age, Snarf remains ever faithful, offering unwavering support that often borders on overprotectiveness. His fussing and frequent lectures may test Lion-O’s patience, yet even he admits they are rarely wrong. Though Snarf speaks with the knowledge and caution of an elder, his nerves often edge into cowardice and pessimism. He is usually the first to protest any reckless adventure, yet when true danger strikes, his bravery, quick thinking, and surprising resourcefulness emerge. Snarf believes his talents go largely unappreciated, despite his many supposed contributions. After all, none can match his accomplishment of reciting all six hundred pages of The Berbil Fruit Cookbook, backward from memory. Today he continues to mostly serve at the Cats’ Lair as chef, though he still insists on being called “Guardian.” Unarmed but far from helpless, Snarf can defend himself with his tail, teeth, and claws when pressed. His small stature grants him remarkable agility, letting him squeeze through tight spaces and slip past danger unseen. He can even communicate with animals, a gift that has proven invaluable more than once during the ThunderCats’ adventures. Snarf Osbert Small Beast (Snarf, Thunderian), Neutral Good AC 12 Initiative +2 (12) HP 31 (7d6 + 7) Speed 30 ft. In addition, once on his turn, he can take the Utilize action using his tail (no action required). Good Fortune. When Snarf rolls a 1 on an ability check or saving throw, he can reroll the die. He must use the new roll. Wily. Snarf has Advantage on saving throws to avoid or end the Charmed condition and against effects that would force Snarf to perform an action or change his behavior, such as the Command or Confusion spells.

Claw. Melee Attack Roll: +1, reach 5 ft. Hit: 3 (1d4 + 1) Slashing damage.

A creature that eats the snack gains 2 (1d4) Temporary Hit Points, or 4 (1d4 + 2) Temporary Hit Points if it is a Thunderian. If handed to a creature, the snack will last for 8 hours before losing its effectiveness, and can be eaten as a Utilize action or Bonus Action. 188

#### TYGRA THE STEALTHY

Tygra is the ThunderCats’ architect and strategist, the quiet mind behind the design of the Cats’ Lair on both Thundera and Third Earth. His work combines artistry with practicality, reflecting the same balance of intellect and discipline that defines him. Though he often retreats into solitude to meditate and collect his thoughts, his calm nature should never be mistaken for weakness. Steadfast and reliable, Tygra remains one of the most dependable members of the team. During the ThunderCats’ voyage to Third Earth, it was Tygra who took command. With Lion-O still a child and unable to lead, Tygra served as the group’s acting head, guiding them with patience and a sense of duty that earned him deep respect. His quiet authority continued even after Lion-O’s ascension, as Tygra became the voice of reason within the ThunderCats’ Council, often mediating between Lion-O’s youthful impulsiveness, Panthro’s temper, and the twins’ mischief. 189

In battle, Tygra is quick and calculating, wielding his bolowhip with precision. The weapon can unleash energy, serve as a lasso, and even manipulate cosmic energy to bend light around him, rendering him invisible for a limited time. He also possesses latent psychic abilities that allow him to project mental images, though he seldom uses them due to the toll they take on him. His composure hides a few vulnerabilities, however; he cannot swim unless cloaked by invisibility, and on rare occasions has shown a susceptibility to addiction. When the ThunderCats returned to Thundera, Tygra and Pumyra remained on Third Earth to maintain the lookout, though he still journeys home whenever his team calls. In his personal life, Tygra once shared a brief romance with Cheetara on Thundera, a relationship that ended when his dedication to work eclipsed his heart. She later pursued a relationship with his younger rival Bengali, but that relationship ended, and with Thundera gone and new beginnings forged, Tygra and Cheetara have quietly begun to rekindle their old flame. #### WILYKAT AND WILYKIT

The youngest members of the ThunderCats, the twin siblings WilyKit and WilyKat, are clever, daring, and endlessly curious. Still in their early teens, they are determined to prove themselves worthy of fighting beside their older companions. Their eagerness often gets them into trouble, as their mischievous experiments and playful recklessness can lead to more problems than they solve. While both are inventive and quick-witted, their personalities differ. WilyKit is spirited and bold, often the first to take action, while WilyKat tends to be more cautious, though no less resourceful. Beneath their teasing rivalry lies a close bond and a shared desire to be taken seriously by the adults they idolize. Panthro’s high-speed hoverboards are their constant companions, giving the twins incredible mobile versatility. Rather than engage foes in direct combat, they prefer to strike from above, using their “bags of tricks” to outsmart enemies. These satchels hold an arsenal of clever gadgets and chemical pellets, from flashbombs and knockout gas to slippery grease and crystal jacks that scatter beneath enemy feet. Both twins also carry a coiled lariat at their waist, useful for combat as well as swinging through the jungles of Third Earth. Their quick reflexes and sharp minds often allow them to escape danger, though their youth and smaller size make them vulnerable against stronger foes. For all their occasional whining or defiance, WilyKit and WilyKat are brave, loyal, and always ready to help their team. Though they sometimes wish they had matured like Lion-O, the ThunderKittens continue to prove that courage, cunning, and creativity knows no age. 191

### Mumm-Ra and Ma-Mutt

Mumm-Ra is a mummified, ancient, and immortal devil-priest of First Earth and the devoted servant of the Ancient Spirits of Evil. Though his emaciated body appears frail, he commands formidable dark magic and an arsenal of cunning tricks. His most fearsome ability is transformation. By invoking the Ancient Spirits, he becomes Mumm-Ra the Ever-Living, a warrior of immense might. This form, however, is not limitless. Over time, his strength wanes, forcing him to return to his Onyx Pyramid to recover. The sight of his own reflection is also a crippling vulnerability, a reminder of the monstrosity he has become. Mumm-Ra’s mortal life began thousands of years ago as Wahankh. The seventh son of the Mystic Shapeshade, who was himself a seventh son, Wahankh was always overshadowed by his older brothers. He became a sem-priest in ancient Egypt along with his faithful hound Ma-Tep, where his explorations led him to the power stones that allowed contact with the Ancient Spirits of Evil. Gradually, he collected these stones and forged a dark pact, gaining tremendous magical power and transforming into Mumm-Ra. This bargain corrupted his flesh, transforming it into necrotic tissue that would never decay and condemning him to eternal life as a living mummy. Ma-Tep underwent a similar transformation, becoming the fearsome Ma-Mutt. When Shapeshade realized the scope of his son’s ambitions, it was too late. Mumm-Ra used his newfound powers to banish his father and brothers from First Earth and attempted to overthrow the pharaoh. The sorcerer Wizz-Ra tried to intervene but was banished by the Great Sphinx due to MummRa’s treachery. Eventually, Mumm-Ra’s wrath was contained. For his crimes against the pharaoh, he was entombed within an Onyx Pyramid, designed to imprison him and his ever-living power forever. Within this tomb, the Ancient Spirits of Evil compelled him to labor, crafting statues in their likeness and corrupting the pyramid into a lair that became a focal point of their power. In return, they granted him the strength to one day return and continue his dark ambitions to conquer all pharaohs. Centuries passed before Mumm-Ra reemerged, only to find that the pharaohs were gone. His actions during this period remain shrouded in mystery. Some legends suggest he assumed countless guises, subtly steering civilizations toward the will of the Ancient Spirits of Evil and influencing the transition from First Earth to Second Earth. Others claim he waited, quietly amassing knowledge and power as civilizations rose and fell. On Second Earth, he became more active, collecting artifacts and clashing with rival mages, conflicts that may have helped shape the transition to Third Earth. By the time of Third Earth, surviving magical beings from Second Earth and those from other planets sought dominance of the world. Evil fought evil, until finally Mumm-Ra seized control of the northern hemisphere, while the four-armed birdlike sorcerer Zaxx ruled the south. Their battles were fierce, and Mumm-Ra ultimately captured Zaxx’s medallion, imprisoning his essence and claiming sole rule over the planet. His reign did not last, however, and was eventually disrupted by the Rammastide uprising, when the oppressed peoples of Third Earth united against him. Despite his overwhelming power, Mumm-Ra was driven back to the Onyx Pyramid. There he rested, allowing the people to grow complacent and believe he had been defeated, waiting patiently for the right moment to return. Over time, the unity of the rebellion dissolved, with tribes and peoples drifting apart, and Mumm-Ra’s evil slowly faded into legend. When the ThunderCats and mu’tants arrived on Third Earth, Mumm-Ra awakened. Seeing an opportunity to reclaim his dominance, he temporarily allied with the mu’tants and attempted to seize the Eye of Thundera, threatening the ThunderCats and the planet itself. Over time, he grew even stronger, learning how to overcome the weakness of his reflection and acquiring powerful artifacts such as the Sword of Plun-Darr from New Thundera. Today, Mumm-Ra remains an eternal schemer, ceaselessly plotting to destroy the ThunderCats, seize the Eye of Thundera, and one day gain power sufficient to rival even the Ancient Spirits of Evil. 193

STR DEX MOD SAVE 15 +2 +2 16 +3 +3 MOD SAVE CON 16 +3 +3 INT 12 +1 +1 MOD SAVE WIS 14 +2 +2 CHA 8 -1 -1 Skills Perception +5 Immunities Charmed (except from Mumm-Ra in any of his forms)a Senses Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 15 Languages – CR 5 (XP 1,800; PB +3)

Project the Master. Ma-Mutt can manifest the effects of the Project Image spell on his turn (no action or Concentration required). The illusory copy of the spell is instead Mumm-Ra in his current form, as if Mumm-Ra had cast the spell himself. Faithful Adjutant. Ma-Mutt has Advantage on D20 Tests while he can see Mumm-Ra in any of his forms. 198

#### ACTIONS

Multiattack. Ma-Mutt makes two Bite attacks. If he is in MaBat form, he instead makes two Slam attacks. Bite. Melee Attack Roll: +5, reach 5 ft. Hit: 5 (1d6 + 2) Piercing damage. #### BONUS ACTIONS

Shape-Shift (3/Day). Ma-Mutt shape-shifts into his Large Ma-Bat form (Speed 5 ft., Fly Speed 50 ft., Strength score 20, Strength saving throw bonus +8, AC 16), or back to his true form. Other than his size, Speed, Strength, and AC, his game statistics are the same in each form. Any equipment he is wearing or carrying isn’t transformed.

### Mu’Tants

The mu’tants of Plun-Darr are a fearsome collection of humanoids, each bearing distinct animalistic traits. On Third Earth, only a small, isolated contingent exists, far from their shattered homeworld. These elite warriors were among the fiercest attackers during the Thunderian Exodus, and now, aware that reinforcements are out of reach, they have entrenched themselves on this new land. Bitter and relentless, they constantly harass the ThunderCats; driven to claim the Eye of Thundera, exact vengeance for the devastation of their world, and finally settle the generations-long war between their people and those of Thundera. Each mu’tant of Third Earth has cast aside their birth name in favor of a title that marks them as the pinnacle of their lineage: Jackalman of the Jackalfolk, Vultureman of the Vulturefolk, and Monkian of the Monkians. Only Slithe and Ratar-O stand apart, their individual prowess earning them renown beyond the confines of their species and commanding respect from all mu’tants alike.

#### JACKALMAN

Born as Kaynar, Jackalman hails from the noblest Jackal pack in Plun-Darran history. Among the mu’tants of Third Earth, Jackalman is crafty but craven, a schemer who prefers deceit to danger. Though second only to Vultureman in intellect, he often lacks the courage to act on his own ideas. He eagerly follows stronger personalities, content to let others take the risks while he plots from the shadows. When battle begins, Jackalman’s instinct is to flee, but his survival depends as much on luck as on wits. His sharp mind, however, makes him a capable swindler and cards player. Cunning is his true weapon, and his sly maneuvering has turned misfortune into opportunity more than once. While his scrawny frame offers little in combat, his agility and keen sense of smell allow him to track foes and strike unexpectedly. On rare occasions, he shows flashes of daring, even challenging Lion-O himself, but such moments are fleeting and often followed by a hasty retreat. Greed pushes Jackalman forward, but fear forever holds him back. His hunger for advantage drives him to treachery, yet his cowardice ensures he seldom reaps its rewards. In the end, Jackalman’s greatest enemy is not the ThunderCats, but his own nature. Jackalman Medium Humanoid (Mu’Tant), Lawful Evil AC 14 Initiative +3 (13) HP 55 (10d8 + 10) Speed 30 ft. STR DEX MOD SAVE MOD SAVE MOD SAVE 13 +1 +1 17 +3 +5 CON 13 +1 +1 INT 15 +2 +4 WIS 15 +2 +2 CHA 18 +4 +4 Skills Acrobatics +5, Deception +6, Perception +4, Survival +4 Gear Battleaxe, Mace Senses Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 14 Languages Common CR 3 (XP 700; PB +2)

#### TRAITS

Lithe Wrestler. When Jackalman is grappling a creature or has the Grappled condition, he can choose to make a DC 15 Dexterity (Acrobatics) check on his turn (no action required). On a success, he takes one object the creature is holding in its hands. Deviously Clever. Jackalman has Advantage on Intelligence, Wisdom, and Charisma saving throws. The first time he holds a complex device or magic item, roll a d6. On a 4 – 6, he understands how it functions. Canine Mu’Tant. Jackalman has Advantage on Wisdom (Perception or Survival) checks made to track or locate other creatures by smell.

#### ACTIONS

Multiattack. Jackalman makes two attacks, using Battleaxe, Mace, or Arm Cannon in any combination. Battleaxe. Melee Attack Roll: +5, reach 5 ft. Hit: 8 (1d10 + 3) Slashing damage. Mace. Melee Attack Roll: +5, reach 5 ft. Hit: 6 (1d6 + 3) Bludgeoning damage. Arm Cannon. Ranged Attack Roll: +5, range 60/120 ft. Hit: 10 (2d6 + 3) Piercing damage.

#### BONUS ACTIONS

Quick Grapple. Dexterity Saving Throw: DC 13, one Medium or smaller creature Jackalman can see within 5 feet. Failure: The target has the Grappled condition (escape DC 13). 199

#### MONKIAN

Monkian, born Addicus, is a bloodthirsty barbarian whose cruelty rivals only Slithe. Raised in the small, weed-choked town of Sump, on the edge of the Slime Forest of Smutch, he rejected his family’s Mildew Harvesting trade, learning instead to swing and climb from an early age. This was a necessity, since Monkian despises getting any part of himself wet. His combination of raw strength and simian grace, paired with the uncanny ability to pick three pockets at once while balancing on one foot, earned him renown among his mu’tant lineage. He is unmatched when it comes to travelling quickly through the trees. He can cover vast distances in the blink of an eye, swinging and leaping through dense foliage with ease. These skills make him Slithe’s preferred scout, capable of silently moving through forests to eavesdrop on the ThunderCats or strike at unsuspecting foes. In combat, Monkian is a formidable force. Muscular and swift, he can often fell an opponent with a single blow. His opposable toes allow him to wield his feet as a second pair of hands, adding versatility to his attacks. Yet, for all his power, Monkian is not known for brains. He follows Slithe’s orders blindly, acting on instinct rather than strategy, which makes him susceptible to manipulation by clever adversaries. Monkian Medium Humanoid (Mu’Tant), Neutral Evil AC 16 Initiative +5 (15) HP 65 (10d8 + 20) Speed 30 ft., Climb 25 ft. STR DEX MOD SAVE MOD SAVE MOD SAVE 16 +3 +3 16 +3 +5 CON 15 +2 +2 INT 8 -1 -1 WIS 10 +0 +0 CHA 8 -1 -1 Skills Perception +4, Sleight of Hand +7, Stealth +7 Gear Flame Thrower Senses Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 14 Languages Common CR 3 (XP 700; PB +2)

#### TRAITS

Reconnaissance Expert. While in forests or obscured by vegetation, Monkian’s steps make no noise and he has Advantage on Dexterity (Stealth) and Wisdom (Perception) checks. Primate Mu’Tant. Monkian has Advantage on Strength (Athletics) checks.

#### ACTIONS

Multiattack. Monkian makes two attacks, using Chained Mace or Shield Cannon in any combination. He can replace one attack with a use of Mu’Tant Flame Thrower. Chained Mace. Melee Attack Roll: +5, reach 10 ft. Hit: 8 (1d10 + 3) Bludgeoning damage. Shield Cannon. Ranged Attack Roll: +5, range 60/120 ft. Hit: 12 (2d8 + 3) Bludgeoning damage. Mu’Tant Flame Thrower (Recharge 4 – 6). Dexterity Saving Throw: DC 13, each creature in a 30-foot-long, 5-foot-wide Line. Failure: 14 (4d6) Fire damage. Success: Half damage. Failure or Success: Objects in the Line that aren’t being worn or carried take 24 Fire damage and start burning if they are flammable. 200

#### RATAR-O

A direct descendant of the great Plun-Darran warlord Ratilla the Terrible, Ratar-O is one of the smartest and fiercest of all the mu’tants. He was the Plun-Darr warlord previous to the reptilian mu’tant Slithe. During his rule, Slithe worked in the Field Kitchens, learning the inner workings of Ratar-O’s military

#### -O

ThunderCats, Ratar-O grew disillusioned with endless war. He abandoned Plun-Darr entirely, setting his sights on new worlds to conquer among the stars. In his absence, Slithe seized the chance to rise, claiming command and filling the void his former master left behind. Ratar-O is conniving, selfish, and quick to betray allies if it benefits him. Arrogant and overconfident in his skills, he often lets his pride dictate his actions, which frequently leads to his downfall. He has a notoriously short temper, especially toward the incompetence of other mu’tants, and relies almost entirely on his weapons and vehicles, retreating when forced to fight without them. His weapons of choice are the Rat’s Eyes, powerful telekinetic sais with large eyes in the hilt capable of firing energy blasts, and he commands the RatStar, a ship capable of deep space travel. When the first calls for mu’tant reinforcements came in from Third Earth, Ratar-O answered and took command of their forces. Despite his position, he was widely despised by the mu’tants, with Slithe in particular loathing him, though Vultureman remained exceptionally loyal. Following defeat at the hands of the ThunderCats, Ratar-O abandoned them to act independently once more. Later, persuaded by Vultureman to strike again during the ThunderCats’ search for Thunderian refugees, he went missing in action, leaving the RatStar for the mu’tants of Third Earth to claim. Ratar-O Medium Humanoid (Mu’Tant), Neutral Evil AC 16 Initiative +7 (17) HP 99 (18d8 + 18) Speed 30 ft., Fly 20 ft. STR DEX MOD SAVE MOD SAVE MOD SAVE 13 +1 +1 18 +4 +7 CON 13 +1 +1 INT 20 +5 +8 WIS 15 +2 +2 CHA 15 +2 +2 Skills Arcana +8, History +8, Insight +5, Perception +5, Survival +5 Gear Ratar-O’s Rat’s Eyes Senses Blindsight 10 ft., Darkvision 120 ft., Passive Perception 15 Languages Common CR 6 (XP 2,300; PB +3)

#### TRAITS

Master of the Rat’s Eyes. Ratar-O has Advantage on Constitution saving throws made to maintain Concentration on Telekinesis spells he has cast. In addition, whenever Ratar-O would exert his will on one creature or object using the Telekinesis spell, he can choose to do so to two creatures or objects.

#### ACTIONS

Multiattack. Ratar-O makes three attacks, using Rat’s Eye Sai or Energy Beam in any combination. Rat’s Eye Sai. Melee Attack Roll: +9, reach 5 ft. Hit: 11 (2d4 + 6) Piercing damage plus 3 (1d6) Force damage. If the target is a Medium or smaller creature, it levitates up to 20 feet straight into the air until the end of its next turn. Creatures without a Fly Speed have Disadvantage on attack rolls while levitating in this way. Energy Beam. Ranged Attack Roll: +7, range 90 ft. Hit: 18 (4d8) Force damage.

#### BONUS ACTIONS

Telekinetic Grasp. While holding both his sais, Ratar-O casts Telekinesis, requiring no Verbal components and using Intelligence as the spellcasting ability (spell save DC 18), or takes the Magic action of a Telekinesis spell he is concentrating on.

#### REACTIONS

Telekinetic Deflection. Trigger: Ratar-O takes damage from a ranged attack roll. Response: Ratar-O instead takes no damage. Until the start of his next turn, Ratar-O has a +5 bonus to AC against ranged attack rolls. This bonus decreases to +2 if he drops one of his sai, and the bonus ends entirely if he drops both of his sai. 201

#### SLITHE

In the Mountains of Misery, deep within the Brood-Nest of Ri-Kaxtr the Unspeakable, ten reptilian eggs were laid beneath the sickly green skies of Plun-Darr. Only one would survive the hatching. That lone hatchling was Slithe. From his earliest days, he showed a ruthlessness and an instinct for survival that would one day define his rule. At the Plun-Darr Military Academy, Slithe rose swiftly through the ranks, earning top honors and drawing the wary attention of Warlord Ratar-O himself. Recognizing both the young mu’tant’s brilliance and his potential threat, Ratar-O assigned him to the Field Kitchens in an effort to humble him. Yet Slithe’s ambition only deepened. While serving there, he studied Ratar-O’s military operations in secret, learning how wars were fought, lost, and won. When Ratar-O grew disillusioned and left Plun-Darr to seek conquest among the stars, Slithe seized the opportunity. Armed with his hard-won knowledge and relentless drive, he rallied the fractured mu’tant forces under his banner. His strength, cunning, and fearlessness earned him respect among all lineages, and upon enduring the Nine Torments of Plixitl, the ancient rite that crowns a mu’tant warlord, his leadership became unquestioned. Under Slithe’s command, the mu’tants launched brutal campaigns against Thundera. His most infamous act came during Thundera’s final days, when he struck at the fleeing ThunderCats, seeking the power of the Eye of Thundera for himself. Though Plun-Darr was left in ruins in the aftermath of Thundera’s destruction, Slithe abandoned his homeland to pursue his prey across the stars. Now, upon Third Earth, Slithe rules the surviving mu’tants with the same cruel cunning that lifted him from the BroodNest to warlord. Ruthless, domineering, and utterly merciless, he commands with an iron fist, untroubled by loyalty or conscience. Slithe will sacrifice allies without hesitation, manipulate enemies without remorse, and employ any deceitful or dishonorable means necessary to achieve his ambitions. His arrogance often clouds his judgment, turning triumph into failure when overconfidence outweighs caution. Though fearless in the hunt, Slithe’s courage bends to his instinct for survival, and he has no shame in fleeing a losing battle. To him, retreat is not defeat but strategy, for every escape grants him time to plot the vengeance he believes inevitable. 202 Slithe Medium Humanoid (Mu’Tant), Neutral Evil AC 17 Initiative +5 (15) HP 102 (12d8 + 48) Speed 30 ft. STR DEX MOD SAVE MOD SAVE MOD SAVE 20 +5 +8 14 +2 +2 CON 18 +4 +7 INT 17 +3 +3 WIS 13 +1 +1 CHA 16 +3 +3 Skills Athletics +8, Survival +4 Gear Battleaxe, Warp Gas Grenade (2) Senses Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 11 Languages Common CR 5 (XP 1,800; PB +3)

#### TRAITS

Viciousness. Slithe has Advantage on attack rolls against Bloodied creatures.

#### ACTIONS

Multiattack. Slithe makes one Tail Whip attack. He also makes two attacks, using Battleaxe or Thundrainium Gun in any combination. He can replace one attack with a use of one of his Warp Gas Grenades (see chapter 3). Tail Whip. Melee Attack Roll: +8, reach 10 ft. Hit: 10 (2d4 + 5) Bludgeoning damage. If the target is a Large or smaller creature, it has the Prone condition and is subjected to the following effect. Constitution Saving Throw: DC 16. Failure: The target has the Stunned condition until the end of Slithe’s next turn. Battleaxe. Melee Attack Roll: +8, reach 5 ft. Hit: 16 (2d10 + 5) Slashing damage. Thundrainium Gun. Ranged Attack Roll: +5, range 60/120 ft. Hit: 10 (2d6 + 3) Bludgeoning damage, plus 17 (5d6) Necrotic damage if the target is a Thunderian.

#### REACTIONS

Double-Crossing Defense. Trigger: Slithe is hit by an attack roll while an ally is within 5 feet of him. Response: Slithe swaps spaces with that ally, and the ally is hit by the attack roll instead.

203

#### VULTUREMAN AND WOLFRAT

Vultureman, born Vultaire, is the most intelligent of the mu’tants on Third Earth, though his brilliance is often clouded by arrogance. A cultured and calculating figure, he relies on his sharp mind rather than physical strength, leaving him as vulnerable as a hatchling without his inventions. Deep within Castle Plun-Darr, he labors in a laboratory brimming with strange devices and experimental weaponry. There, he creates gadgets, weapons, and even potions, often arming the mu’tants with clever tools to challenge the ThunderCats. His ingenuity extends to the skies, where he pilots his birdshaped Flying Machine with ace precision. Vultureman’s logical approach often puts him at odds with his fellow mu’tants, who favor brute force over intellect. Repeated clashes with Slithe are also common, particularly when Slithe blames Vultureman’s inventions for his own failures. These tensions have honed Vultureman’s treacherous tendencies, making him willing to ally with anyone whose ambitions serve his own, making him unpredictable and dangerous. At times, mental instability and moments of vindictive hatred sometimes drive him to reckless mistakes. Even so, his creativity, intellect, and mastery of technology make him a formidable adversary when he has access to his arsenal of devices. Vultureman Medium Humanoid (Mu’Tant), Lawful Evil AC 14 Initiative +5 (15) HP 55 (10d8 + 10) Speed 30 ft. STR DEX MOD SAVE MOD SAVE MOD SAVE 10 +0 +0 17 +3 +5 CON 13 +1 +1 INT 18 +4 +6 WIS 16 +3 +3 CHA 13 +1 +1 Skills Perception +6, Survival +6 Gear Tinker’s Tools Senses Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 16 Languages Common CR 3 (XP 700; PB +2)

#### TRAITS

Mu’Tant Inventor. A vehicle created by Vultureman increases its Maximum Charges and Component Slots by 2. In addition, he automatically succeeds on any ability check related to the use of Thundrillium Devices (see chapter 3), and at the start of combat roll 1d6. On a roll of 1 or 6, Vultureman has (at GM discretion) a ranged Thundrillium Device on hand. He can make attack rolls using the Thundrillum Device in place of any attack in his Multiattack (see below). Tinkering Expertise. Vultureman has Expertise in Tinker’s Tools when they are used to create or repair any type or complex device, vehicle, or gadget. Avian Mu’Tant. Vultureman has Advantage on Wisdom (Perception) checks that rely on sight.

Multiattack. Vultureman makes two attacks, using Beak or Sonic Gun in any combination. Beak. Melee Attack Roll: +5, reach 5 ft. Hit: 7 (1d8 + 3) Piercing damage. Sonic Gun. Ranged Attack Roll: +6, range 90 ft. Hit: 15 (2d10 + 4) Thunder damage. If the target is a creature, it has the Deafened condition until the end of Vultureman’s next turn.

#### BONUS ACTIONS

Deploy Wolfrat (1/Day). Vultureman summons Wolfrat in an unoccupied space within 10 feet of him and can choose to immediately have it move up to its Speed without provoking Opportunity Attacks. Wolfrat rolls its own Initiative and acts on its own turn. 204

### Berserkers

This band of cybernetic pirates plundered the seas of Third Earth under the banner of Hammerhand, choosing a life of violence and conflict, which soon had everyone referring to them by a fitting moniker: the Berserkers. In their own time, generations before Lion-O and the others came to Third Earth, the original Berserkers and their ship were actually lost off the northern shores of the Unicorn Forest, but the powerful magics of Mumm-Ra and the Ancient Spirits of Evil returned Hammerhand (and his mighty, marauding ship) to the living world to serve their ill desires. Since their return, Hammerhand has replenished his crew with new, cybernetically-enhanced warriors, and they have resumed their marauding mercenary ways at top speed. From kidnapping Mumm-Ra’s enemies, pillaging the Ro-Bear berbils of their natural resources, and laying claim to technologies that appeared on Third Earth, the Berserkers are all too happy to fill their coffers and hold with ill gotten gains. They are true mercenaries, working for whomever can pay their wage, but also getting a requisite thrill from the pirate’s life at the same time.

#### CRUNCHER

The only Berserker that is stronger than Hammerhand’s augmented arm, Cruncher is the ship’s muscle and chief laborer. If it needs pushed, pulled, hefted, or tossed, Cruncher can be counted on to get the job done. He has demonstrated intense and seemingly limitless physical strength, able to crush, or rather crunch, anything unfortunate enough to get caught between his mighty hands. Cruncher Large Humanoid (Cyborg), Neutral Evil AC 18 Initiative +0 (10) HP 85 (10d10 + 30) Speed 30 ft. #### TRAITS

Aye, Aye, Cap’n. Cruncher is always focused on fulfilling orders given to him by the Berserker captain, Hammerhand. If Cruncher is within 30 feet of Hammerhand, he may reroll a D20 Test result of a 1 on the d20, once per turn. He must use the new roll. Unbreakable Grip. Cruncher has Advantage on Strength checks and Strength saving throws.

#### ACTIONS

Multiattack. Cruncher makes two Iron Knuckles attacks. Iron Knuckles. Melee Attack Roll: +9, reach 5 ft. Hit: 10 (1d6 + 7) Bludgeoning damage. Two-Handed Grab. Melee Attack Roll: +9, reach 5 ft. Hit: 12 (2d4 + 7) Bludgeoning damage. If the target is a Huge or smaller creature or object, it has the Grappled condition (escape DC 22). This attack can’t be used if a target is currently Grappled by Cruncher.

#### BONUS ACTIONS

Crunch! Cruncher deals 21 (6d6) Force damage on any object or creature he is grappling. 205

#### RAM-BAM

The newest of Hammerhand’s Berserkers, Ram-Bam is an avarice-driven pirate that literally throws himself at his enemies. His head and neck have been cybernetically augmented to withstand tremendous impacts brought upon

#### -B

belly wheel! Diving forward and blasting toward a target is his most common tactic, un-ironically ramming into something as a living battering ram. No matter if it is an enemy of Hammerhand, a contracted target, or just an obstacle in the way of his next pay day, Ram-Bam doesn’t let anything get in the way on his road to gold and riches.

#### TOP-SPINNER

Even Captain Hammerhand has a hard time keeping tabs on this whirling storm of cybernetics, as Top-Spinner is the kind of anarchic fighter that nothing nearby is truly safe. Altered to be able to revolve at great speeds and with significant force, his role on the Berserkers is generally to press the assault, knocking foes down and away for the other Berserkers to sweep in and finish them off. Top-Spinner hasn’t been a Berserker for too long, but in that time he has proven his loyalty to wealth is second only to his loyalty to Hammerhand himself. Top-Spinner

#### HAMMERHAND

Medium Humanoid (Cyborg), Chaotic Evil AC 17 Initiative +3 (13) HP 52 (8d8 + 16)

#### -S

STR DEX MOD SAVE MOD SAVE MOD SAVE 16 +3 +3 16 +3 +5 CON 15 +2 +4 INT 12 +1 +1 WIS 10 +0 +0 CHA 16 +3 +3 Skills Athletics +5, Acrobatics +5, Perception +2 Senses Passive Perception 12 Languages Common, Thieves’ Cant CR 3 (XP 700; PB +2)

#### TRAITS

Aye, Aye, Cap’n. Top-Spinner is always focused on fulfilling orders given to him by the Berserker captain, Hammerhand. If Top-Spinner is within 30 feet of Hammerhand, he may reroll a D20 Test result of a 1 on the d20, once per turn. He must use the new roll. Spinning. At the start of Top-Spinner’s turn, he can choose to start or cease spinning. While spinning, his first attack roll each turn has Advantage and he has Disadvantage on ability checks. In addition, he gains access to his Tornado of Blows action (see below). Top-Spinner also ceases spinning if he has the Prone condition or when his Speed is reduced to 0. Captain of the Berserkers, Hammerhand is a ruthless mercenary and pirate captain that has plagued Third Earth for multiple generations. Hammerhand and his pirates have been known to raid settlements and stragglers up and down the northern coasts, greedily claiming loot of all kinds. If he could wrap his iron fingers around it, he would happily make it his. The Berserkers sell their services to the highest bidder, but Hammerhand prefers to take on bounties that are not penned in by moral obstacles or a need for restraint. He is not subtle, and expects all of those who crew for him to follow suit. After the sinking and sorcerous return of the Berserker pirate galleon and its hard-hearted captain, Hammerhand underwent significant cybernetic enhancements to overcome what only he believed were exploitable weaknesses. His metallic arm and fist was upgraded to a bulkier, but stronger model complete with a chain winch so it could be launched beyond normal melee range. One of his legs became an armored anchor of sorts, and his cannoneering eye was replaced with a scanning lens that reveals his foes at great ranges. The new and improved Hammerhand, along with his Berserkers, are a fearsome threat on and off Third Earth’s seas, as he has known what awaits in the afterlife already, and is not soon going to go back!

#### ACTIONS

Multiattack. Top-Spinner makes one Flail attack and one Shield Bash attack. Flail. Melee Attack Roll: +5, reach 5 ft. Hit: 7 (1d8 + 3) Bludgeoning damage and the target has Disadvantage on its next attack roll before the start of Top-Spinner’s next turn. Shield Bash. Melee Attack Roll: +5, reach 5 ft. Hit: 6 (1d6 + 3) Bludgeoning damage and target has its Speed reduced by 10 feet until the end of its next turn. Tornado of Blows (Recharge 6; Spinning Only). Top-Spinner moves up to his Speed, and makes two Flail attacks and two Shield Bash attacks at any point during this movement. Opportunity Attacks targeting him during this movement have Disadvantage.

#### BONUS ACTIONS

Keep It Going! Top-Spinner makes a DC 15 Constitution saving throw. On a failed save, he has the Prone condition, and has the Pacified condition until the end of his turn. On a successful save, he recharges his Tornado of Blows action. 207
