If he does so, it deals an extra 9 (2d8) Fire damage plus 9 (2d8) Lightning damage.

#### BONUS ACTIONS

Fly the Colors! (1/Short Rest). A single cyborg or Berserker Crew within 60 feet of Hammerhand has the Empowered condition for 1 minute. 208 Few places in the known universe are as dreadful as the planet Plun-Darr, except its nigh-inhospitable moons. For this reason, many dangerous evil-doers who fought magical guardians of good were exiled there for their crimes. For generations, this served as an effective punishment. Over time, however, the exiles learned to survive in these harsh environments and turn the corrupting influence of the moons into benefits. A few resourceful ones eventually discovered ways to communicate across the moons, using mu’tant technology scavenged from failed Plun-Darran explorations or commandeered ships. Through these connections, they formed an alliance and called themselves the Lunataks. The Lunataks quickly rose in power, gaining a fearsome reputation on Plun-Darr as they raided mu’tant settlements for resources, knowledge, and weapons. But the planet’s meager offerings could not satisfy their ambitions. Seeking greater conquest, they looked beyond Plun-Darr. Their leader, Luna, was the granddaughter of the notorious Queen Luna, a witch who had terrorized Third Earth shortly after its transition from Second Earth. Guided by her grandmother’s tales, Luna led the Lunataks to Third Earth, intent on finishing what Queen Luna had started. Upon arrival, they discovered the planet already ruled by the devil-priest Mumm-Ra. The battle that followed was titanic, but ultimately, Mumm-Ra trapped the Lunataks in molten lava in the region now known as the Dark Side, marking it as forbidden. Years passed, and Mumm-Ra’s reign faded into history. Eventually, frustrated by the mu’tants’ repeated failures against the ThunderCats, the reawakened Mumm-Ra himself ordered the Lunataks freed. Persuaded to join forces against the ThunderCats with promises of splitting Third Earth, the Lunataks rose once more. They established their base, Sky-Tomb, and launched a relentless campaign against the ThunderCats.

#### ALLURO

From the fifth moon of Plun-Darr, Alluro is the psionic powerhouse of the Lunataks, feared for his unmatched control over minds. Towering and imposing, he speaks with a voice that is both sinister and oddly soothing, drawing others under his influence. As the Lunataks’ master of manipulation, Alluro wields his psychic club and its crystal ball capable of inducing trances, conjuring false vision, and bending wills. Though physically formidable, Alluro rarely engages in direct combat. When his mental strategies fail, he prefers to retreat rather than risk hand-to-hand confrontation, relying on his intellect and psychic prowess to maintain the advantage.

Failure: The attack roll misses and the target is affected by the Hypnotic Pattern spell until the end of Alluro’s next turn. From the Third Moon of Plun-Darr, Amok is a hulking, quadrupedal brute who seems to be a cross of bulldog and demon, with only a limited capacity for vocalization. He serves as both the bodyguard and transportation for Lunatak leader Luna. She is rarely seen without him, perched atop his head while gripping his horns for support. Massive and powerful, Amok is the muscle of the group. He often walks on his fists like an ape and is strong enough to carry people or objects while standing on his hind legs. His incredible strength even allows him to punch through walls or subdue enemies with a bear hug. Despite being treated harshly by Luna at times, Amok is fiercely loyal. He will defend her against any threat, even the other Lunataks, and his presence is a key reason she maintains her position as their leader. Amok relies on brute force over brains. His intellect is feral and animalistic, meaning he can easily be duped by an opponent. He also harbors a surprising weakness for candy, a quirk the ThunderCats have occasionally exploited to distract him.

#### CHILLA

Chilla hails from the frozen Second Moon of Plun-Darr, a desolate world of perpetual frost. Shaped by its unforgiving climate, she wields mastery over ice and cold, capable of freezing anything in an instant with her breath. Paradoxically, however, she burns with a fiery temper, and can unleash searing heat beams from her eyes and hands when provoked. She has little regard for life, and she often wields her powers out of spite rather than necessity. Chilla relies on the agility of her slender body and cunning as much as sheer elemental force. She strikes with precision and moves with purpose, her confidence bordering on arrogance. Fearless and calculating, she meets every battle as if she cannot lose, an attitude that often puts her at odds with her leader, Luna, and anyone else who dares to command her. Despite her formidable power, Chilla is not without weaknesses. Her body is bound to the essence of ice, leaving her highly vulnerable to heat and especially to rock salt, which causes her to melt upon contact. Her icy breath, though devastating, can only be sustained for short bursts, forcing her to rely on sharp timing and tactical precision. She carries no weapon but her own body and elemental powers. She also pilots the Ice Runner, a swift flying vehicle capable of rapid flight and even interstellar travel. Though part of the Lunataks, Chilla is largely a lone operator. Ambitious and domineering, she often bullies the mu’tants into aiding her schemes against the ThunderCats, seeking to claim victory and glory for herself alone. 209

#### LUNA

From the Third Moon of Plun-Darr, the diminutive and sharpvoiced Luna is far more dangerous than her frail appearance suggests. Though weak and unable to fight or even run, she compensates with razor-sharp intellect and a ruthless streak that keeps her rivals in line. Though she is the leader of the Lunataks, she is cruel and entirely self-serving, with no loyalty to anyone, not even her own kind. Her leadership is built not on respect or charisma, but on manipulation, strategy, and her loyal bodyguard and steed, Amok. With his brute strength and unquestioned devotion, Amok serves as both her weapon and her means of travel, protecting her from danger and acting as the muscle behind her cruelty. Unlike the rest of the Lunataks, Luna possesses no magical powers and carries no true weapons beyond her riding crop. Yet her lack of physical might is overshadowed by her calculating mind, which remains her greatest and only true strength. Small in stature but vast in ambition, Luna proves that power does not always come from might, but from the mind that wields it. #### RED-EYE

From the Fourth Moon of Plun-Darr, a world shrouded in endless darkness, Red-Eye possesses extraordinary sight. His enhanced vision allows him to perceive heat signatures and detect invisibility, making him an invaluable scout among the Lunataks. Red-Eye also serves as both engineer and pilot aboard the Sky-Tomb, the Lunataks’ fearsome flying fortress. Fiercely loyal to Luna, he fights with a weapon known as the Sidewinder, a spinning discus that attaches to his chest plate. Beyond reconnaissance, he serves as engineer and pilot aboard the Sky-Tomb, their fearsome flying fortress. Fiercely loyal to Luna, Red-Eye wields the Sidewinder, a spinning discus that locks onto his chest plate and channels lethal electrical beams when mounted. 211

14 (4d6) Lightning damage. Multiattack. Tug-Mug makes two attacks, using Slam or Gravity Carbine in any combination. TUG-MUG Slam. Melee Attack Roll: +11, reach 5 ft. Arguably the most powerful of the Lunataks, Tug-Mug comes from the First Moon of Plun-Darr, where gravity is several times stronger than on Third Earth. This environment forged his compact, rounded physique and immense physical power. His three metal stumps, ending in smooth spheres, allow him to roll swiftly across the ground or launch himself like a living projectile, leaping vast distances and striking with unstoppable momentum. Once he begins moving, few can stand in his way. He is also very strong physically, and is known to have effortlessly snapped the Sword of Omens in two with his bare hands. In battle, Tug-Mug wields the formidable Gravity Carbine, a cannon-like weapon capable of altering an enemy’s weight at will. With a single blast, he can render a foe weightless and helpless in the air, or crush them beneath their own impossible mass. Gravity Carbine. Ranged Attack Roll: +6, range 120 ft. Hit: 17 (4d6 + 3) Force damage. If the target is a creature, its weight is either decreased or increased by a factor of ten (Tug-Mug’s choice). If its weight is decreased, the target levitates up to 30 feet straight into the air until the end of Tug-Mug’s next turn. Creatures without a Fly Speed have Disadvantage on attack rolls while levitating in this way. If its weight is increased, the target takes an extra 3 (1d6) Force damage and has the Restrained condition until the end of Tug-Mug’s next turn. If Tug-Mug hits a target with this attack that already has its weight decreased or increased, he can choose to extend its effects until the end of his next turn; a target with its weight decreased continues to levitate at the same altitude, and a target with its weight increased continues to have the Restrained condition.

### Other Allies and

### Enemies (NPCs)

The ThunderCats are not the only champions of good in the universe, just as Mumm-Ra, the Mutants, and the Lunataks are not the only agents of evil. Many have encountered them in conflict or alliance, leaving legacies both fleeting and everlasting.

### Amortus

Hidden behind a shimmering energy barrier lies the Land of No Return, a forbidden region of New Thundera sealed away for generations. It is a barren wasteland where no life can endure, a place reserved for the most dreadful of Thunderians’ enemies. Here, the dark being known as Amortus was cast into exile long ago. Amortus dwells within a monstrous mountain whose jagged maw opens like a living thing. Inside its cavernous depths stand the petrified remains of Thunderians who ventured too near, their forms frozen in eternal terror. One touch from Amortus drains the spark of life itself, transforming flesh into lifeless stone. Though imprisoned, Amortus is not powerless. His dark influence seeps through the boundaries of his prison, reaching across New Thundera through the ethereal. From within his cave, he projects a spectral form to mark chosen victims with his touch, leaving behind a faint sigil that links their minds to his will. Those afflicted hear whispers in their dreams and feel a quiet dread they cannot name, unaware that their thoughts are no longer entirely their own. Bound by both ambition and imprisonment, Amortus waits in the shadows of the Land of No Return, ever plotting the day his reach becomes freedom. Amortus can communicate telepathically with the cursed target across any distance, even across planes of existence, or immediately chooses to subject it to the following effect. Constitution Saving Throw: DC 16. Second Failure: The target has the Petrified condition instead of the Restrained condition if it is still Restrained, and the curse ends.

Whispers Through the Mark (Recharge 4 – 6). Amortus casts Suggestion, requiring no spell components and using Intelligence as the spellcasting ability (spell save DC 16). The spell can only be cast on a target cursed by Amortus’ Touch of Amortus and the suggestion is delivered telepathically at any range

Absorbent Retort. Trigger: A creature hits Amortus with an attack roll using a weapon or ammunition. Response— Strength Saving Throw: DC 16, the triggering creature. Failure: The attack’s weapon or ammunition is absorbed into Amortus. If the weapon or ammunition is absorbed, Amortus can then choose a creature he can see within 60 feet of him that isn’t behind Total Cover. That creature must succeed on a DC 16 Dexterity saving throw or take 17 (3d8 + 4) damage as the weapon or ammunition is shot out of Amortus. The damage is the same type dealt by the triggering attack. 213

### Baron Tass

Baron Tass is a shrewd and merciless noble whose fortune survived Thundera’s fall untouched. A master of deceit, he built his wealth by binding others through treacherous bargains and debts that could never be repaid. Apart from being a devious businessman, the baron is also an enchanted amulet capable of releasing destructive beams and commanding his colossal stone guardsmen. These towering sentinels once stood deep within a cavern beneath his Thundera estate, where the Well of Doubt also rested. This estate has survived into New Thundera as well, ready for the baron’s return. In the age of New Thundera, as the ThunderCats strive to build a world founded on fairness and unity, Baron Tass seethes at the loss of his old dominion. Refusing to yield, he schemes to regain influence by spreading the waters of the Well of Doubt among the rebuilding society. If his plan succeeds, doubt will take root in the hearts of the ThunderCats, weakening their resolve until the baron can once again rise to power. Baron Tass Medium Humanoid (Thunderian), Lawful Evil AC 14 Initiative +1(11) HP 68 (8d8 + 32) Speed 30 ft. Tass has a magical amulet that he wears around his neck at all times. While bearing it, he can use his Magical Bolt attack and Awaken the Guardsmen action. The amulet has AC 20, HP 5, and Immunity to all damage that isn’t from the attack roll of a magic weapon. Thunderian Toughness. Tass reduces all sources of nonmagical damage by 2 (minimum of 1). In addition, he has Advantage on saving throws made to avoid or end the Paralyzed, Poisoned, or Stunned conditions.

Awaken the Guardsmen (Requires Magical Amulet; 1/Day). While holding his amulet, Tass magically animates up to two of his guardsmen, massive stone statues that he usually keeps within his estate, within 60 feet of himself. Each guardsman uses the Stone Golem stat block. The guardsmen take their turns immediately after Tass on the same Initiative count, and obey him. A guardsman remains animate for 1 day or until it dies, the amulet is destroyed, or if the amulet is removed from Tass’ person.

### Burnout

Burnout is a sentient criminal robot wanted by the Interplanetary Control Force for a long list of violent offenses. Unstable and aggressive, he attacks anything, or anyone, without hesitation, charging like an enraged bull. With no regard for life, Burnout revels in destruction, taking cruel pleasure in crushing helpless victims beneath his wheels. Once he builds momentum, there’s no stopping him.

### Captain Bragg

In a weird combination of roles, Captain Bragg is possibly the galaxy’s only part time circus train conductor, part time interstellar bounty hunter. With his wise-cracking, genetically modified, corvid sidekick Crownan by his side, or on his shoulder, at all times, Bragg makes a living travelling around this corner of space capturing bounties. Using the enhanced security systems of his space-capable circus train, his captives are put on display behind thundranium-laced unbreakable bars, earning his fair share of coin by paying customers who want to come and see “such dangerous do-badders” before he transports them to more permanent storage in the Way Outback.

### Captain Cracker

A ruthless and creaky robotic renegade, Captain Cracker is one of the most wanted criminals in the galaxy, relentlessly pursued by the Interplanetary Control Force. A notorious space pirate, he scours the stars in search of ships to rob and worlds to plunder. His vessel, the Jolly Rogers, is a massive spacecraft styled like an ancient sailing ship, complete with billowing solar sails and the iconic skull-and-crossbones flag. Its crew is composed entirely of robotic marauders, each loyal to their mechanical captain. With a peg leg, an eye patch, and his talkative parrot companion Polly at his side, Cracker himself fully embraces the image of a classic pirate, speaking in sea-faring slang even as he roams the sea of stars. 216 Burnout Large Construct, Chaotic Evil AC 18 Initiative +4 (14) HP 85 (9d10 + 36) Speed 50 ft. ### Captain Shiner

Captain Shiner left his homeworld of Sarpedon the moment he earned his star-skipper credentials, quickly joining the ranks of the Interplanetary Control Force. There, he assembled a trusted cadre of officers, but ambition and a thirst for freedom soon led him and his crew to leave the ICF and forge their own path as bounty hunters and mercenaries. Mercenary to the core, Shiner is driven by profit above all else, yet he is far from reckless. Sophisticated and sharp, he carries himself with calm, measured intelligence. He is neither inherently good nor evil, but he always demands a steep price for his services. Once a contract is accepted, however, he sees it through with unwavering dedication, earning a great deal of respect from his crew. Shiner commands the Vertus, a massive, heavily armed, and technologically advanced starship capable of hyperspace travel. With it, he can traverse the galaxy from one end of the stars to the other in an instant. Captain Shiner Medium Humanoid, Chaotic Neutral AC 16 Initiative +4 (14) HP 110 (17d8 + 34) Speed 30 ft. STR DEX MOD SAVE MOD SAVE MOD SAVE 15 +2 +2 18 +4 +4 CON 15 +2 +2 INT 17 +3 +3 WIS 14 +2 +2 CHA 17 +3 +3 Skills Insight +8, Investigation +6, Perception +5 Gear Manacles Senses Passive Perception 15 Languages Common CR 5 (XP 1,800; PB +3)

#### TRAITS

Mercenary Discipline. Shiner has Advantage on saving throws made to avoid or end the Frightened and Stunned condition. In addition, being within 5 feet of an enemy doesn’t impose Disadvantage on Shiner’s ranged attack rolls.

#### ACTIONS

Multiattack. Shiner makes three Laser Gun attacks. Laser Gun. Ranged Attack Roll: +7, range 80/150 ft. Hit: 18 (4d6 + 4) Radiant damage.

#### BONUS ACTIONS

Captain’s Orders. Shiner commands one ally who can see or hear him within 60 feet to move up to its Speed or make one attack roll.

#### REACTION

Uncanny Dodge. Trigger: Shiner is hit by an attack roll. Response: Shiner halves the damage (round down) he takes from that attack.

### Charr-Nin

Charr-Nin is a formidable genie, imprisoned within a grand golden harp hidden in an underground pit on Third Earth. Bound to the harp generations ago, he was lost to time, languishing in anticipation of release. Anyone who plays the harp awakens him, compelling him to grant their wishes to the best of his ability. True freedom, however, can only be achieved if a harp player wishes it for him. Driven by this goal, Charr-Nin will go to extraordinary lengths and commit any deed if he is promised his freedom. Should that promise be broken, however, his wrath is absolute, and he will unleash the full force of his magic upon the betrayer. 218

These hills are no mere terrain, however, but the Charr-Nin Large Elemental (Genie), Neutral AC 17 Initiative +6 (16) HP 184 (16d10 + 96) Speed 30 ft., Fly 60 ft. ### Driller

The Driller is a mechanical-yet-sentient lifeform from the depths of Third Earth who seems to have only one goal and drive: to acquire diamonds used by his people to maintain their sharp points. He and the other driller people have transformable lower bodies that come together into an augur-shape that spins blindingly fast. It is up to the Driller as to whether he skips along the ground like a spinning top, or bores into whatever is beneath his ultradense tip. Driller Medium Construct, Neutral AC 18 Initiative +4 (14) HP 119 (14d8 + 56) Speed 30 ft., Burrow 35 ft. ### Grune the Destroyer

Grune, great-grandson of the first Lord of the ThunderCats, served Thundera diligently as one of its champion generals for many successful campaigns. But as years of peace lingered on, something within the Thunderian grew restless and eager for new conquests. A hunger for battle and a thirst for conflict drove Grune to try and claim Thundera with a violent coup. He was thwarted by Jaga and the Sword of Omens, became incarcerated in a crystalline containment cell, and was banished to the void of space. His crystal cell crashed upon Third Earth, freeing him to begin a vicious conquest of whatever crossed his path and earning him the name Grune the Destroyer. Having most of a planet against him, he was defeated and magically sealed away in a timeless tomb, only to be freed by foolish treasure hunters. He soon discovered that the descendants of his nemesis Jaga had come to the planet too, and that he might be able to seek his revenge. Grune Large Undead (Thunderian), Neutral Evil

#### ACTIONS

### Hachiman

### Mad Bubbler

Wielding the Thunder-Cutter, a katana capable of slicing through most materials, Hachiman is the prince of the Samurai Empire on the Planet of the Red Sun and a master swordsman devoted to the code of Bushido. His unwavering sense of honor, quick temper, and occasional overconfidence make him both fiercely determined and susceptible to manipulation. Mumm-Ra exploited these traits when he summoned Hachiman to Third Earth and convinced him that the ThunderCats were evil. After facing Lion-O, Hachiman realized he had been deceived, and from that moment, became a loyal ally of the ThunderCats. Beneath the frozen peak of Hook Mountain lies an abandoned thundrillium mine, whispered about in fearful tones as a place cursed beyond reason. Legends claim that anyone who ventures inside never returns, but few know the truth. The mine is haunted by the Mad Bubbler, an ancient phantom that can materialize and dematerialize at will, and releases iridescent bubbles filled with a mind-warping gas. Those who breathe it fall victim to “mine madness,” a delirious fury that awakens the darkest corners of the soul. Consumed by rage and hatred, the afflicted become fixated on annihilating one another. Hachiman Mad Bubbler Medium Humanoid, Lawful Good Large Monstrosity, Chaotic Evil AC 17 Initiative +5 (15) HP 120 (16d8 + 48) Speed 30 ft. A Good aligned creature has its alignment swap to its Evil alignment equivalent. A Neutral aligned creature has its alignment change to Neutral Evil. While a creature’s alignment is Evil, it treats all of its former allies as enemies, and must attack its former allies each turn if it can. ### Mandora

One of the most decorated Evil-Chasers in the Interplanetary Control Force, Mandora is the ICF’s chief agent in the corner of space that contains Third Earth. She is a no-nonsense incarceration agent whose primary concern at all times is to uphold interplanetary law while tracking down and catching fugitives. Mandora’s introduction to the ThunderCats was rocky at first, with her treating Lion-O as more of an obstruction to justice than an ally. Once he proved to her, though, that the Code of Thundera was not far from the foundation of ICF legal codes, Mandora and the ThunderCats became staunch allies and friends. Her headquarters were once on the Gray Penal Planet, but she spends a lot of time in the near vicinity of Third Earth and later, New Thundera. Mandora Medium Humanoid, Lawful Good Unarmed Strike. Melee Attack Roll: +6, reach 5 ft. Hit: 6 (1d4 + 4) Bludgeoning damage. AC 17 Initiative +4 (14) HP 75 (10d8 + 30) Speed 30 ft. ### Mumm-Rana

The Ever-Good. The Scion of the Opal Pyramid. The Champion of the Ancient Spirits of Goodness. Mumm-Rana has gone by many names over the ages. No matter what the people around her choose to call her, one truth shines brightly: she is the personification of the good to be found in the Ancient Spirits of Third Earth. As their Champion, Mumm-Rana is an undying extension of their will on the material plane. When things are dire, she can even channel their immeasurable power into herself and transform into Mumm-Rana the Ever-Good, a tremendous weapon for goodness. From within her sanctuary of the Opal Pyramid, Mumm-Rana monitors Third Earth and has even been called away by the Ancient Spirits of Good to distant planets and moons to strike out against unbalancing acts of Evil. When the sadistic Queen Luna (grandmother to Luna, leader of the Lunataks) forged her mighty magical belt and threatened to use it to enslave countless innocents, Mumm-Rana was there to thwart her plans and strip her of the powerful artifact. These journeys away from the Opal Pyramid are taxing, and she always must return to her peaceful torpor within after such applications of her power. When she is resting like this, or in times of great malice when the minions of evil are too many and too varied, Mumm-Rana often seeks outsiders to uphold one or more of the tenets of Goodness. It is this need for warriors to battle on Good’s behalf that brought the ThunderCats to her, forging an alliance that would be tested many times over, but her faith in the Ancient Spirits of Good has never faltered. ### Nemex

Nemex is the warden and keeper of all those incarcerated in the Astral Prison, a facility saved for powerful spellcasters and beings able to ignore physical bondage like that used on the Gray Penal Planet. He is a devilish, purple-skinned imp of a being that normally appears as a four-armed, three-eyed elderly thing, but is able to transform into a larger, more physically intimidating version of himself when conflicts arise. Nemex doesn’t care who comes to serve time in the Astral Prison, but personally profits from all those who do. He can drain knowledge and experiences from them, fortifying his own mind with ideas and thoughts from all over existence. Nemex Medium Fiend, Neutral Evil AC 15 Initiative +2 (12) HP 97 (13d8 + 39) Speed 30 ft. ### Plutar

The infamous criminal Plutar hails from the bleak planet Onyx. Known throughout the galaxy for his toxic touch, Plutar’s very hands secrete a lethal poison that withers life itself; trees rot, flowers blacken, and animals perish in his wake. His only goal is to spread death and decay across every world he encounters. So notorious is his devastation that the Interplanetary Control Force has issued the highest possible bounty for his capture.

His might comes not only from his combat skills but also from his ability to generate and hurl fire, living up to the power his name implies. When the Ancient Spirits of Evil face grave peril and their influence over a world is at risk, they summon Pyron. ### Queen Tartara

The infamous monarch of the Crystal Kingdom, Queen Tartara is driven by a desire to own and hoard anything that could be seen as wealth or valuable; the rarer and more coveted it is, the more Tartara wants to add it to her collection. In her rise to power, her ambition and greed only fueled her prideful and cruel nature. She doesn’t see any difference between placing a shiny bauble on a shelf for only her to look upon, and capturing a living, sentient being and putting it behind glass for her sole pleasure. Even her palace guards are forced to blindfold themselves or avert their eyes from her treasures, lest they be punished for infringing upon her selfishness. d6 Result Magic Item 1 Bag of Holding 2 Weapon of Warning (Dagger) 3 Potion of Healing (Greater) 4 Slippers of Spider Climbing 5 Ring of Mind Shielding 6 Wand of Magic Missiles Queen Tartara Medium Humanoid (Human), Neutral Evill AC 13 Initiative +3 (13) HP 44 (8d6 + 8) Speed 25 ft., Climb 25 ft. ### Ro-Bear Bill

Ro-Bear Bill is the unofficial leader of the Third Earth berbils. He serves as both a gentle authority figure within his community and the spokesperson for his people when interacting with the other species of their adopted planet. He lives contentedly in Berbil Village alongside his wife, Ro-Bear Bella, and their son, Ro-Bear Beebo. True to berbil nature, Ro-Bear Bill is friendly, generous, thoughtful, and reliable. Like all berbils, he is clever and hardworking, and he has proven himself a steadfast ally to the ThunderCats whenever they are in need.

### Safari Joe and Mule

Safari Joe is a ruthless intergalactic hunter who roams the galaxies in search of formidable prey. Having already conquered the Big Cats, Sky Cats, and Aqua Cats, he now turns his sights on the ThunderCats, the ultimate challenge. His only companion is Mule, a loyal robotic sidekick, and he pilots a state-of-the-art spaceship outfitted with cutting-edge technology. Joe’s ship can deploy an “electronic blind,” rendering it invisible to detection, while his “Holojector” generates detailed 3D holograms of his prey, allowing him to study their strengths, weaknesses, and habits before a hunt. He never attacks without careful preparation, gathering all possible intelligence to ensure he gains the upper hand. His signature weapon is a versatile gatling gun capable of firing nitro-darts, explosives, holograms, jet-powered shackles, and even flames. He also makes use of thundrainium cages designed specifically to weaken and imprison the ThunderCats, preventing any chance of escape. Despite his skill and firepower, Joe’s dependence on advanced weaponry often feeds his overconfidence. Beneath his bravado lies a streak of cowardice, and his lack of honor means he will betray his own word as a sportsman whenever it serves his interests. MULE Safari Joe’s robotic companion Mule uses the Scout stat block (see Fifth Edition gaming rules), except his creature type is Construct, his size is Small, he has no gear, and the only actions he can take are the Help action or those made to pilot a vehicle. Safari Joe Medium Humanoid, Neutral Evil AC 14 Initiative +2 (12) HP 127 (15d8 + 60) Speed 30 ft. If Safari Joe hits a Friendly creature with an attack roll, the attack is automatically a Critical Hit. In addition, during the first round of each combat, Safari Joe has Advantage on attack rolls against any Beast, Monstrosity, or Thunderian that hasn’t taken a turn. If he hits such a creature during that round, the damage the target takes is doubled.

An object frozen against a surface can be removed by a creature within 5 feet of it by succeeding on a DC 10 Strength check as an action. Alternatively, if Snow Knight touches a 5-foot Cube of ice he can choose to shape it into any crude shape he desires. Snow Knight is a solitary warrior. Once a proud and highly decorated general in the armies of King Hanuman, ruler of the Kingdom of Snowfolk, he has always been a hermit at heart, shy of company and devoted to his own comforts. For the past twenty years, since his honorable discharge, Snow Knight has lived in seclusion in a remote snowy retreat perched high on the slopes of Hook Mountain. His only companion is Snowmeow, a rare breed of wild sabercat from a lost equatorial plateau in Third Earth. Though Snow Knight and Snowmeow are fearsome in battle when called upon, the old general would far prefer the quiet of his home, tending a warm fire and carving intricate bookends. Before settling permanently in New Thundera, Lion-O appointed Snow Knight to the League of Third Earth, a group of trusted ThunderCat allies tasked with protecting the planet and its inhabitants in the ThunderCats’ absence. ### Technopede

The metallic monstrosity known as the Technopede is a feared example of what can be produced by unscrupulous Mechanists and Operators who find themselves with near unlimited resources and a need for an autonomous weapon. The Technopede is an eight-legged construct of immense size, power, and durability. Although it is simple-minded in its programming, its firepower and ability to withstand most damage makes it formidable to the largest and most powerful of weapons. The tuska people have felt the might of the Technopede directly. Tuskania was once made the target of the mechanical titan, laying waste to anything that got in the way of its heavy munitions. This even included the once-thought-impervious ThunderTank, which was brought to a stand still by a single salvo. The Technopede was eventually disabled by the ThunderCats, saving hundreds of tuska, and sending the monstrosity to the bottom of the sea. Its own internal self-repair systems and the dark magics of Mumm-Ra would cause the Technopede to return and be dealt with once more, but even now it might be reassembling itself again… Technopede Gargantuan Construct, Chaotic Evil AC 20 Initiative +7 (17) HP 297 (18d20 + 108) Speed 45 ft. Whenever the Technopede would take Fire, Lightning, Necrotic, Poison, or Psychic damage, it gains 2 (1d4) charges instead. Plasma Salvo (Requires Charges). Ranged Attack Roll: +7 (the Technopede expends 2 charges), range 60/120 ft. Hit: 24 (5d8 + 2) Lightning damage plus 22 (5d8) Fire damage. Immutable Form. The Technopede can’t shape-shift. Twin-Barrelled Thunderer (Requires Charges). Ranged Attack Roll: +7 (the Technopede expends 3 charges), range 200/400 ft. Hit: 27 (5d10) Force damage plus 27 (5d10) Thunder damage. Minimum Safe Distance. The Technopede can’t make ranged attack rolls against targets within 15 feet of it. Targeting Deficiency. At the start of its turn, the Technopede has Disadvantage on ranged attack rolls against targets it has not already attacked this turn. Damage Threshold. The Technopede has a damage threshold of 12.

#### ACTIONS

Multiattack. The Technopede makes two attacks, using Autocannons, Flak Guns, Plasma Salvo, or Twin-Barrelled Thunderer if available, in any combination. It also makes two Assault attacks. The Technopede can replace one ranged attack roll with a use of Barrage Blaster if available. 234

#### BONUS ACTIONS

Autofire Cyclers (Requires Charges). The Technopede can only take this Bonus Action after it has taken its Multiattack this turn. The Technopede expends 2 charges and makes one Autocannons attack. Self-Repair (Requires Charges). The Technopede expends 3 charges and regains 22 (4d8 + 4) Hit Points. Tri-Tanium Payloads (Requires Charges). The Technopede expends 3 charges. Until the end of its turn, its ranged attack rolls and Barrage Blaster action ignore Resistances and damage thresholds.

### Willa

Leader and queen of the warrior maidens, Willa and her people live in the treetops of her forest village, but oversee a great deal of the surrounding areas of Third Earth. Willa’s people have had a long history of battling against the evils that often ravage the lands, and it has forced her to harden somewhat and become distrusting of outsiders. She teaches her people that life should be filled with compassion and care for the natural world, to always keep a place at your table for your allies, and be forever ready to extinguish threats to their peace or safety. Willa’s honest distrust nearly turned her first encounter with Lion-O and the ThunderCats into a vicious battle, but events unfolded that turned the two communities into staunch allies. As a demonstration of how strong this alliance became, Willa even managed to wield the Sword of Omens and trigger the Eye of Thundera in one particularly dire time of need. It is not easy to convince Willa that any outsider is accepted at her table, but once her trust is earned, it is ironclad. Willa Medium Humanoid (Human), Neutral Good AC 19 Initiative +8 (18) HP 112 (15d8 + 45) Speed 35 ft. STR DEX MOD SAVE MOD SAVE MOD SAVE 12 +1 +1 20 +5 +8 CON 16 +3 +6 INT 14 +2 +2 WIS 16 +3 +3 CHA 16 +3 +3 Skills Acrobatics +8, Animal Handling +6, Arcana +5, Nature +5, Perception +6, Stealth +8, Survival +6 Gear Armor, +1 (Leather Armor), Shortbow Senses Passive Perception 16 Languages Common, Sylvan, Wollo CR 5 (XP 1,800; PB +3)

#### TRAITS

Home in the Trees. Willa can move on tree branches, vines, and similar forested terrain with expending extra movement, and has Advantage on Dexterity (Acrobatics and Stealth) checks in such an environment. Queen of the Warrior Maidens (1/Day). During combat, after an ally of Willa reduces an enemy of her to 0 Hit Points, Willa can reroll any die immediately after rolling it during the same combat. She must use the new roll.

#### ACTIONS

Multiattack. Willa makes three Unicorn Mane Bow attacks or two Unarmed Strike attacks. Unarmed Strike. Melee Attack Roll: +8, reach 5 ft. Hit: 7 (1d4 + 5) Bludgeoning damage. Unicorn Mane Bow. Ranged Attack Roll: +9, range 80/320 ft. Hit: 12 (1d6 + 9) Piercing damage and target’s Speed is reduced by 10 feet until the start of Willa’s next turn. Spellcasting. Willa casts one of the following spells, requiring only Verbal components, and using Charisma as her spellcasting ability (spell save DC 14, +6 to hit with spell attacks bonus): 1/Day Each: Acid Arrow, Animal Friendship, Sight Beyond Sight (a spell in chapter 1), Speak With Animals

#### REACTIONS

Sidestep. Trigger: Willa is targeted by an attack roll or spell. Response: Willa moves up to 10 feet away without provoking Opportunity Attacks. If Willa’s final position invalidates the attack roll or spell, she is unaffected by the effect, and any action, Bonus Action, or Reaction used to make the attack roll or cast the spell is wasted. 235

### Astral Moat Monster

### Creatures of Third

### Earth and Beyond

The cataclysmic shifts throughout Third Earth’s history have allowed for all manner of creatures, born of both science and magic. At the same time, the tremendous pressures which built up when New Thundera was reforming gave rise to bizarre and unpredictable mutations. Beyond these worlds also exist an untamed array of beings from distant dimensions and alien planets. The arietta bird is a dazzling creature that often visits the Berbil Village on Third Earth. Its glittering plumage rivals even the most magnificent peacock, and its mesmerizing song helps ripen the berbils’ fruits. Yet its beauty is also its greatest danger, as the bird’s radiant feathers and melodious voice attract numerous evil beings, eager to capture it for their own purposes. Over time, these threats have reduced its numbers, and now only a single

Arietta Bird STR DEX The Astral Moat Monster is a fierce amphibious predator that prowls the waters surrounding the astral jailor Nemex’s prison in the Astral World, striking at any who dare trespass. Its razor-sharp claws allow it to scale the steep, slick prison walls, while rows of jagged teeth make it a deadly sentinel.

### Black Widow Shark

The Black Widow Shark lurks within the River of Despair on Third Earth, a terrifying fusion of shark and spider. With the massive body of a great white and six spindly, spider-like legs, it strikes with deadly precision at anything daring enough to cross its waters. It can launch itself high above the river, jaws snapping with teeth sharp enough to rend bone and metal alike. ### Brutefolk

The Brutefolk are one of the indigenous species of Third Earth. Their bodies combine humanoid forms with features reminiscent of hammer-headed bats and wildebeests, as if assembled from mismatched parts. Though endowed with extraordinary strength and endurance, they are naturally docile. Communication is a struggle for them, as they speak poorly and possess limited intelligence, a vulnerability frequently exploited by the villains of Third Earth. This lack of awareness also makes them highly susceptible to mind-altering substances. Under such influence, the Brutefolk can become fearsome opponents, capable of overwhelming enemies through sheer numbers and raw power. Brutefolk Medium Humanoid, Chaotic Neutral AC 12 Initiative +1 (11) HP 57 (6d8 + 30) Speed 35 ft. ### Firebat

### Fish-Eating Flower

Firebats are fiery, bat-like creatures that dwell in the molten hearts of volcanoes. Composed entirely of living flame, they are among the few beings on Third Earth capable of withstanding the scorching heat of the volcanic core. Firebats typically gather in swarms, fiercely defending their territory and attacking any intruders who dare enter their domain. The fish-eating flower is the generalized term for carnivorous plants that thrive on the bottom of the River of Despair. They are a naturally occurring part of the local ecosystem, making meals out of fish that draw too close to their grasping tendrils. To them however, there is nothing different from a fish or a foolish swimmer! ### Gaw-Rak-Rak

Gaw-Rak-Rak An ancient and powerful denizen of the River of Despair, the Gaw-Rak-Rak is a massive reptilian beast with two heads that preys upon anything that draws too near. The Gaw-Rak-Rak is an apex predator that has an almost supernatural ability to sniff out prey with flicks of its twin tongues, clawed legs surging it forward on land almost as quickly as its powerful tail pushes it through water and mud. Anything not fast, or wise, enough to avoid the Gaw-Rak-Rak is likely to end up a morsel torn apart by one or both of its saber-fanged maws.

### Molefolk

Molefolk are one of Third Earth’s many native species, small in stature and generally timid. They live in winding tunnels of their own making, laboring quietly beneath the surface. Their lives are dominated by the cruel Molemaster, a slightly taller molefolk whose insatiable greed for gold has twisted him into a ruthless despot. With a large whip in hand, he drives his kin ever deeper into the earth, enforcing his harsh rule with unyielding authority. Molefolk Small Monstrosity, Neutral AC 13 Initiative +4 (14) HP 31 (7d6 + 7) Speed 25 ft., Burrow 25 ft. ### Mudhog

Subterranean raiders that live beneath anywhere there is solid ground on Third Earth, Mudhogs live on what they can dig up under and grab from the surface dwellers at night. Their eyes are sharp in darkness, but stymied heavily in bright light, causing them to try and stay underground when Third Earth’s sun is shining. They will even struggle against the daylight in his name. Mudhog Medium Humanoid, Chaotic Neutral AC 14 Initiative +2 (12) HP 39 (6d8 + 12) Speed 30 ft., Burrow 15 ft. They are fearsome warriors, but they are not necessarily cruel or vicious. It is all based on who is directing their collective communities at the time. During their first interactions with the ThunderCats, they followed the orders and instructions given by their crowned monarch, the greedy Mudhog King. After he is vanquished, perhaps they will be ruled by someone less obsessed with stealing riches from above?

### Sea-Quine

Skills Acrobatics +5 Senses Passive Perception 12 Languages – CR 1/2 (XP 100; PB +2) Normally timid animals found in most of Third Earth’s seas and rivers, the Sea-Quines are the primary non-vehicular mode of aquatic transport. ### Tongue-a-Saurus

The Tongue-a-Saurus is a monstrous amphibian lurking in the River of Despair. Its massive claws can tear through anything in its path, and its gaping maw reveals teeth large enough to crush bones. The mere whisper of its name sends the residents of Third Earth fleeing in terror.

### Trollog

This species of canine humanoids have called the hills and lowlands of Third Earth home for millennia. Trollogs are physically strong, driven by the instincts to survive, and do not strive for technological advances. They live in large, packlike communities, hunting and foraging for food where they can find it. This has caused trollog communities to encroach on peaceful agricultural people like the Ro-Bear berbils and the wollos. Trollogs are fierce warriors that can overwhelm their foes with raw ferocity and numbers, despite shying away from high tech tools and weapons. A pack of trollogs can do more with simple spears and blades than some might do with energy shields and plasma pistols. Hit: 19 (2d12 + 6) Piercing damage. If the target is a Large or smaller creature, it has the Grappled condition (escape DC 17), and it has the Restrained condition until the grapple ends.

#### BONUS ACTIONS

Swallow. Strength Saving Throw: DC 17, one Large or smaller creature Grappled by the tongue-a-saurus (it can have up to two creatures swallowed at a time). Failure: The target is swallowed by the tongue-a-saurus, and the Grappled condition ends. A swallowed creature has the Blinded and Restrained conditions, it has Total Cover against attacks and other effects outside the tongue-asaurus, and it takes 10 (3d6) Acid damage at the start of each of the tongueosaurus’ turns. STR DEX MOD SAVE MOD SAVE MOD SAVE 16 +3 +3 14 +2 +2 CON 15 +2 +4 INT 8 -1 -1 WIS 14 +2 +2 CHA 9 -1 -1 Skills Athletics +5, Perception +4, Stealth +4 Gear Longsword, Spear (2) Senses Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 14 Languages Common, Giant CR 1 (XP 200; PB +2)

#### TRAITS

Pack Tactics. The trollog has Advantage on an attack roll against a creature if at least one of the Trollog’s allies is within 5 feet of the creature and the ally doesn’t have the Incapacitated condition. Sure Footed. The trollog has Advantage on saving throws to keep its balance and avoid the Prone condition.

#### ACTIONS

Handcrafted Blade. Melee Attack Roll: +5, reach 5 ft. Hit: 8 (1d8 + 3) Slashing damage. If the attack roll result on the d20 is a 1, the weapon breaks (which removes Longsword from its gear). Handcrafted Spear. Melee or Ranged Attack Roll: +5, reach 5 ft. or range 20/60 ft. Hit: 6 (1d6 + 3) Piercing damage if used for a ranged attack roll, or 7 (1d8 + 3) Piercing damage when used to make a melee attack roll. If the attack roll result on the d20 is a 1, the weapon breaks (which removes one Spear from its gear).

#### BONUS ACTIONS

Bite. Melee Attack Roll: +5, reach 5 ft. Hit: 5 (1d4 + 3) Piercing damage. If the tongue-a-saurus takes 30 damage or more on a single turn from a creature inside it, the tongue-a-saurus must succeed on a DC 15 Constitution saving throw at the end of that turn or regurgitate all swallowed creatures, each of which falls in a space within 5 feet of the tongue-a-saurus and has the Prone condition. If the tongue-a-saurus dies, any swallowed creature no longer has the Restrained condition and can escape from the corpse by using 15 feet of movement, exiting Prone. 245

### Vampire Mermaid

### Winged Water Snake

The River of Despair is ripe with predator species, but none so insidious and vicious as the Vampire Mermaids. While not undead, these aquatic fey-things feed on the blood and life essence of their victims just the same. A Vampire Mermaid uses a mystical song to enchant and lure prey into their waters, then reveal their true form to them. Seeing the creature in its horrifying visage and the possibility of drowning, causes the victim’s blood to become filled with adrenaline, adding spice as well as fuel to the mermaid’s favorite meal! These flying reptiles, drawn to chronomantic energies, live in and around the mystical Geyser of Life on Third Earth. They are fiercely territorial and, as a result, thrive as the sacred location’s natural guardians. They have vicious bites with powerfully debilitating venom; one bite can impose days of exhausting aches and pain. Medium Monstrosity, Neutral Evil AC 15 Initiative +6 (16) HP 44 (8d8 + 8) Speed 5 ft., Swim 45 ft. CR 3 (XP 700; PB +2)

#### TRAITS

Amphibious. The vampire mermaid can breathe air and water. Powerful Swimmer. The vampire mermaid has Advantage on Strength (Athletics) checks while in water. Violent Embrace. A target that is hit by both of the vampire mermaid’s Claw attacks in the same turn has the Grappled condition (escape DC 13).

#### ACTIONS

Multiattack. The vampire mermaid makes two Claw attacks. Claw. Melee Attack Roll: +6, reach 5 ft. Hit: 7 (1d6 + 4) Slashing damage. Leeching Bite. Melee Attack Roll: +6, reach 5 ft. Hit: 21 (6d6) Necrotic damage. The target of this attack must either be Charmed, Grappled, Restrained, or Unconscious. Spellcasting. The vampire mermaid casts one of the following spells, requiring only Verbal components, using Charisma as the spellcasting ability (spell save DC 15): At Will: Alter Self, Command, Minor Illusion 3/Day Each: Charm Person, Dissonant Whispers, Sleep 246 Large Monstrosity, Unaligned AC 13 Initiative +5 (15) HP 52 (8d10 + 8) Speed 30 ft., Fly 60 ft., Swim 30 ft. Amphibious. The winged water snake can breathe air and water. Chronomantic Immunity. The winged water snake doesn’t provoke Opportunity Attacks when it flies out of an enemy’s reach.

Bite. Melee Attack Roll: +5, reach 5 ft. Hit: 10 (2d6 + 3) Piercing damage. If the target is a creature, it is subjected to the following effect. Constitution Saving Throw: DC 13.
