### Wollo

The wollos are a native species to Third Earth, and form some of the most stable naturalist communities and culture across the entire planet. Their farming, fishing, and artisanal communities are scattered throughout the temperate biomes of the planet, thriving wherever they can put down roots. Wollo traditions are bent toward kindness, sharing, and hard work, creating a culture of togetherness and fostering a gentle life. They have long forsaken the ways of higher technology; not because they do not understand it, but rather feel that it takes away from their old-fashioned strength and familial reliance. This can sometimes get them into trouble with raiders or unscrupulous thieves looking to bully the wollo folk into handing over their hard work or surplus goods, but they are not completely defenseless. Each wollo is a short, stout humanoid with perceptive eyes, thin brown and tan fur, and surprising strength from a lifetime of physical labor. They would always rather negotiate with or acquiesce to minor inconveniences whenever possible. When a wollo community is put in harm’s way however, they are more than capable of turning simple tools and perseverance into a worthy defense of their families and friends. Wollo Small Humanoid (Wollo), Typically Lawful Good AC 12 Initiative +1 (11) HP 22 (4d8 + 4) Speed 30 ft. STR DEX MOD SAVE MOD SAVE MOD SAVE 14 +2 +2 12 +1 +1 CON 14 +2 +2 INT 12 +1 +1 WIS 12 +1 +3 CHA 14 +2 +4 Skills Athletics +4, Perception +3, Nature +3 Gear Artisan’s Tools (2; Varies; see The Wollo Way) Senses Passive Perception 13 Languages Common, Wollo CR 1/2 (XP 100; PB +2)

#### TRAITS

Never Give Up. The wollo rerolls saving throw results of 1 on the d20. It must use the new roll. The Wollo Way. The wollo has two random Artisan’s Tools and has Expertise in those two Artisan’s Tools.

#### ACTIONS

Hand Tool. Melee Attack Roll: +4, reach 5 ft. Hit: 5 (1d6 + 2) Bludgeoning, Piercing, or Slashing damage (choose one based on one of the wollo’s Artisan’s Tools).

#### BONUS ACTIONS

Catch My Breath (1/Day). The wollo regains 6 (1d10 + 1) Hit Points. Communal Aid. The wollo takes the Help action. The ally in either effect of the Help action must be a wollo. 247

## Chapter 5: Thundrillium Quest

248

“For years I’ve heard all sorts of tales of what lives in these waters. Of bloodthirsty mermaids, who tempt sailors to the edge of their ships so they can snatch them, drown them, and feast upon their flesh. And of giant squids sent from the hellish depths to fasten themselves to ships so tightly that vessels crumble from the pressure, leaving shards to float upon the foamy sea for eternity. And of cats who come to Third Earth with their exotic diseases and weaponry, who seek to spread their plague, so that the planet can be theirs and theirs alone. Until today, I would have written all of these off as myth.” —Hammerhand, Berserker Captain of the Bedlam This chapter presents an adventure designed for four to six player characters levels 5 to 8. You can adjust for larger or smaller groups by changing the number of foes in encounters and giving a small group additional resources. It is intended as a short, self-contained story in the spirit of a ThunderCats episode, but it can just as easily serve as the start of a larger campaign or fit neatly into an ongoing one. In this story, the characters take the role of heroes who must confront formidable villains and explore a perilous location to see the adventure through to its dramatic conclusion.

### Running the Adventure

To run the adventure, you need the fifth edition core rules. Text that appears in a box like this is meant to be read aloud or paraphrased for the players when their characters first arrive at a location or under a specific circumstance as described in the text. When a creature’s name appears in bold type, that’s a visual cue pointing you to its stat block. If a stat block appears after the adventure in this chapter, or in Chapter 4, the adventure’s text tells you. Spells, equipment, and magic items mentioned in the adventure are described in the Fifth Edition core rules unless otherwise specified.

### Network–Approved

Before starting an adventure with a new group of players, have an open discussion about any topics or themes that should be avoided during gameplay. Players may have phobias or triggers that you aren’t aware of, so it’s important to establish boundaries. Any content that makes a player feel unsafe should be excluded. If a topic or theme makes someone uneasy but they consent to include it, handle it with care and be prepared to move on quickly if it becomes uncomfortable. While characters can experience stress and anxiety, your players should always feel safe and have fun.

### Rerun This Adventure

Think of this adventure like a batch of animation cels. Each scene, character, and plot twist can be lifted, recolored, and dropped into your own campaign world. Swap names, redraw the backgrounds, and mix in your own epic heroics. Grab your editor’s pen and this adventure is fully ready for a rerun in any campaign you can imagine!

### Key Plot Points

The following information is key to this adventure’s story: Mysterious Map. The characters attend a ThunderCats briefing about a recently uncovered Thundrillium Map Facility when Mumm-Ra’s forces launch a sudden attack on a nearby Berbil village. Suspecting the assault is a diversion while other minions infiltrate the facility, Lion-O orders the characters to secure the site while the ThunderCats confront Mumm-Ra. Not long after exploring the facility, the characters realize the mu’tants have already arrived and have seen combat. Rampaging Robot. The ancient facility is sealed behind color-coded keycard locks, forcing the characters to scour its chambers for access. During their search, they come face to face with a haywire robot that appears nearly indestructible. Hunted through the corridors, the characters must decide to stand alone or form an uneasy alliance with the mu’tants. 249

Spritely Seize. Time is running out. While evading the rampaging robot, the characters race to recover the remaining keycards and reach the chamber housing the Thundrillium Map before the mu’tants can claim it for Mumm-Ra. Success brings the final challenge of escaping the facility, and possibly the wrath of Mumm-Ra himself.

### Preparation

Before running the adventure, prepare as follows: Step 1. Review the “Third Earth” section of Chapter 2 and the “Thundrillium” section of Chapter 3. Step 2. Read the “Adventure Background” section. Step 3. Familiarize yourself with the Key NPCs table below. The characters will interact with these individuals throughout the adventure. Step 4. Familiarize yourself with the Weapon Malfunctions Table under “5: Security Chamber”. The characters will likely use items that can suffer these malfunctions during the adventure. Step 5. Bookmark the following Thundrillum Devices from Chapter 3: Plasma Blade Thundrillium Blaster Plasma Cannon Step 6. Bookmark the following stat blocks from Chapter 4: Jackalman Slithe Monkian Vultureman Mumm-Ra The Ever-Living

### Adventure Background

Third Earth locals have recently spoken of a long-lost facility from First Earth, hidden deep within the remote reaches of Third Earth. Within its ancient halls lies a map of thundrillium deposits once charted across First Earth. Though the data is surely outdated, it may still hold information vital to locating new thundrillium deposits across Third Earth today. Before the ThunderCats could begin their investigation of this facility, however, the always watching Mumm-Ra learned of this facility, too. Ever cunning, he devised a scheme to distract the ThunderCats while dispatching the mu’tants to claim the map for themselves. Now, while the ThunderCats are busy, a small team races to the forgotten facility, determined to secure the map before their enemies can seize it, and the power it represents. PLAYING AS THE THUNDERCATS This adventure assumes the player characters are aspiring ThunderCats, or at least not yet members of the core team. If your players prefer to take on the roles of the well-known ThunderCats, you can adjust the story so that the characters not controlled by the players stay behind to confront Mumm-Ra, while the players’ characters are sent on this mission. KEY NPCS Name Role Stat Block Jackalman Mu’tant trying to claim the map Jackalman (see the “Mu’Tants” section of Chapter 4) Control Chamber (area 6) Monkian Mu’tant trying to claim the map Monkian (see the “Mu’Tants” section of Chapter 4) Control Chamber (area 6) Mumm-Ra (Optional) Mu’tants’ patron and final Mumm-Ra The Ever-Living (see the “Mumm-Ra and battle Ma-Mutt” section of Chapter 4) External Ruins (area 1) Slithe Mu’tant leader Slithe (see the “Mu’Tants” section of Chapter 4) Control Chamber (area 6) Vultureman (Optional) Mu’tant trying to claim the map Vultureman (see the “Mu’Tants” section of Chapter 4) Control Chamber (area 6) 250 Location

### The Adventure Begins

The party has been welcomed as friends and allies to the ThunderCats and gathered with the noble Thunderian heroes in the meeting chamber of Cats’ Lair to discuss an important discovery. Once everyone has introduced themselves, read or paraphrase the following: The meeting chamber of Cats’ Lair hums with energy as the ThunderCats gather around a glowing projection table. At the center, an image flickers into view of an ancient, metallic ruin half-buried in the wilds of Third Earth. A wollo traveler discovered the site only days ago. Snarf insists it matches the descriptions of the fabled Thundrillium Map Facility he read about in the Lair’s library, a relic from the age when Third Earth was still First Earth. Legends speak of a vast civilization that once charted every thundrillium vein across the planet. Though those records are surely ancient and outdated, their rediscovery could guide the ThunderCats to new sources of the vital substance. Before plans can be finalized, a distress call blares through the chamber. The Berbil Village is under attack by walking corpses! The timing feels too precise to be coincidence. Panthro’s expression hardens as he voices what everyone suspects. “Mumm-Ra. If that miserable mummy knows of the facility, he will stop at nothing to claim its secrets first.” Lion-O turns to you, his eyes shining with determination. “You have proven yourself allies, and now we ask a great boon of you. While we face Mumm-Ra’s forces, please head to the ruins. Take the Feliner, find the facility, and secure the map before our enemies can seize it!” He raises the Sword of Omens high, its Eye blazing with crimson light. “ThunderCats, Ho!” In a blur of motion, the ThunderCats leap into action, racing to defend their allies. You make your way to the Feliner, its engines roaring to life as it lifts off from Cats’ Lair. The skies of Third Earth stretch before you, your mission clear, but the dangers ahead uncertain. Allow the players to describe how they prepare for the task ahead, roleplay conversations, and/or narrate their brief journey. When finished, they arrive at the First Earth Facility.

### First Earth Facility

The ruins of a strange metallic facility lies twisted and broken, its once sleek walls now fractured and halfburied in the mountain’s rocky surface, broken fragments scattered all across. Embedded within a mountain range at the edge of the known regions lies one of the final relics of First Earth’s advanced civilization: a thundrillium survey facility. Its crew long dead, the technological wonder now rests in silence. Thundrillium Map. The Thundrillium Map has manifested itself inside the facility’s Navigation Chamber (area 8). This chamber was considered highly restricted to the original crew, and is heavily locked down. In order to be accessed, it requires a key card from each of the three crew divisions: Engineering, Science, and Security. Artificial Aggression. The original crew was wiped out a long time ago by a security robot that went haywire, deeming everything but itself an intruder. It was able to gain access to the facility’s mechanisms, releasing a deadly pathogen into the air supply and sending shutdown orders to all other robots. By the time the crew discovered what was happening, it was too late. This rampaging robot still stalks the facility’s desolate corridors, ready to annihilate anything that dares enter the wreckage. 251

Mu’Tants. The mu’tants have already arrived and entered the ruins. They’ve encountered the robot and have barricaded themselves in Control Chamber (area 6), where they are trying to hack into the facility’s mechanisms and override its controls. Once they succeed, they’ll take command of the facility, lock down the characters’ location, and seize control of the robot to help them access the Navigation Chamber and keep the party at bay. If they manage to obtain the Thundrillium Map, they will call for Mumm-Ra to teleport them out. USING THE MU’TANTS TO PACE THE ADVENTURE The mu’tants’ plan introduces a time-sensitive challenge for the players. Use your best judgment to maintain pacing, but if an hour has passed and the players have not yet retrieved the Thundrillium Map, begin enacting the mu’tants’ plan (see area 6). If the players are close to acquiring the Thundrillium Map and can do so within the next few minutes, this can create an exciting, climactic race to the finish. Otherwise, they may fail to obtain the Thundrillium Map altogether. If the mu’tants obtain the Thundrillium Map, they will call out for Mumm-Ra to teleport them out of the facility, but depending on time it may take a few rounds for Mumm-Ra to oblige, creating an intense final battle where the players must give everything they have.

#### AREA INFORMATION

The facility’s interior has the following features. Dimensions & Terrain. The interior of the facility is made of smooth, metal sheets with glass bulbs and panels on them. Unless otherwise noted, the facility’s ceilings are 10 feet high. Lighting. The entire interior of the facility is enveloped in complete darkness. The facility’s fluorescent light panes set into the ceiling are no longer functional. 252 Smells & Sounds. A metallic tang tinges the otherwise foulsmelling stale air. The facility’s interior is eerily silent, broken only by the occasional creak of metal and the sporadic groans reverberating through its structure. Doors. The doors are sleek, rectangular slabs of polished metal that slide into recessed spaces within the walls. Though their original mechanism no longer functions, they can still be manually forced open or closed with some effort as an action. A creature attempting to quickly open or close the door, as they would with a standard door, must succeed on a DC 12 Strength check.

#### 1: EXTERNAL RUINS

Shrapnel and deep fissures litter the outside of the ruins, surrounding a gaping hole torn into the facility’s wall. The interior lies in darkness, its depths quickly swallowed by blackness. Entering into the hole leads to area 2. Not Alone. A character who makes a successful DC 15 Wisdom (Survival) or DC 15 Intelligence (Investigation) check notices humanoid tracks in the area and traces of bog-smelling clothing snagged on pieces of sharp metal. This indicates that the mu’tants have already been here.

#### 2: RUINED OPENING

The opening leads in through the wall of a 10-foot wide corridor that stretches 30 feet in both directions, towards the facility’s west and south, with doors positioned at each end. Rubble. There is a lot of detritus around the opening, making the first 15 feet in either direction Difficult Terrain. Corpse. Just before the south-side door lies the corpse of a terrified mu’tant, arms outstretched toward the opening. A character who examines the body can discern that the death is recent, caused by a perfectly round, scorched hole in its back. If a character uses a Speak With Dead spell or similar magic to communicate with the corpse, the mu’tant recalls little about the attack, only the clanging of metal and the intense heat. They are unaware of the mu’tants’ current plans, as they died before they were made, but they do remember their comrades fleeing toward the door leading to the ship’s south.

Doors. The south-side door leads to area 3, while the westside door leads to area 4. Ruined Tube. Across from the opening is an alcove containing a smashed 10-foot-wide, cylindrical chute. This chute originally allowed for passage between the facility’s levels, but it is currently filled with debris and collapsed, preventing anything from using it. Clearing the debris would take longer than the characters have. Service Ducts. A character with Passive Perception 15 or higher, or who succeeds on a DC 15 Intelligence (Investigation) check, notices small grates at the base of the corridor walls, 15 feet from the opening in either direction. These ducts are sized for Small creatures, as they were intended for small service robots, but a Medium creature could squeeze through it if they are not wearing heavy armor. The ducts extend 30 feet east-side to 3a: South Control Corridor, where a deactivated service robot blocks the way, and 30 feet north-side to area 4a. Moving the robot requires a DC 20 Strength check, but doing so is very noisy and will alert the haywire security robot (see area 3a).

#### 3: ENGINEERING CHAMBER

Several screens and devices populate this 30-foot diameter circular room. Three mu’tant corpses, bearing nearly identical wounds to the one in area 2, lie scattered throughout the area. There are three doors facing west, north, and east respectively, while two smaller storage rooms can be found toward the south. Loud metallic pounding can be heard echoing from the room behind the north-side door. Corpses. The three corpses of mu’tants are transfixed in expressions of terror, killed in the same manner as the body found in area 2. If a character casts Speak With Dead or similar magic, the mu’tants will describe the deadly robot that attacked them and mention that their comrades escaped through the west-side door. However, they are unaware of the mu’tants’ current plans, having died before they were made. They can also share that their brethren managed to grab supplies from the storage rooms before retreating. Doors. The west-side door leads to area 2, while the northside door leads to area 3a, and the east-side door leads to area 3b. Storage Rooms. There are a total of 1d4 energy cells kept within the two storage rooms. A character that succeeds on a DC 12 Intelligence (Investigation) check notices that there was more but they seem to have been recently removed.

#### 3A: SOUTH CONTROL CORRIDOR

A 10-foot wide and 30-foot-long corridor resembling area 2, including the service ducts, but without the opening or ruined tube (see area 2). There are two doors facing south and north respectively. This corridor contains two additional mu’tant corpses like those in area 3, as well as the haywire security robot which is banging on the north-side door. Corpses. The two corpses of mu’tants are badly scorched and damaged, but are otherwise similar to those in area 3. If a character casts Speak With Dead or similar magic, the mu’tants will be able to recount everything the corpses in area 3 can, with the addition of the mu’tants’ current plans. Doors. The south-side door leads to area 3. The north-side door has a violet trim, differentiating it from the others, designating that it is locked and requires a violet key card to open it. This door has been sabotaged by the mu’tants, however, and can’t be opened even with a key card. This lock can’t be bypassed with Thieves’ Tools or a Knock spell either. It is completely jammed shut. Beyond this door lies area 6. Service Ducts. The service ducts extend 30 feet west-side to area 2 where a deactivated service robot blocks the way unless it’s been moved (see area 2), and 30 feet starboard-side to area 3b. Key Card. One of the corpses carries a violet key card. Haywire Security Robot. The Haywire Security Robot (see further in this adventure) has suffered severe degradation of its artificial mind, but remains fixated on its mission to eliminate all that is not itself, which includes the characters. Unfortunately it has long ago lost the protocol for opening locked doors. USING THE ROBOT TO PACE THE ADVENTURE The robot also adds another timekeeping mechanism. Once it detects the characters, it will pursue them relentlessly but at a slow, steady pace. Use your narration, alongside the robot’s stats to emphasize the prolonged and intense nature of the combat. This fight is meant to push the players toward fleeing rather than direct confrontation. You don’t need to track every move the robot makes. It takes about 1 minute for it to cross a corridor (1 and a half minutes in area 2 due to the difficult terrain). If the characters linger too long, describe the distant clanking of the robot’s metallic footsteps growing steadily louder. Have the robot burst through whichever door feels appropriate. It’s also a good idea to reward any clever thinking the players try, to slow the robot down and give them more breathing room. If the characters haven’t stumbled into the robot yet, you should have it show up after the characters get their first key card due to it attempting to find another way into the Control Chamber (area 6). If the players insist on fighting the robot and an hour has elapsed, have the mu’tants take advantage of the distraction. While the party is occupied with the robot, the mu’tants sneak off to retrieve the Thundrillium Map. 253

#### 3B: SOUTH-EAST CORRIDOR

This 10-foot wide and 60-foot long corridor, similar to area 2, features a ruined tube and service ducts (see area 2). However, unlike area 2, there are no openings or corpses. It stretches towards the facility’s south and east, with doors positioned at each end. Doors. The south-side door leads to area 3, while the eastside door leads to area 5. Service Ducts. The service ducts extend 30 feet port-side to area 3a, and 30 feet north-side to area 5a.

#### 4: SCIENCE CHAMBER

This 30-foot diameter circular room is cluttered with metal desks, screens, slabs, and furniture all covered in disks, sheets of metal, and debris. Scattered among the furnishings are several inert robots, long since deactivated. There are three doors facing south, north, and east respectively. A faint green glow emanates from the badly dented east-side door, bathing the room in dim light. Radiation. Radioactive chemicals seep into the room, leaking through the east-side door. A creature that enters the room for the first time on a turn or starts its turn there takes 5 (1d10) Radiant damage and must make a DC 10 Constitution saving throw. On a failed save, the creature suffers 1 level of 254 Exhaustion. While this exhaustion persists, the creature emits a green-hued dim light in a 5-foot radius. This light makes it impossible for the creature to benefit from having the Invisible condition. Constructs are immune to this effect. Key Card. This room is a complete mess, and finding the needed green key card will be difficult to do in a timely fashion. Any character who takes the Study action while in the room and succeeds on a DC 20 Intelligence (Investigation) check will locate a key card. Disabled Robots. The Worker Robots (see further in this adventure) are completely inactive, but a character who takes the Study action and succeeds on a DC 15 Intelligence (Investigation) check notices a slot on each robot that looks like it would fit an energy cell. If an energy cell is inserted into the slot, the robot powers up for 1 minute. During this time, it mindlessly performs ancient, irrelevant tasks. However, one of these tasks involves organization, causing it to locate and pick up the green key card from a desk and placing it on a shelf near the north-side door, allowing the characters to retrieve it without passing through the radiation. If the haywire security robot enters the room while a worker robot is active, it will attempt to fight it in a futile effort to defend the ship. Though the worker robot will ultimately lose, it can provide a temporary distraction. Doors. The south-side door leads to area 2, the east-side door leads to area 4a, and the north-side door leads to area 4b.

#### 4A: WEST CONTROL CORRIDOR

A 10-foot wide and 30-foot-long corridor resembling area 2, including the service ducts, but without the opening, corpse, or ruined tube (see area 2). It has completely collapsed roughly halfway down the corridor, preventing further access. A faint green glow emanates from the debris bathing the corridor in dim light. Clearing the detritus would take more time than the characters have. Doors. The west-side door leads to area 4. Service Ducts. The service ducts extend 30 feet stern-side to area 2, and 30 feet bow-side to area 4b.

Radiation. Radioactive chemicals seep into the room, leaking through the east-side of the collapse. A creature that enters the room for the first time on a turn or starts its turn there takes 5 (1d10) Radiant damage and must make a DC 10 Constitution saving throw. On a failed save, the creature suffers 1 level of Exhaustion. While this exhaustion persists, the creature emits a green-hued dim light in a 5-foot radius. This light makes it impossible for the creature to benefit from having the Invisible condition. Constructs are immune to this effect.

#### 4B: NORTH-WEST CORRIDOR

This 10-foot wide and 60-foot long corridor, similar to area 2, features a ruined tube and service ducts (see area 2). However, unlike area 2, there are no openings or corpses. It stretches towards the facility’s west and north, with doors positioned at each end. Doors. The west-side door leads to area 4, while the northside door leads to area 7. Service Ducts. The service ducts extend 30 feet stern-side to area 4a, and 30 feet starboard-side to area 6a.

#### 5: SECURITY CHAMBER

This 30-foot diameter circular room contains several metallic lockers and weapon racks. There are three doors facing south, north, and west respectively. Doors. The south-side door leads to area 3b, while the north-side door leads to area 5b, and the west-side door leads to area 5a. Energy Cells. There are 1d6 energy cells stored in this room. Key Card. Finding the red key card requires taking the Study action and succeeding on a DC 15 Intelligence (Investigation) check. Weaponry. There are many weapons in the room, but they have degraded over time. By taking the Study action and succeeding on a successful DC 20 Intelligence (Investigation) check, a character can discover a working Plasma Blade (see Chapter 3). Otherwise, only 1d4 Plasma Cannons (see Chapter 3) and 1d4 Thundrillium Blasters (see Chapter 3) are still in relatively decent condition, but each carries a risk of malfunction. The first time one of these weapons is used, roll a d10. On a result of 10, the weapon functions properly. On any other result, roll a d4 or consult the table below to determine its malfunction. WEAPON MALFUNCTIONS d4 Weapon Malfunction 1 The weapon functions normally for 1d6 uses, but then completely shuts down, burning through its energy cell’s remaining charges. 2 The weapon short circuits dealing 11 (3d6) Lightning damage to the creature wielding it. The creature must immediately drop the weapon, or take the same damage at the start of each of its turns. After 1 minute, the weapon will be totally destroyed. 3 Crossed circuitry causes the weapon to behave erratically, firing randomly every 1d10 minutes. 4 The weapon’s energy cell ignites, causing a deadly gas to fill a 10-foot sphere around it. Creatures in the affected area must make a DC 15 Constitution saving throw or have the Poisoned condition for 1 minute. A Poisoned creature can repeat this saving throw at the end of each of its turns.

#### 5A: EAST CONTROL CORRIDOR

A 10-foot wide and 30-foot-long corridor resembling area 2, including the service ducts, but without the opening, corpse, or ruined tube (see area 2). There are two doors facing starboard and port respectively. Doors. The east-side door leads to area 5. The west-side door has a violet trim, differentiating it from the others, designating that it is locked and requires a violet key card to open it. This lock can’t be bypassed with Thieves’ Tools, though a Knock spell or similar magic opens it as normal. To unlock the door, a violet key card must be inserted into a slot on the panel next to the door. The door then unlocks, and the panel ejects the card. Beyond this door lies area 6. Service Ducts. The service ducts extend 30 feet south-side to area 3b, and 30 feet north-side to area 5b.

#### 5B: NORTH-EAST CORRIDOR

This 10-foot wide and 60-foot long corridor, similar to area 2, features a ruined tube and service ducts (see area 2). However, unlike area 2, there are no openings or corpses. It stretches towards the facility’s east and north, with doors positioned at each end. Doors. The east-side door leads to area 5, while the northside door leads to area 7. Service Ducts. The service ducts extend 30 feet south-side to area 5a, and 30 feet north-side to area 6a. 255

#### 6: CONTROL CHAMBER

This 30-foot diameter circular room is where the various systems within the ship are controlled. There is a large 10-foot diameter metal cylinder in the center covered in knobs and switches, with several consoles surrounding it. There is also a map readout of the ship displaying on screens, though it’s labeled in an unknown language. Several mu’tants are intently accessing the consoles. The west-side of the room is collapsed, with the faintest dim green glow behind the detritus. There are three doors facing the north, east, and south respectively. Doors. The north-side door leads to area 7a, while the east-side door leads to area 3b. The south-side door has been sabotaged and cannot be opened (see area 3a). All three doors have a violet trim, differentiating it from the others, designating that they are locked and require a violet key card to open them. These locks can’t be bypassed with Thieves’ Tools, though a Knock spell or similar magic opens them as normal. To unlock a door, a violet key card must be inserted into a slot on the panel next to the door. The door then unlocks, and the panel ejects the card. Mu’Tants. The mu’tants are slowly figuring out and learning how to operate the intricate devices and mechanisms within the chamber. The group is led by Slithe (see the “Mu’Tants” section of Chapter 4), accompanied by Monkian (see the “Mu’Tants” section of Chapter 4) and Jackalman (see the “Mu’Tants” section of Chapter 4). If there are 6 or more characters, Vultureman (see the “Mu’Tants” section of Chapter 4) is there as well. The mu’tants do not engage in combat 256 right away, as Slithe recognizes the skill of the characters and seeks to avoid unnecessary casualties. Instead, he attempts to manipulate the characters into retrieving the three key cards needed to unlock the door to the Navigation Chamber. He claims this will allow him to neutralize the robot, which threatens both sides, letting them continue their rivalry. However, this is a ruse. His plan is actually to let the characters handle the robot and collect the key cards for them while the mu’tants proceed with their current plan as backup. If the characters entered through the north-side door, however, Slithe assumes they already understand the key cards’ purpose. In this case, he will try to convince them to leave his group alone while secretly plotting to lure the robot into Operations (area 7) as the characters open the door to the Navigation Chamber, so he and the mu’tants can secure the Thundrillium Map before the characters do. If questioned about the mu’tants at the controls or his intentions, Slithe lies, claiming they are protective measures against the haywire security robot. Should tensions escalate, he will warn the characters that, while the mu’tants have yet to fully master the facility’s systems, they have learned how to trigger its self-destruct mechanism. Any sign of hostility toward the mu’tants will provoke this very real danger. Leading the Robot Here. If the characters lure the haywire security robot into the chamber, it will immediately attack the mu’tants alongside the party. The mu’tants are forced to defend themselves, creating a chaotic melee that offers an ideal distraction for the characters to stealthily retrieve the Thundrillium Map, though the mu’tants will likely retreat after

them as soon as possible. This tactic may also be seen as going against the Code of Thundera by the ThunderCats if they later learn of it. Self-Destruct. The ship’s self-destruct can be activated in this chamber as a Utilize action while standing within 5 feet of the central cylinder. Currently, only the mu’tants know how to do this, but a character can figure it out by taking the Study action and succeeding on a DC 20 Intelligence (Investigation) check. If the check fails by 11 or more, however, the character accidentally triggers the self-destruct. Once activated, the self-destruct engages after 1 minute, destroying everything in the facility. If the self-destruct is triggered, the mu’tants call for Mumm-Ra to teleport them out of the facility, which he does. CHOOSING VIOLENCE If the characters decide to engage the mu’tants in combat despite Slithe’s warnings, their response will depend on the time remaining in the adventure. If an hour or more has passed, the mu’tants will immediately trigger the facility’s self-destruct sequence and teleport away. Otherwise, they will fight defensively, only initiating the self-destruct if the adventure’s time is running short or it becomes clear they are losing the battle.

#### 6A: NORTH CONTROL CORRIDOR

A 10-foot wide and 30-foot-long corridor resembling area 2, including the service ducts, but without the opening, corpse, or ruined tube (see area 2). There are two doors facing north and south respectively. Doors. The north-side door leads to area 7. The south-side door has a violet trim, differentiating it from the others, designating that it is locked and requires a violet key card to open it. This lock can’t be bypassed with Thieves’ Tools, though a Knock spell or similar magic opens it as normal. To unlock the door, a violet key card must be inserted into a slot on the panel next to the door. The door then unlocks, and the panel ejects the card. Beyond this door lies area 6. Service Ducts. The service ducts extend 30 feet west-side to area 4b, and 30 feet east-side to area 5b.

#### 7: OPERATIONS

This 30-by-30-foot chamber once served as the facility’s main operations room. The space is divided into two levels. The upper level features high-backed chairs molded from a single unknown material with the port-side wall consisting of shattered screens and broken glass. There are three doors facing west, south, and east respectively. Multiple stairs lead down to a lower level, visible from above. A large door with violet, green, and red trim stands on the west-side wall of the lower floor. 257

Doors. The west-side door leads to area 4b, while the south-side door leads to area 6a, and the east-side door leads to area 5b. The large door with violet, green, and red trim requires a violet key card, green key card, and red key card to be inserted in order to be unlocked. This door’s lock can’t be bypassed with Thieves’ Tools or a Knock spell. To unlock the door, all three key cards must be inserted into their respective colored slots on the panel next to the door. The door then unlocks, and the panels eject the cards. Beyond this door lies area 8.

#### 8: NAVIGATION CHAMBER

This 15-foot diameter circular room is adorned with shimmering metal panels. At its center, a massive, advanced astrolabe-like array of rotating rings dominates the space. Surrounding it are control panels with buttons and screens. In the center of the array, a hologram of First Earth marked with several glowing points is projected by a ball bearing-like device. Thundrillium Map. The ball bearing-like device is the Thundrillium Map.

#### ESCAPE!

Once the characters obtain the Thundrillium Map, their mission isn’t over yet. They must still escape the facility with their prize intact, dealing with both the mu’tants and the haywire security robot. 258 For an added challenge or more climactic finale, you can opt to have Mumm-Ra the Ever-Living (see Chapter 4) himself arrive in one final desperate effort to prevent the map from getting to the ThunderCats. If you do introduce Mumm-Ra and he is proving to be more than a match for the characters, they have two options. They can either flee to the Feliner and fly away from Mumm-Ra or stay and fight. If they choose to stay and things are looking bleak, consider having the ThunderCats arrive in a timely rescue, turning the tide at the last possible moment. However, do not allow the ThunderCats to defeat Mumm-Ra outright. This is the characters’ story, so ensure they deliver the final blow.

### Conclusion

Once the characters reach the Feliner, they have succeeded! Upon returning to Cats’ Lair, they are welcomed as heroes and honorary ThunderCats. If you wish to extend the adventure into a full campaign, their next mission might be to investigate the thundrillium deposits revealed by the map into the vast unknown dangers of Third Earth. If the mu’tants seized the map first, or if the facility self-destructed before the characters could recover it, the mission failed. In that case, the party must return to the ThunderCats empty-handed… if they make it back at all.

### ThunderCats, Ho!

The battle cry of Lion-O echoes through stormcharged skies, his Sword of Omens blazing with ancient power. It’s crimson beam cuts across the wilderness of Third Earth, a world where forgotten science and wild sorcery intertwine. The vile mu’tants plot from the shadows, while the deathless Mumm-Ra schemes in his decaying tomb, ever hungry for the Eye of Thundera’s light. Now you must answer its call. This is your chance to live your own ThunderCats adventures using the rules of the “World’s Greatest Roleplaying Game”. Harness the power of thundrillium to sustain your technology, pilot starships through cosmic storms, or wield enchanted armaments and ancient spells. On Third Earth, laser rifles clash with mystic blades, wizards duel robots, and courage is as vital a resource as energy cells. Inspired by the timeless spirit of 1980s adventure, where heroism meets heart and every battle carries a lesson, this book brings the action, spectacle, and excitement of ThunderCats to your tabletop. This book is your gateway to bringing the world of ThunderCats to life in your Fifth Edition games. It guides both players and Game Masters through creating characters, adventures, and stories set in this iconic universe. You’ll need the Fifth Edition core rules to use this book effectively. www.lynnvander.rocks
